### Lesson-by-lesson filing (printed pages)

- **L1 Numbers, pp. 1–6.** Numbers to 100,000: 10,000 ten times is 100,000 (ten ten-thousands = one hundred-thousand); 100,000 is read "one saen" (មួយសែន) or "one hundred thousand" (មួយរយពាន់) (p. 1). Reading 5-digit numbers in words (78 560, 80 564, 92 150, 95 246) and writing numbers from words (e.g. ប្រាំមួយម៉ឺនពីរពាន់ហាសិបប្រាំ = 62,055; ពីរម៉ឺនប្រាំបួនពាន់ប្រាំបីរយហិតសិបប្រាំបួន = 29,859; ប្រាំមួយម៉ឺនប្រាំពាន់ប្រាំបួនរយហិតសិបប្រាំពីរ = 65,957; ប្រាំមួយម៉ឺនប្រាំពាន់ប្រាំពីរ = 65,007) (p. 1). Place value to 100,000 (p. 2); comparing/ordering (p. 3); number line (p. 4); place-value exercises (p. 5); completing sequences (p. 1: 10 000, 20 000 … 50 000 … 70 000 …; 20 000, 40 000 … 80 000 …; 10 000, 30 000 … 90 000 …; 10 001, 10 003 … 10 009 … 100 015 …). Exercises (pp. 2–6).
- **L2 Fractions, pp. 7–12.** Comparing fractions with the same denominator (p. 7): two 8-part bars, 5/8 shaded vs 7/8 shaded — 5 < 7, so 5/8 < 7/8 and 7/8 > 5/8; rule box: to compare fractions with the same denominator, compare the numerators. Exercises (p. 7): compare 4/7 □ 5/7, 4/6 □ 5/6, 4/9 □ 4/9, 2/3 □ 3/3, 5/6 □ 4/6, 7/7 □ 6/7, 9/12 □ 12/12, 8/10 □ 4/10, 6/8 □ 7/8, 15/16 □ 13/16 (all blank). Adding fractions with the same denominator (p. 10): 1/4 + 1/4 = 2/4 and 2/8 + 3/8 = 5/8, with bars; rule box: add the numerators, keep the denominator (2/4 is NOT simplified). Subtracting fractions with the same denominator (p. 11); exercises (pp. 8, 9, 11, 12).
- **L3 Addition, pp. 13–22.** 5-digit + 4-digit with carrying (p. 13): village story 56 590 + 4 845 = 61 435 (0+5=5, 9+4=13 write 3 carry 1, 5+8+1=14 write 4 carry 1, 6+4+1=11 write 1 carry 1, 5+1=6 — correct). 5-digit + 3-digit (p. 15); 5-digit + 5-digit (p. 17); 5-digit + 2-digit (p. 19); mixed addition (pp. 20–21); exercises (pp. 14, 16, 18, 20, 22).
- **L4 Geometry, pp. 23–28.** The square (ការ៉េ) (p. 23): all sides equal (ជ្រុង កខ = ខឃ = ឃគ = គក), all four angles are right angles; rule box: a square is a plane shape with 4 equal sides and 4 right angles. Exercises (p. 23): pick the squares from shapes (rectangle, oval, pentagon, diamond, square, triangle); a square with side 5 cm — the other sides are 5 cm; "what is a square?". Rectangle and triangles (pp. 24–25); points, lines and segments (p. 26); drawing shapes (p. 27); exercises (p. 28).
- **L5 Weight, pp. 29–34.** Weighing in kg, g and kham (ខាំ) (p. 29): tomatoes 300 g or 3 kham; fish 1 kg or 10 kham (consistent: 1 kham = 100 g); pineapple 2 kg 400 g or 2 គក 4 ខាំ; rule box: kg and g are units of weight; kg written "គក" or "kg", g written "ក" or "g". Comparing weights (p. 30); adding weights (p. 31); subtracting weights (p. 32); word problems (p. 33); exercises (p. 34).
- **L6 Subtraction, pp. 35–44.** 5-digit − 4-digit with borrowing (p. 35): 24 860 − 8 575 = 16 285 (0−5 borrow → 10−5=5; 5−7 borrow → 15−7=8; 7−5=2; 4−8 borrow → 14−8=6; 1−0=1 — correct, borrows shown in the column work). 5-digit − 3-digit (p. 37); 5-digit − 5-digit (p. 39); 5-digit − 2-digit (p. 41); mixed addition and subtraction (p. 43, e.g. 638+705−174+95); exercises (pp. 36, 38, 40, 42, 44).
- **L7 Money, pp. 45–50.** Types of banknotes (p. 45): 10,000, 20,000, 50,000, 100,000 riel. Naming notes (exercises p. 45: 5000, 20,000, 5000, 50,000, 100,000, 10,000). Counting money (p. 46); exchanging notes (p. 47); adding/subtracting money (p. 48); money word problems (p. 49); problems (p. 50 — 7 problems: buying with change (2000+1500, pay 5000), 2 rulers at 1500 each + 5 notebooks at 1200 each (pay with a 20,000 note), selling for a profit (56,000 → 88,000), selling price = cost 75,000 + profit 25,000, 78 books × 800 riel, 24,000 ÷ 1500, 930 l × 800 riel sold for 837,000 less 25,000 transport).
- **L8 Multiplication, pp. 51–60.** 4-digit × 1-digit with carrying (p. 51): school story 2 375 × 4 = 9 500 (4×5=20 write 0 carry 2; 4×7=28+2=30 write 0 carry 3; 4×3=12+3=15 write 5 carry 1; 4×2=8+1=9 — correct); creating a word problem (p. 51: 1500 × 5). 3-digit × 2-digit (p. 53): 235 × 25 = 5 875 (235×5=1175; 235×20=4700; 1175+4700=5875 — correct); creating a word problem (p. 53: 850 × 15). Multiplying by round numbers (p. 55): 500 × 30 = 15,000 (3×5=15, add the three zeros); rule box: to multiply numbers ending in zeros, multiply the non-zero digits then add the zeros. 4-digit × 1-digit with carrying (p. 56); 4-digit × 2-digit with carrying (p. 57); word problems (pp. 58–59); exercises (pp. 52, 54, 56, 58, 60).
- **L9 Division, pp. 61–70.** 4-digit ÷ 1-digit, no remainder (p. 61): 7 165 ÷ 5 = 1 433, long division shown (7÷5=1 r2, 21÷5=4 r1, 16÷5=3 r1, 15÷5=3 r0 — correct). 4-digit ÷ 1-digit with remainder (p. 63); 3-digit ÷ 2-digit (p. 65: e.g. 528÷4, 1,344÷2); 4-digit ÷ 2-digit (p. 67: e.g. 1,254÷12); word problems (pp. 68–69: e.g. 5,880 ÷ 21); exercises (pp. 62, 64, 66, 68, 70).
- **L10 Time, pp. 71–78.** Reading and writing time (p. 71): a girl wakes at 6:10 in the morning (6:10 ព្រឹក; analogue clock shows hour near 6, minute at 2 — correct); pupils leave school at 11:40 (11:40 ព្រឹក; clock shows hour near 12, minute at 8 — correct). Time duration (p. 73); calendar (p. 75, per the text-layer header — not read in detail); exercises (pp. 72, 74, 76, 78).
- **L11 Length, pp. 79–82.** Measuring in cm and mm (p. 79): a caterpillar is 10 mm long; a leaf is 45 mm long (ruler: leaf spans ~0–4.5 cm — correct); rule box: mm and cm are units of length; cm written "សង់" or "cm", mm written "មម" or "mm"; **1 cm = 10 mm**. Exercises (p. 79): measure objects in mm (brush, pencil, pliers, wrench) and in cm+mm (comb, scissors). km and m (p. 81: 1,000 m = 1 km, per the text-layer header — not read in detail); exercises (pp. 80, 82).
- **L12 Decimal numbers, pp. 83–90.** Tenths (p. 83): a rectangle divided into 10 parts, 7 shaded → 7/10 = 0.7; "0.7 is a decimal number with tenths, read 'zero point seven' (សូន្យចុចប្រាំពីរ)". Exercises (p. 83): write fraction and decimal for shaded tenths-bars; shade the decimal (0.6, 0.8, 0.5, 0.9); write fractions as decimals (2/10, 6/10, 1/10 — blank). Comparing decimals (p. 85); ordering decimals (p. 87); decimals on a number line (p. 88); exercises (pp. 84, 86, 88, 90). Tenths only — no hundredths.
- **L13 Adding decimals, pp. 91–100.** Adding decimals (tenths) without carrying (p. 91): 2.5 kg + 2.3 kg = 4.8 kg, with tenths-bars (2 whole + 5 tenths, 2 whole + 3 tenths → 4 whole + 8 tenths) and column method (5+3=8 tenths, 2+2=4 ones — correct). Adding decimals with carrying (p. 93); word problems (p. 95); mixed decimal addition (pp. 97–99); exercises (pp. 92, 94, 96, 98, 100).
- **L14 Subtracting decimals, pp. 101–110.** Subtracting decimals (tenths) without borrowing (p. 101): 2.7 m − 1.4 m = 1.3 m, with a bar model and column method (7−4=3 tenths, 2−1=1 one — correct). Subtracting decimals with borrowing (p. 103); word problems (p. 105: e.g. 4.5 l − 1.8 l); mixed decimal addition and subtraction (p. 107); exercises (pp. 102, 104, 106, 108, 110).
- **L15 Angles, pp. 111–114.** What an angle is (p. 111): two rays កខ and កគ with common endpoint ក form angle ខកគ (vertex ក); the parts of an angle are its vertex and its sides; an angle is formed by two rays with a common vertex. Exercises (p. 111): name the angles; name the vertex and sides and read the angle (6 angles shown with arcs — acute, obtuse and right). Measuring angles with a protractor (p. 113); exercises (pp. 112, 114).
- **L16 Perpendicular and parallel lines, pp. 115–118.** Perpendicular lines (p. 115): two lines meeting and forming a right angle are called perpendicular lines; rule box: lines that meet forming a right angle are perpendicular. Exercises (p. 115): which pairs are perpendicular? which lines are perpendicular to line កខ? which everyday objects show perpendicular lines? Parallel lines (p. 117); exercises (pp. 116, 118).
- **L17 Statistics, pp. 119–124.** How to collect data (p. 119): a picture of mixed objects (TV, elephant, fish, tiger, horse, man, crocodile, shoes, chicken, snake, dog, tree, giraffe, lamp, ball, goose, chair, cow, violin); sorting data into tables (p. 119): living things (រូបមានជីវិត) vs non-living things (រូបគ្មានជីវិត). Data tables and bar charts (pp. 120–123, per the text-layer headers — not read in detail). Dice tally exercise (p. 124, last page): 40 numbered cards (1–6) in a 5×8 grid; roll the dice, tally the results in a table (number / tally / count) and total.

### Confirmed book errors

**None found.** Every worked example checked visually is correct (listed in "Checked and NOT errors" below).

### Checked and NOT errors (do not "correct" these)

- **p. 19 (L3 interior):** the scan suggested "4+5=9" — this is a correct column step in a 5-digit addition, not an error.
- **p. 53 (L8):** 235 × 25 = 5 875 — correct (235×5=1175, 235×20=4700, sum 5875). The scan flags on pp. 53, 55–57 were the column-work digits glued into fake equations.
- **p. 55 (L8):** 500 × 30 = 15,000 — correct (3×5=15, add three zeros).
- **p. 10 (L2):** 1/4 + 1/4 = 2/4 — correct as the book teaches it (fractions are NOT simplified in this book; 2/4 stays 2/4).

### Honest negatives (Grade 4)

- No decimals beyond tenths (no hundredths/thousandths); no decimal multiplication or division; no fraction multiplication/division; no equivalent fractions as such (fractions are compared/added only with the same denominator; 2/4 is never simplified to 1/2).
- No multiplication of 2-digit × 2-digit; no division by 2-digit numbers beyond the 3-digit ÷ 2-digit and 4-digit ÷ 2-digit cases in L9 (1,254÷12, 5,880÷21 are the largest).
- No area/perimeter (the square's sides are named and measured, but no area or perimeter is computed); no volume of solids (despite L7 in Grade 3 naming មាឌ/volume, no volume is taught); no symmetry; no coordinates; no 3D solids.
- Measurement: length is cm/mm/km/m only; weight is kg/g/kham only (no tonnes); capacity is litres only (no ml); time is hours/minutes and the calendar (no seconds, no elapsed-time computation as a taught method, no 24-hour clock).
- Angles are named and measured with a protractor, but no angle is measured in degrees on the pages read in detail (L15 p. 111 names angles by vertex/sides; degree measurement is on p. 113, not read in detail).
- Statistics is collecting, sorting into tables and tallying (L17); bar charts are named in the lesson headers (pp. 120–123) but not read in detail.
- No percentages; no negative numbers; no Roman numerals; no algebra; no probability beyond the dice tally in L17.

### Time-sensitive content (do not teach as current fact without checking the date)

- **p. 45:** 10,000 / 20,000 / 50,000 / 100,000 riel banknote designs (with portraits).
- **p. 75:** calendar (per the text-layer header; year not verified in detail).
- **Imprint:** 12th printing 2020; approval Prakas 1984 of 2 June 2010; the book follows the **2006 curriculum** (foreword, PDF p. 4).


# BAND B — Grades 5–9 + Grade 8 teacher guide (6 files, 1,162 PDF pages)

Five MoEYS student books (Grades 5–9, all 2020 printings) plus the Grade 8 teacher guide. Grades 5–7 read visually from renders; Grade 8 from a mix of text layer + visual renders; Grade 9 visually with selected interiors. Citation check: **112 anchors (107 passed, 4 corrected, 1 not found)** — check-band-b.md. The Grade 8 guide is a **PARTIAL TRUE COMPANION** (7 of the student book's 18 lessons). The Grade 9 check verified five further book errors found by a second independent read and struck one candidate (#7, p. 159 AD-diameter — not an error).


## 1. `g05.pdf` — Grade 5 student, Mathematics (MoEYS)

- **File:** `g05.pdf` — 129 PDF pages, 20,064,064 bytes, sha256 `48c56869c061…` (full hash in `grab-results.json`)
- **Country / subject:** Cambodia, Mathematics, Grade 5
- **Method:** Visual read of all 129 pages from 110 DPI renders, plus Khmer OCR text (tesseract `khm`, 250 DPI) used as a reading aid for structure and prose. Every number cited below was checked against the render, not the OCR (OCR misreads digits). Three items were re-checked at 300 DPI: L9 p. 49 exercise, L12 title, L16 p. 103 clocks. No word-for-word transcription; all summaries below are my own.
- **Second independent read (2026-11-05):** a second agent read the whole book independently. It corroborated the p. 49 error below and the identity/offset, and added three confirmed errors (pp. 66, 95, 103) now carried in the Errors section. Its full supplement is preserved at `/tmp/cam-b/g05-supplement.md`.

### Identity

- **Title as printed:** គណិតវិទ្យា — *Kanitvityea* (Mathematics)
- **Grade:** ថ្នាក់ទី ៥ (Grade 5)
- **Publisher:** គ្រឹះស្ថានបោះពុម្ពនិងចែកផ្សាយ (Publishing and Distribution House), Building 148, Preah Norodom Blvd, Phnom Penh
- **Ministry:** ក្រសួងអប់រំ យុវជន និងកីឡា (Ministry of Education, Youth and Sport, MoEYS)
- **Approval:** MoEYS Prakas No. 214 (ប្រកាសលេខ ២១៤ អយក.ប្រក.), dated 23 February 2011
- **Edition as printed:** 11th printing, 2020, 140,000 copies (បោះពុម្ពលើកទី១១ ឆ្នាំ២០២០ ចំនួន១៤០ ០០០ច្បាប់)
- **ISBN as printed:** `ISBN 9-789-995-001-308` (digits 9789995001308; grouping as printed)
- **Preface (PDF p. 4, អារម្ភកថា):** prepared according to the 2006 curriculum; 18 lessons (18 មេរៀន); each lesson in three parts — worked example/model, guided practice, exercises. Cover art: bar chart, 1 + 3/5 = 1 3/5, 35/100 = 0.35 = 35%, 2,750 ÷ 25, ratio 3:4.

### Printed-page offset

**Printed = PDF − 5**, verified at both ends: PDF p. 6 = printed p. 1; PDF p. 129 = printed p. 124. Front matter (cover, title page, imprint, preface, contents) is PDF pp. 1–5, unnumbered. Printed pages run 1–124. No offset break inside the book.

### Unit-by-unit filing

#### Lesson 1 — Numbers (ចំនួន), pp. 1–4
Reading and writing numbers to 1,000,000 and beyond. Two ways to read a number: "by place" (អានតាមខ្ទង់) and "by class" (អានតាមថ្នាក់). Place names: លាន million, សែន hundred-thousand, ម៉ឺន ten-thousand, ពាន់ thousand, រយ hundred, ដប់ ten, រាយ one. Opening data: Cambodia's population per the 2008 census, 13,395,682. Abacus (p. 3): 3,507,624 expanded as 3,000,000 + 500,000 + 0 + 7,000 + 600 + 20 + 4. Exercises: read/write numbers such as 3,256,875; 4,813,725; 5,712,409; 5,920,124; 8,724,504; 9,807,005; 12,324,412; 24,758,600; form the largest and smallest numbers from digit cards 5, 3, 7, 2, 0, 8, 1.

#### Lesson 2 — Multiplication (វិធីគុណ), pp. 5–12
4-digit × 2-digit column multiplication, e.g. 1,530 × 25 = 38,250. Estimation (ការប៉ាន់ស្មាន) before computing. Calculator use (p. 9): 3,583 × 465 = 1,666,095 (calculator display "1666095"). Exercises up to 4-digit × 4-digit (e.g. 7,005 × 8,045; 9,431 × 5,024).

#### Lesson 3 — Division (វិធីចែក), pp. 13–20
4-digit ÷ 3-digit exact long division, e.g. 9,030 ÷ 215 = 42. Estimation, e.g. 2,750 ÷ 25. Decimal quotient (p. 17): 3,538 ÷ 232 = 15.25, done by writing 3,538 = 3,538.00 (232 × 15.25 = 3,538, correct). Challenge: 3,152 ÷ 25.

#### Lesson 4 — Order of operations with brackets (ប្រមាណវិធីមានរង្វង់ក្រចក), pp. 21–24
150,000 − (4,500 × 15) = 82,500, worked two ways (stepwise, and brackets-first). Rule box: do the bracketed operation first. Exercises include (8,625 ÷ 25) × 356 and 25 × (6,000 ÷ 300).

#### Lesson 5 — Ratio and percent (ផលធៀប និងភាគរយ), pp. 25–30
Ratio (ផលធៀប) written as a fraction and as a:b: cats:mice 3:4, mangoes:bananas 5:7, girls:boys 14:17 (counts verified correct). Equal ratios (p. 28): fill in equivalent fractions (3/5 = □/15, 5/6 = □/42, 8/12 = □/36, 35/85, 78/100, 11/33, 128/168, 435/280, 333/999); write ratios in simplest form (5:15 = 1:3, 6:4 = 3:2, 12:15 = 4:5, 45:55 = 9:11, 64:24 = 8:3, 20:16 = 5:4); check whether pairs are equivalent (1/2 vs 3/6 yes; 3/5 vs 15/20 no; 2:4 vs 8:16 yes). Percent (p. 29): 35/100 = 0.35 = 35%, with a 100-square grid, 35 shaded, and the three writings (fraction / decimal / percent). Percent of a number (p. 30): a 250-page book, 70% read → 70/100 × 250 = 175 pages.

#### Lesson 6 — Statistics (ស្ថិតិ), pp. 31–40
Tally and frequency table (favourite fruit; counts 6, 8, 11, 6, 9 — tallies verified correct). Line graph (ក្រាបខ្សែ, p. 33): plant height over weeks. Bar chart (ក្រាបសសរ): reading one (p. 34, trees planted by class) and constructing one (p. 35, pupils per grade 2010–2011: G1 42, G2 55, G3 60, G4 52, G5 38, G6 35; bar heights match the values). Constructing a line graph (p. 37): a boy's weight at ages 7–11: 22, 24.5, 24.5, 28, 30.5 kg (the repeated 24.5 at ages 8 and 9 is as printed). Pie chart (ក្រាបផ្អិត, pp. 39–40): a school of 120 pupils, 48 = 40%, Khmer 25%; exercises to construct pie charts (animals, sports, TV).

#### Lesson 7 — Mixed numbers (ចំនួនចម្រុះ), pp. 41–44
1 and 3/5, i.e. 1 + 3/5 = 1 3/5, with bar and circle models. A mixed number = a whole number + a fraction. Number lines 0–3 and 0–6.

#### Lesson 8 — Decimals (ចំនួនទសភាគ), pp. 45–48
Hundredths grids: 9/100 = 0.09; 175/100 = 1.75. Reading decimals in words. Number line 0.1–0.2 marked in hundredths.

#### Lesson 9 — Adding and subtracting decimals (វិធីបូក វិធីដកចំនួនទសភាគ), pp. 49–62
Adding via fractions with denominator 100: 29.29 + 35.75 = 2,929/100 + 3,575/100 = 6,504/100 = 65.04 (p. 49, correct). Rule box: add decimals by aligning decimal points. Adding by columns (p. 50): 55.84 + 88.6, aligning the decimal points. Estimating decimal sums (p. 53): 5.75 + 6.25 + 4.80 + 5.60 ≈ 6 + 6 + 5 + 6 = 23 (exact 22.40); the symbol ≈ is glossed "ប្រហែល" (approximately). Subtracting via fractions (p. 56): car fuel 50.75 l − 5.89 l. Subtracting by columns (p. 57): rice 87.8 kg − 69.57 kg. Word problems, including fish sauce 27.75 l + 30.24 l. Exercises p. 49, Ex. 1: 0.09 + 0.01; 0.91 + 0.50 (see Errors); 0.57 + 0.77; 1.04 + 5.27; 45.21 + 62.32; 78.40 + 56.85. Ex. 2 (fill in): 77.58 + 6.90 = 7,758/100 + 690/100 = 8,448/100 = 84.48; 5.82 + 14.95 = 582/100 + 1,495/100 = 2,077/100 = 20.77.

#### Lesson 10 — Multiplying decimals (វិធីគុណចំនួនទសភាគ), pp. 63–68
Decimal × whole number: 0.18 m × 12 = 2.16 m. Decimal-places rule: multiply as whole numbers, then place the point. Placing the decimal point in products (p. 65). Estimating decimal products (p. 66): 3.75 × 501 ≈ 4 × 501 = 2,004. Exercises to decimal × 3–4 digit (4.25 × 374, 26.25 × 2,011, 16.07 × 3,349). See Errors: the p. 66 estimation example 2 header and its worked solution disagree by a factor of 100 (7.45 × 43.4 asked, 745.8 × 43.4 solved).

#### Lesson 11 — Fractions (ប្រភាគ), pp. 69–84
Simplifying with bar models: 2/10 = 1/5 (÷2); 6/9 = 2/3 (÷3). Simplest fraction (irreducible fraction) defined (p. 69). Adding fractions with the same denominator (pp. 71–72): 7/15 + 7/15, 10/22 + 10/22, etc. Subtracting fractions with the same denominator (p. 73): 5/6 − 3/6 = 2/6 = 1/3; 3/4 − 1/4 = 2/4 = 1/2. Multiplying a fraction by a whole number (p. 79). Dividing a fraction by a whole number (p. 81). Word problems (pp. 83–84): a teacher shares oranges among 44 pupils, each getting half an orange.

#### Lesson 12 — Money (រូបិយវត្ថុ); content is the arithmetic mean, pp. 85–88
Title as printed is រូបិយវត្ថុ (Money / currency), confirmed at 300 DPI on both the lesson heading (PDF p. 90) and the contents page. The mathematical content is the arithmetic mean (តម្លៃមធ្យម): four shops sell the same item at 8,500; 8,600; 8,400; 8,900 riel, so the mean price = (8,500 + 8,600 + 8,400 + 8,900) ÷ 4 = 34,400 ÷ 4 = 8,600 riel (correct). Exercises: means of sets of 5–6 numbers.

#### Lesson 13 — Length measurement (រង្វាស់ប្រវែង), pp. 89–94
km ↔ m: 12 km 300 m = 12.3 km; 5 km 800 m = 5,800 m; 1 km = 1,000 m.

#### Lesson 14 — Weight measurement (រង្វាស់ទម្ងន់), pp. 95–98
kg ↔ g: 5 kg 800 g = 5,800 g; 9 kg 70 g = 9.07 kg; 1 kg = 1,000 g. See Errors: exercise item ឈ on p. 95 asks "45.8 kg = …… kg" (kg-to-kg); the intended item is 45.8 kg = 45,800 g.

#### Lesson 15 — Capacity measurement (រង្វាស់ចំណុះ), pp. 99–102
l ↔ ml: 3 l 500 ml = 3.5 l; 4 l 350 ml = 4,350 ml; 1 l = 1,000 ml.

#### Lesson 16 — Time measurement (រង្វាស់ពេល), pp. 103–106
Reading and writing time in the 24-hour format. 1 day = 24 hours, shown as two 12-hour halves on a timeline. Three clocks (p. 103, verified at 300 DPI): 00:00 night (midnight), 12:00 day (noon), 12:00 night (midnight, end of the day) — see Errors: the third clock is mislabelled and should read 24:00. 1 hour = 60 minutes = 3,600 seconds. Conversions: 1:00 day → 13:00; 1:00 night → 1:00; 8:15 day → 8:15; 8:15 night → 20:15. Reading/writing times (p. 104). Multiplying time units (p. 105): weaving takes 45 minutes.

#### Lesson 17 — Geometry (ធរណីមាត្រ), pp. 107–120
Lines (បន្ទាត់) and segments (អង្កត់): line NO and line PQ; segment PQ, PR, RQ; counting lines and segments in figures (p. 108). Types of angles (p. 109): 90° is right, less than 90° is acute; exercises naming angles and their types (45°, 120°, 90°, 85°). Constructing angles with a protractor (p. 111): draw angle BAC = 40° (draw ray AC, place the protractor centre at A with 0 on AC, mark B at 40°, draw ray AB); exercises to construct 35°, 45°, 60°, 55°, 75°, 90°, 125°, 120°, 150°. Triangles (p. 112): a triangle is a closed figure with 3 sides; types of triangles; equilateral/isosceles questions (p. 113). Construct a triangle given one side and two angles (p. 114): side 40 mm, angles 60° and 45°. Construct a triangle given two sides and one angle (p. 115).

#### Lesson 18 — Plans, maps and scale (ប្លង់ ផែនទី និងមាត្រដ្ឋាន), pp. 121–124
A plan (ប្លង់) is a top-view drawing. Map reading with a compass rose (north arrow). Scale (មាត្រដ្ឋាន): 1:400, 1:100, 2:1000, 1/500, 1/2,000,000. Real length = plan length × scale. Final exercises (p. 124): school plan at 1:400; a rectangle 8 m × 6 m at 1:100; a road 40 m at 2:1000; a field 60 m × 20 m at 1/500; a 1/500 plan rectangle 5 cm × 3 cm; a map at 1/2,000,000 with points A–B 3 cm apart.

### Confirmed book errors and slips

1. **Printed p. 49, L9 Ex. 1 (ខ): "0.91 + 050" — misprint.** Confirmed at 300 DPI: there is no decimal point before "50"; every other item in the same exercise has one (0.09 + 0.01, 0.57 + 0.77, 1.04 + 5.27, 45.21 + 62.32, 78.40 + 56.85). The intended addend is **0.50**. Correct sum: **0.91 + 0.50 = 1.41**. Teach it corrected; never as printed. Corroborated by the second independent read.
2. **Printed p. 66, L10 estimation example 2: the problem statement and the worked solution disagree by a factor of 100.** The header (verified at 200 DPI by the second read) asks **7.45 × 43.4 ≈ □**, but the worked boxes and concluding line all solve **745.8 × 43.4 ≈ 32,078** (745.8 → 746, 43.4 → 43, 746 × 43 = 32,078). Teach: 7.45 × 43.4 ≈ 301 by the book's own method (7 × 43), exact 323.33; the book's 32,078 is the estimate for 745.8 × 43.4 (exact 32,367.72). Most likely the header's decimal point is misplaced.
3. **Printed p. 103, L16 24-hour timeline: the end-of-day midnight clock is labelled "12:00" instead of "24:00".** The timeline runs 00:00 (midnight) → 12:00 (midday) → a third clock captioned night/midnight but labelled "12:00" (verified at 200 DPI by the second read). In a lesson explicitly about the 24-hour format (1 day = 24 hours) the third label should be **24:00**. Teach 24:00.
4. **Printed p. 95, L14 exercise table, item ឈ: "45.8 kg = …… kg" (kg-to-kg).** Every other item converts between kg and g (verified at 200 DPI by the second read). Teach as the intended item: **45.8 kg = 45,800 g**.
5. No other worked example or answer checked in this book was wrong. All sums, products, quotients, conversions and means spot-checked above are correct. The second read additionally verified as correct: p. 4 mean 98 ÷ 7 = 14; p. 7 1,625 × 315 = 511,875; p. 9 3,583 × 465 = 1,666,095; p. 17 3,538 ÷ 232 = 15.25; p. 30 70% of 250 = 175; p. 43 7 ÷ 3 = 2 1/3; p. 60 estimate 75.75 − 63.29 ≈ 13; p. 63 0.18 × 12 = 2.16; p. 65 0.6 × 0.8 = 0.48, 1.3 × 0.5 = 0.65, 1.15 × 0.5 = 0.575, 2.24 × 1.17 = 2.6208; p. 88 46 × 4,190 = 192,740; p. 101 4.62 l + 1,900 ml = 6.52 l, 2.7 l − 1,200 ml = 1,500 ml; p. 119 circumferences 9.43/12.57/15.71 cm are measured values, ratios ≈ 3.142 correctly support π ≈ 3.14.

### Honest negatives

Topics a Grade 5 book might be expected to teach that this one does NOT:
- No division of decimals (no decimal ÷ whole number, no decimal ÷ decimal).
- No fraction addition or subtraction with unlike denominators (L11 is same-denominator only, plus fraction ×/÷ a whole number).
- No area or perimeter formulas (L17 is lines, angles, triangles and constructions only).
- No mean beyond the arithmetic mean of small sets (L12); no median or mode.
- No negative numbers; no powers or roots; no probability.

### Held-copy defects

- None found: all 129 PDF pages present, in order, legible; no missing, duplicated or truncated pages.
- Quirk, not a defect: a diagonal watermark ("facebook.com/krou.moeys.gov.kh", "youtube.com/moeys") runs across many pages — a scan-source watermark, text remains readable.

### Notes for the lessons builder

- The book's own lesson shape: worked example → guided practice → exercises, with a rule box per topic.
- L12's title ("Money") does not name its maths content; file its content under arithmetic mean, with the shop-price context kept.
- The G5 ratio/percent groundwork (L5) is the prior learning the G8 teacher guide cites for G8 L3; G6 L17 interest is the later development.


## 2. `g06.pdf` — Grade 6 student, Mathematics (MoEYS)

- **File:** `g06.pdf` — 145 PDF pages, 21,537,677 bytes, sha256 `fbc35c152d65…` (full hash in `grab-results.json`)
- **Country / subject:** Cambodia, Mathematics, Grade 6
- **Method:** Visual read of all 20 lesson openings from 110 DPI renders, plus Khmer OCR text (tesseract `khm`, 250 DPI) for the interiors of all 20 lessons, with every number cited checked against the render, not the OCR (OCR misreads digits — e.g. it read the L20 surface-area total as 1,080 where the render shows 108). Two pages re-checked visually at 110 DPI: printed pp. 31 and 136. No word-for-word transcription; all summaries below are my own.
- **Second independent read (2026-11-05):** a second agent read the whole book independently (all 20 lesson openings at 100 DPI, key pages at 200 DPI). It corroborated the identity, offset and most findings, and its corrections are merged below: one confirmed error the first read missed (p. 16 transposed digits), the L9 and L5 title corrections, the L15 worked-example correction, the L7 circle-area derivation, the L20 cylinder/cone volumes, and the verified additions. Its full supplement is preserved at `/tmp/cam-b/g06-supplement.md`.

### Identity

- **Title as printed:** គណិតវិទ្យា — *Kanitvityea* (Mathematics)
- **Grade:** ថ្នាក់ទី ៦ (Grade 6)
- **Publisher:** គ្រឹះស្ថានបោះពុម្ពនិងចែកផ្សាយ (Publishing and Distribution House), No. 148 Preah Norodom Blvd, Phnom Penh
- **Ministry:** ក្រសួងអប់រំ យុវជន និងកីឡា (Ministry of Education, Youth and Sport, MoEYS)
- **Approval:** MoEYS Prakas No. 257, dated 16 November 2011
- **Edition as printed:** 11th printing, 2020, 110,000 copies (បោះពុម្ពលើកទី១១ ឆ្នាំ២០២០ ចំនួន១១០ ០០០ច្បាប់)
- **ISBN as printed:** `ISBN : 9-789-995-001-445` (digits 9789995001445; grouping as printed)
- **Preface (PDF p. 4):** prepared according to the 2006 curriculum; 20 lessons; content: whole numbers, decimals, fraction and mixed-number operations, GCD, LCM, ratio, proportion, percent, speed, area, volume, statistics, time. Same three-part lesson shape as Grade 5. Cover formulas: V = πr²h, V = 4πr³/3, 2/5 = 0.4 = 40/100 = 40%, a/b = c/d ⟺ ad = bc.
- **Imprint (PDF p. 3):** committee Prum Vibol, Sour Channborey, Ki Deth, Ham Seng; adviser Khem Mari; illustrator In Kimsoun; layout Khem Mari; reviewers Duong Socheang, Bu Son.

### Printed-page offset

**Printed = PDF − 5**, verified at both ends: PDF p. 6 = printed p. 1; PDF p. 145 = printed p. 140 (footer "140" read on the render). Front matter is PDF pp. 1–5, unnumbered. Printed pages run 1–140. No offset break inside the book.

### Unit-by-unit filing

#### Lesson 1 — Numbers (ចំនួន), pp. 1–4
Numbers to 10 digits, read by place (ខ្ទង់) and by class (ថ្នាក់): 870,465,250. Exercises to 2,871,135,463 and 6,053,871,189.

#### Lesson 2 — Decimals (ចំនួនទសភាគ), pp. 5–8
Decimals to thousandths, with cube models: 1,025/1,000 = 1.025; 135/1,000 = 0.135. Reading and writing decimals; fractions with denominator 1,000 ↔ decimals.

#### Lesson 3 — Adding and subtracting decimals (វិធីបូក វិធីដកចំនួនទសភាគ), pp. 9–18
Adding decimals to whole numbers: 15.545 + 14 = 29.545 (14 written as 14.000, decimal points aligned). Adding decimal to decimal (p. 10): 3.356 + 40.513 = 43.869; 163.251 + 32.8 = 196.051 (32.8 padded to 32.800). Rule: write the addends so the decimal points line up, pad with zeros, add as whole numbers. Subtracting a whole number and a decimal (p. 15): a mother buys 5 l of cooking oil and uses 2.245 l; 5 − 2.245 = 2.755 l remain. Exercises: 2 − 0.750; 8 − 5.185; 15 − 9.30.

#### Lesson 4 — Angle bisector and angle construction (កន្លះបន្ទាត់ពុះមុំ និងសំណង់មុំ), pp. 19–24
A ray CD bisects angle ACB into ACD = BCD. Definition: a bisector is a ray from the vertex splitting an angle into two equal angles. Exercises identifying bisectors in diagrams. Constructing a 90° angle with ruler and compass (p. 24): step 1 draw segment AB; step 2 draw arcs centred at A and B meeting at M and N; step 3 draw line MN cutting AB at O — angles BOM, AOM, AON and BON are all 90°. Exercises: construct 90° angles from a given ray.

#### Lesson 5 — Replacing numbers with letters (ការដំឡើងលេខដោយអក្សរ), pp. 25–28
5 + 5 + 5 + 5 = 4 × 5, so a + a + a + a = 4a. 4a means 4 times a; 4 is the coefficient (មេគុណ), a the variable/factor (កត្តា). Like terms: 2n + 4n = 6n; 5n − 3n = 2n. Solving equations (p. 27): n + 5 = 9 → n = 4; n/3 = 12 → n = 36; 2n = 10 → n = 5. Exercise set p. 27 (verified at 200 DPI by the second read): three of the sixteen items have NON-whole-number answers — 15 = 6x + 1 → x = 7/3; 4 + 2x = 9 → x = 5/2; 2(5 + a) = 15 → a = 5/2. Not errors (the equations are solvable); teach the fractional answers. All other items are whole (n: 2, 17, 8, 16; x: 4, 4; t: 3, 12, 4, 10; a: 4, 6, 18).
- **Title-word correction (second read):** the lesson header reads **ការដំឡើងលេខដោយអក្សរ** ("ដំឡើង", not "ជំនួស" as first filed). Same meaning (using letters for numbers / first algebra); the printed word differs.

#### Lesson 6 — Perimeter (បរិមាត្រ), pp. 29–32
Perimeter of a polygon: P = 2 + 3 + 3 + 3 + 4.5 = 15.5 cm. Rectangle: P = 2(a + b); square: P = 4a. Perimeter of a circle (បរិមាត្រថាស, p. 31): a circle of radius R = 4 cm fits inside a square of side 8 cm; the square's perimeter is 4 × 8 = 32 cm, so the circle's perimeter P < 32 cm. Measuring a circumference by rolling a plate once along a ruler: diameter 3 cm → 9.43 cm (9.43/3 = 3.143); diameter 4 cm → 12.57 cm (12.57/4 = 3.143); diameter 5 cm → 15.71 cm (15.71/5 = 3.143). Rule box: π = 3.14 (approximately); circumference P = Dπ = 2πR.

#### Lesson 7 — Area (ផ្ទៃក្រឡា), pp. 33–42
Area units: 1 cm², 1 m²; 1 m² = 1 ca (centiare); 100 m² = 1 a (are); 10,000 m² = 1 ha (hectare); table km² hm² dam² m² dm² cm² mm² in ×100 steps. Rectangle area: A = length × width (a rectangle counted as 120 cm² of unit squares). Square area: A = side × side. Triangle area (p. 36): a right triangle with legs 30 cm and 40 cm has half the area of the rectangle built on those legs (1/2 × 3 × 4 = 6 in the worked example, p. 36). Parallelogram area (p. 38): base 50 cm, height 30 cm → A = 30 × 50 = 1,500 cm²; in general A = base × height (a parallelogram has the area of the rectangle with the same base and height). Trapezoid area (p. 39): (2 + 5)/2 × 3 = 10.5. **Circle area (p. 41) — derived and boxed, not by counting squares:** a circle cut into 12 sectors is rearranged into a near-parallelogram of height R and length πR, giving **A = R × πR = πR²**, boxed as **A = πR²; π = 3.14**, with worked example R = 3 cm → A = 3.14 × 3 × 3 = **28.26 cm²** (verified at 200 DPI by the second read; the first filing's "counting squares" description was wrong). Exercises (p. 42): a stone fence 18 m long and 2.2 m high; bricks per m².

#### Lesson 8 — Multiplying and dividing decimals (វិធីគុណ វិធីចែកចំនួនទសភាគ), pp. 43–52
Multiplying a decimal by a whole number (p. 43): one packet weighs 1.255 kg; 15 packets weigh 1.255 × 15 = 18.825 kg. Rule: multiply as whole numbers, count the decimal places. Exercises: 18.050 × 12; 25.115 × 18; 33.183 × 27; 135.205 × 43; 102.480 × 56; 215.178 × 62; 285.312 × 65. Dividing a whole number by a decimal (p. 48): a rectangular field of area 620 m² and width 15.5 m has length 620 ÷ 15.5 = 40 m. Rule: if the divisor has 1 decimal place multiply both numbers by 10; if 2 decimal places, by 100.

#### Lesson 9 — Factors and multiples of natural numbers (កត្តានៃចំនួនធម្មជាតិ - ពហុគុណនៃចំនួនធម្មជាតិ), pp. 53–62
Factors: the divisors of 15 are 1, 3, 5, 15; of 21 are 1, 3, 7, 21. A factor is a number that divides exactly. Exercises: is 5 a factor of 35 (yes), 8 of 58 (no), 17 of 156 (no). Multiples (p. 58): multiples of 2 are 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, …; multiples of 5 are 5, 10, 15, 20, 25, 30, …. GCD by prime factorisation: 18 = 2 × 3², 30 = 2 × 3 × 5, so the GCD of 18 and 30 is 2 × 3 = 6. LCM by listing multiples (pp. 55–62): LCM(6, 8) = 24, LCM(8, 12, 10) = 120, LCM(6, 4) = 12 (verified by the second read).
- **Title correction (second read):** the lesson opening (PDF p. 58, printed p. 53) and the TOC both title this lesson **កត្តានៃចំនួនធម្មជាតិ - ពហុគុណនៃចំនួនធម្មជាតិ — "Factors and multiples of natural numbers"**. GCD and LCM are sections *inside* L9 (pp. 55–62), not the lesson title. The first filing's "GCD and LCM" title was wrong.

#### Lesson 10 — Adding and subtracting fractions (វិធីបូក វិធីដកប្រភាគ), pp. 63–78
Common denominator: for 2/3 and 1/5 the common denominator is 15; for 1/3, 2/5, 4/7 it is 105 (the product of the denominators). Adding fractions with different denominators: make the denominators the same, then add. Subtracting fractions with different denominators: the same, then subtract. Word problems (p. 73) include percent-of-a-quantity items (e.g. 5 × 4 = 20 items, 75% remaining = 25 items — exact wording garbled in OCR; figures not pinned).

#### Lesson 11 — Multiplying and dividing fractions (វិធីគុណ វិធីចែកប្រភាគ), pp. 79–86
Multiplying fraction by fraction (p. 79): area model 1/2 × 1/3 = 1/6; 2/3 × 2/5 = 4/15. Rule: multiply numerators together and denominators together. Division of a fraction by a fraction (section present, p. 80 onwards): multiply by the reciprocal. Exercises (p. 84): fill-in patterns with ratios (1:3, …) and fraction-division items.

#### Lesson 12 — Ratio (ផលធៀប), pp. 87–90
Ratio as a fraction: 1 teacher : 2 pupils = 1:2 = 1/2; 2 balls : 5 pupils = 2:5 = 2/5. Exercises: 700 pupils / 20 teachers; 24 books / 48 pupils.

#### Lesson 13 — Proportion (សមាមាត្រ), pp. 91–94
1 teacher teaches 35 pupils → ratio 1/35; 2 teachers teach 70 pupils → 2/70; 1/35 = 2/70 is a proportion. Definition: a proportion is two equal ratios. Exercises: tick which pairs are proportions; put = or ≠; fill in missing terms.

#### Lesson 14 — Speed (ល្បឿន), pp. 95–98
A ship travels 120 km in 3 h → speed = 120/3 = 40 km/h; v = d/t. Table exercises: 147 km at 70 km/h → 2.1 h; 90 km/h × 1 h 30 min → 135 km; 40 km in 3/4 h → 53.33 km/h; 100 km at 80 km/h → 1.25 h.

#### Lesson 15 — Operations on mixed numbers (ប្រមាណវិធីលើចំនួនចម្រុះ), pp. 99–110
Adding mixed numbers: 6 3/4 + 5 2/4 = 12 1/4 (the intermediate "11 + 1 1/4" step is not the answer); 7 3/4 + 5 2/3 = 13 5/12 (common denominator 12). Rule: common denominator, add fractions and whole numbers. Subtracting mixed numbers (p. 101): a shop has a length of cloth, sells part of it, how much remains. Multiplying mixed numbers (p. 104): Channa walks at **4 5/8 km/h**; distance in **2 2/3 hours** = 4 5/8 × 2 2/3 = 37/8 × 8/3 = 37/3 = **12 1/3 km** (convert mixed numbers to fractions, multiply, convert back). Dividing mixed numbers (p. 106 onwards): 6 1/2 ÷ 1 1/4 = 13/2 ÷ 5/4 = 26/5 = **5 1/5** (p. 107).
- **Worked-example correction (second read, 200 DPI):** the first filing's "4 4/5 km/h × 2 1/2 h = 12 km" was an OCR misread of 4 5/8 and 2 2/3. The correct figures are 4 5/8 km/h, 2 2/3 h, 12 1/3 km.

#### Lesson 16 — Percent (ភាគរយ), pp. 111–114
Percent as a ratio compared to 100: 5 shaded of 100 squares = 5/100 = 5%; 12/100 = 12%; 100/100 = 100%. The % sign. Exercises: 75% of 9,500 = 7,125; 1.25% of 6,400 = 80; 7% of 2,400 = 168; 120% of 675 = 810.

#### Lesson 17 — Interest (ការប្រាក់), pp. 115–118
Deposit $100 in a bank for one year; at maturity the total is $106. Principal $100, final amount $106, interest 106 − 100 = $6; rate = 6/100 = 6%, called the interest rate (អត្រានៃការប្រាក់). Formula: Interest = Principal × Rate × Time. Table exercises (all verified): 3,500,000៛ at 3% for 4 years → 420,000៛; 7,000,000៛ at 5% for 2.5 years → 875,000៛; interest 2,400,000៛ at 4% for 5 years → principal 12,000,000៛; 6,700,000៛, interest 502,500៛, 1.25 years → 6%; 1,200,000៛ at 8%, interest 336,000៛ → 3.5 years.

#### Lesson 18 — Statistics (ស្ថិតិ), pp. 119–126
Data table: a survey of 40 telephone customers (A, B, C, D). Frequency table (verified by recount): A 12, B 16, C 8, D 4 — total 40. Bar chart (ក្រាបសសរ): staff by gender and year (2008, 2009, 2010); questions comparing groups (e.g. smokers vs drinkers). Pie chart (ក្រាបផ្អិត) section present (pp. 125–126; exact figures not pinned — OCR garbled).

#### Lesson 19 — Operations on time measurement (ប្រមាណវិធីលើរង្វាស់ពេល), pp. 127–130
Adding times: 4 h 47 min 32 s + 3 h 50 min 45 s = 8 h 38 min 17 s. Subtracting times (borrowing 60 s / 60 min): 12 h 10 min 5 s − 9 h 45 min 30 s = 2 h 24 min 35 s. Rules in a box.

#### Lesson 20 — Volume and surface area of solids (មាឌ និងផ្ទៃក្រឡានៃសូលីត), pp. 131–140
Volume units: 1 cm³ is a cube with 1 cm edges. A box with edges 2 cm, 3 cm, 4 cm holds 24 unit cubes → volume 24 cm³. Formula: V = 2 × 3 × 4 = 24 cm³; cuboid V = a × b × c; cube V = a³. Volume–capacity relations (p. 134): 1 dm³ = 1 l; 1 cm³ = 1 ml; 1 m³ = 1000 dm³ = 1000 l; 1 dm³ = 1000 cm³. **Cylinder volume (p. 135): V = πr²h = 3.14 × 3 × 3 × 6 = 169.56 cm³. Cone volume (p. 135): V = 1/3 πr²h = 1/3 × 3.14 × 3 × 3 × 6 = 56.52 cm³. Hollow cylinder (cylinder minus cone, p. 135) = 169.56 − 56.52 = 113.04 cm³. Hemisphere + cylinder (p. 134): hemisphere (1/2)(4/3)π·2³ = 16.74 cm³; cylinder π·2²·5 = 62.80 cm³; total 79.54 cm³.** Surface area of a box (p. 136, verified visually): a box with edges 3 cm, 4 cm, 6 cm unfolded into a net — base area 2 × 6 × 4 = 48 cm²; side area 2 × 3 × 6 = 36 cm²; side area 2 × 3 × 4 = 24 cm²; total surface area A = 48 + 36 + 24 = 108 cm². Rule box: total surface area = base area + side areas. Cylinder total surface area (p. 138): lateral 2πrh = 2 × 3.14 × 3 × 5 = 94.2; two bases 2πr² = 56.52; total 150.72 cm². Final exercises (p. 140): a road 600 m × 6 m with 0.1 m of concrete (1 m³ of cement = 7 bags; 0.8 m³ sand and 0.5 m³ gravel per m³; 1 t = 20 bags); a room 12 m × 4.50 m with 5 m walls (one wall 16.50 m²); a pond 45.75 m × 10.25 m × 2 m deep (1 m³ = 100,000៛); a water tank of diameter 1.20 m and height 2 m (1 m³ = 1,000 l), 200 l used per day by 5 people.

### Confirmed book errors and slips

1. **Printed p. 16, L3 estimating a difference: the word problem and the worked subtraction use different numbers (digits transposed).** The problem states the first basket of fish weighs **10.752 kg**, but the worked subtraction underneath uses **10.725** (15.145 − 10.725 ≈ □). Verified by the second read at 100 DPI and in the text layer (both numbers on the same page). Teach: the intended number is unclear; either way the estimate is about 4 kg (exact: 15.145 − 10.752 = 4.393; 15.145 − 10.725 = 4.420). The rounding shown (15.145 → 15, 10.725 → 11, difference 4) is correct for the number used.
2. No other worked example or answer checked in this book was wrong. Every worked example and answer checked above is correct, including the π table (9.43/3, 12.57/4, 15.71/5 all ≈ 3.143), the GCD example (18, 30 → 6), the interest table, and the time arithmetic. Two OCR artefacts are NOT book errors: the OCR read L3 p. 10 as "43.809" where the correct sum 3.356 + 40.513 = 43.869 is intended (digit misread), and it read the L20 surface-area total as 1,080 where the render shows 108 cm². The second read adds: p. 14 "33.305" is the printed "03.305" (32.550 − 29.245 = 3.305, a text-layer misread of the leading 0); p. 99 the sum 6 3/4 + 5 2/4 is 12 1/4, not 11 1/4 (the "11 + 1 1/4" is an intermediate step).

### Honest negatives

Topics a Grade 6 book might be expected to teach that this one does NOT:
- No negative numbers (they arrive in Grade 7); no powers, roots or scientific notation.
- No equations or inequalities (Grade 7); no coordinates or graphs.
- No probability (Grade 7); no median/mode (statistics here is tables and bar/pie charts only).
- ~~No circle area formula as a formula~~ — **REMOVED (false): L7 p. 41 derives and boxes A = πR²** (see L7 above). **Circle area IS taught.**
- ~~No volume of cylinders, cones or pyramids~~ — **REMOVED (false): L20 teaches cylinder, cone, hollow-cylinder and hemisphere volumes** (see L20 above). **Cylinder and cone volumes ARE taught.** (Pyramid volume is still not taught.)

### Additions from the second independent read (verified, with pages)

- **L1 (pp. 1–4):** place value on an abacus; expanded form 3,024,103 = 3,000,000 + 0 + 20,000 + 4,000 + 100 + 0 + 3 (p. 2); comparing 3,256,401 < 3,257,250 (p. 3); rounding to the nearest million, 2,782,150 ≈ 3,000,000 (p. 4).
- **L2 (pp. 5–8):** place value 36.458 (3 tens, 6 ones, 4 tenths, 5 hundredths, 8 thousandths, p. 6); equivalents 0.2 = 0.20, 0.9 = 0.90 = 0.900, 12.000 = 12.00 = 12.0 = 12 (p. 7); comparing 36.272 vs 36.254 (p. 8).
- **L3 (pp. 9–18):** subtraction with different decimal places 32.55 − 29.245 = 3.305 kg (p. 14); complement to a whole number 5 − 2.245 = 2.755 (p. 15); estimating differences (p. 16, see Error 1).
- **L8 (pp. 43–52):** decimal × decimal 8.25 × 3.8 = 31.350 (p. 44); 10.8 × 5.25 = 56.7 (p. 45); division 55.75 ÷ 5 = 11.15 (p. 47), 620 ÷ 15.5 = 40 (p. 48), 96.6 ÷ 5.25 = 18.4 (p. 49); estimating a quotient (p. 50): a circle's circumference 43.12 m, diameter ≈ 43.12 ÷ 3.14 ≈ 43 ÷ 3 ≈ 14 m (exact 13.73 m).
- **L10 (pp. 63–78):** two fully verified word problems. P. 73: 75 motorbikes; 2/5 sold day 1, 1/3 day 2 — sold 2/5 + 1/3 = 11/15, remaining 1 − 11/15 = 4/15, 75 × 4/15 = **20**; check 30 + 25 + 20 = 75. P. 74: a class where 1/2 got one grade, 1/3 another, and 7 pupils a third — 1/6 of the class is 7 pupils, so the class is **42** pupils (21 + 14 + 7 = 42).
- **L13 (pp. 91–94):** exercise answers verified (p. 91): proportions — 2/5 = 4/10 ✓, 7/9 = 14/18 ✓, 3:9 = 9:3 ✗, 2/7 = 4/14 = 16/56 ✓, 4/8 = 8/16 = 10/18 ✗ (10/18 = 5/9), 3/8 = 12/32 = 15/40 ✓; fill-ins: 2/3 = 6/9, 14/16 = 7/8, 1:9 = 4:36, 5:10 = 15:30, 2/5 = 4/10 = 8/20, 9/15 = 3/5 = 18/30, 10:5 = 4:2 = 6:3.
- **L17 (pp. 115–118):** interest table cell "5 02 500 ៛" is **502,500** riel (200 DPI), giving rate 502,500 ÷ (6,700,000 × 1.25) = **6%** — the first filing's 6% is right.
- **L18 (pp. 119–126):** frequency table verified by recounting the 5×8 letter grid on p. 119: A 12, B 16, C 8, D 4 — total 40. Corroborates the first filing. (The second reader could not confirm the first filing's "telephone customers" wording from the Khmer header; categories are A, B, C, D.)
- **L19 (pp. 127–130):** multiplication 3h 35mn 07s × 9 = **32h 16mn 03s** (p. 129); division 15h 45mn 25s ÷ 8 = **1h 58mn 10s remainder 5 s** (p. 129).

### Held-copy defects

- None found: all 145 PDF pages present, in order, legible; no missing, duplicated or truncated pages.
- Quirk, not a defect: a diagonal watermark ("facebook.com/krou.moeys.gov.kh", "youtube.com/moeys") runs across many pages; text remains readable.

### Notes for the lessons builder

- L6's π lesson (rolling a plate, ratio ≈ 3.143, then π = 3.14) is the book's own introduction to π — keep the experiment, not just the formula.
- L17 interest is the prior learning the G8 teacher guide cites for G8 L3 (simple and compound interest); G6 L12/L13 ratio and proportion are likewise cited there.
- L9's GCD-by-prime-factorisation (18, 30 → 6) is the clean worked example to carry forward.


## 3. `g07.pdf` — Grade 7 student, Mathematics (MoEYS)

- **File:** `g07.pdf` — 224 PDF pages, 30,346,500 bytes, sha256 `8f538e7d6b7a…` (full hash in `grab-results.json`)
- **Country / subject:** Cambodia, Mathematics, Grade 7
- **Method:** Visual read of all 22 lesson openings from 110 DPI renders, plus visual read of selected interiors (L2 p. 15, L3 p. 25, L7 p. 76, L9 p. 93, L12 p. 120, L16 p. 170, L17 p. 180, L18 p. 187, L19 p. 193, L22 p. 220) and Khmer OCR text (tesseract `khm`, 250 DPI) for the L3 title check. Three items re-checked at 300 DPI: L2 p. 15 worked exercise, L3 and L19 lesson titles, L22 p. 220 pie-chart exercise. Every number cited was checked against the render, not the OCR. No word-for-word transcription; all summaries below are my own.
- **Design note:** unlike G5/G6, this is an A4 black-and-white series. Title page = PDF p. 1 (no separate colour cover). Each lesson has four parts (preface, PDF p. 3): (1) opening situation/examples, (2) worked exercises with answers, (3) practice exercises, (4) end-of-lesson exercises.
- **Second independent read (2026-11-05):** a second agent read the whole book independently (all 22 lesson openings at 100 DPI, ~25 interior pages, 200 DPI crops for disputed items). It corroborated the two errors below and most findings, and its corrections are merged below: the L6 worked-exercise wording (checker-corrected again: workers, not pupils — see L6), the L1 powers/roots/calculator section (the first filing's "No powers, roots" honest negative was WRONG), the −60°C rounded-fact note, and the verified interior additions. Its full supplement is preserved at `/tmp/cam-b/g07-supplement.md`.

### Identity

- **Title as printed:** គណិតវិទ្យា — *Kanitvityea* (Mathematics)
- **Grade:** ថ្នាក់ទី ៧ (Grade 7)
- **Publisher:** គ្រឹះស្ថានបោះពុម្ពនិងចែកផ្សាយ (Publishing and Distribution House), No. 148 Preah Norodom Blvd, Phnom Penh
- **Ministry:** ក្រសួងអប់រំ យុវជន និងកីឡា (Ministry of Education, Youth and Sport, MoEYS)
- **Approval:** MoEYS Prakas No. 2210, dated 15 July 2009
- **Edition as printed:** 14th printing, 2020, 110,000 copies (បោះពុម្ពលើកទី១៤ ឆ្នាំ២០២០ ចំនួន១១០ ០០០ច្បាប់)

**Printed = PDF − 4**, verified at both ends: PDF p. 5 = printed p. 1; PDF p. 224 = printed p. 220 (last exercises page). Front matter (title page, imprint, preface, contents) is PDF pp. 1–4, unnumbered. Printed pages run 1–220. No offset break inside the book.

Title notes: L3's title is ចំនួនគត់អវិជ្ជមាន (Negative integers) — confirmed from the contents page in regular type and from the lesson's content (7 − 8 = −1; −60°C); the display-font heading is hard to read at low resolution. ### Unit-by-unit filing

#### Lesson 1 — Integers (ចំនួនគត់), pp. 1–14
Whole numbers 0, 1, 2, …; natural numbers 1, 2, 3, …; the number line; ordering (a number further right is greater); even and odd numbers. Permutations of 3, 4, 5 (largest 543, smallest 345). Numbers between > 4 and < 11: 5–10.
- §4 addition/subtraction (p. 3): 735 + 196 = 931; 680 + 302 + 96 = 1078; 589 + 546 + 503 = 1638 (school pupils, 3 classes); 903 − 347 = 556; 8023 − 4571 = 3452. Rule box (p. 4): if a + b = c then a = c − b and b = c − a; if a − b = c then a = c + b and b = a − c. Word problem: 3250 kg − 1598 kg = 1652 kg.
- Multiplication/division inverses (p. 6): a × 15 = 450 → a = 30; a ÷ 7 = 35 → a = 245. Rule: if a × b = c then a = c ÷ b, b = c ÷ a. Exercises: 85 items/day × 14 days = 1190; 1280 pupils ÷ 32 rooms = 40 per room.
- §5 properties (p. 6): commutativity 4 + 5 = 5 + 4 = 9; associativity 2 + 3 + 5 = (2 + 3) + 5 = 2 + (3 + 5) = 10.
- §6 order of operations (p. 9): 19 − 3 + 7 = 23 (the book shows the wrong grouping 19 − (3 + 7) = 9 as a warning); 12 − 2 + 10 − 14 = 6; 6 × 12 ÷ 4 = 18; 125 ÷ 5 × 15 = 375; 52 − 4 × 12 + 18 ÷ 6 × 3 = 13. Rule: +/− left to right; ×/÷ left to right; ×/÷ before +/−.
- Rounding (p. 11): 15824 ≈ 15800 (nearest 100), ≈ 16000 (nearest 1000).
- **§8.3 powers, roots, calculator (pp. 12–13) — taught here, not in Grade 8:** powers 5 × 5 = 5² = 25; 5 × 5 × 5 = 5³ = 125 (p. 12). Square roots: √100, √625 = 25 (p. 13); √4900 + √1600 = √(70²) + √(40²) = 70 + 40 = 110 (p. 13). Cube roots: ³√27 (p. 13); ³√(8/125) = ³√((2/5)³) = 2/5 (p. 13); activity ³√(216/1000) (p. 13). Calculator use (Casio shown): 321 + 4027 − 2902 = 1446; 150 × 12 ÷ 18 = 100; 9² = 81; 11³ = 1331 (p. 13).

#### Lesson 2 — Divisors and multiples (តួចែកនិងពហុគុណ), pp. 15–24
Objectives: determine whether a number is divisible by 2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 9; divide a number with no remainder; find GCD and LCM; apply GCD/LCM to problems. §1 divisibility (p. 15): 42 ÷ 2 = 21, 56 ÷ 2 = 28, 198 ÷ 2 = 99, 210 ÷ 2 = 105; 70 ÷ 5 = 14, 95 ÷ 5 = 19, 160 ÷ 5 = 32, 525 ÷ 5 = 105. Rules: a number is divisible by 2 if its last digit is even; by 5 if its last digit is 0 or 5. Exercise (p. 15): find 9,018 + 1,457 and 1,995 + 1,329 without dividing, and say whether each sum is divisible by 2 or by 5 — see Errors (the printed sum for the first is wrong). Later sections: divisibility by 3, 4, 8, 9; prime factorisation; GCD; LCM.

#### Lesson 3 — Negative integers (ចំនួនគត់អវិជ្ជមាន), pp. 25–40
Objectives: explain problems and show the notion of negative integers; do operations on negative integers; solve problems with negative integers. §1 the notion of negative integers (p. 25): 7 − 5 = 2, 7 − 6 = 1, 7 − 7 = 0, 7 − 8 = ? — with only positive integers subtraction cannot always be done, so negative integers are created: 7 − 8 = −1, 7 − 9 = −2, 7 − 10 = −3. Example 2: the lowest temperature recorded in the USA, 1971, −60°C — 60°C below zero, written −60°C; the number −60 is a negative integer. Thermometer number line from −60 to 60. **Rounded fact (second read): the book's −60°C is rounded — the recorded figure is −80°F (−62.2°C; officially −79.8°F / −62.1°C) at Prospect Creek, Alaska, 23 January 1971. Teach the record as −62°C if it is mentioned.** Later sections: ordering, adding/subtracting/multiplying/dividing negative integers — addition with +/− counters and number lines (p. 30): 3 + 4 = 7; (−4) + (−1) = −5; 4 + (−2) = 2; multiplication sign rules (p. 34): (+) × (+) → (+); (−) × (+) → (−); (+) × (−) → (−); (−) × (−) → (+); 4 × (−3) = −12, so (−4) × (−3) = 12; any number × 0 = 0; division (p. 34): 6 ÷ 3 = 2; 6 ÷ (−3) = −2.

#### Lesson 4 — Fractions (ប្រភាគ), pp. 41–52
a/b with a the numerator, b the denominator (b ≠ 0), including negative fractions: 4/5, −2/3, 3/−5, −2/5. Objectives: equivalent fractions, comparing/ordering/simplifying, operations on fractions.

#### Lesson 5 — Decimals (ចំនួនទសភាគ), pp. 53–62
1/10 = 0.1, 1/100 = 0.01; a decimal is a fraction with denominator 10, 100, 1,000, …; examples 3.71, 0.50, 0.034, −1.25, −10.016, 246.31. Objectives: convert fraction ↔ decimal, compare/order, round, operations on decimals.

#### Lesson 6 — Percent (ភាគរយ), pp. 63–72
30 of 100 = 30% = 30/100; percent is a fraction with denominator 100, with the % sign. Worked exercise (p. 63, checker-corrected): a workshop with 100 workers, **40 women → 40% women, 60% men** — the second read's "100 pupils, 40 boys → 40% boys, 60% girls" was wrong; the book's example is about workers (កម្មករ/កម្មការិនី), not pupils, and the 40% group is the women. The G8 teacher guide's prior-learning table confirms this lesson also covers profit/loss, discount, commission and interest as percent applications. Interior (pp. 63–72, second read): 2/25 = 0.08 = 8%; 42/70 × 100% = 60%; price rise 30% on 260,000; discount 640,000 → 560,000, saving 80,000 = 12.5%; interest items (pp. 69–70); 40% of 7,200,000 = 2,880,000. (Commission not verified by the second reader; profit/loss, discount and interest are in the book.)

#### Lesson 7 — Measurement units (រង្វាស់រង្វាល់), pp. 73–80
Standard units. Length: dm = 0.1 m, cm = 0.01 m, mm = 0.001 m; dam = 10 m, hm = 100 m, km = 1,000 m; milestone example 62 km / 16 km. Traditional Khmer mass units (p. 76): 1 ហាប (hap) = 60 kg; 1 ថង់ (thang) = half a hap = 30 kg; 1 តាង់ (taong) = half a thang = 15 kg; 1 ដន្រ្តី = 16 តម្លឹង = 600 g; 1 តម្លឹង (tamleung) = 10 ជី = 37.5 g; 1 ជី (chi) = 10 ហ៊ុន = 3.75 g; 1 ហ៊ុន (hun) = 10 លី = 0.375 g. Exercise 1 (p. 76): a farmer's harvest of 200 hap + 5 thang, 8 baskets: 8 × (200 × 60 kg + 5 × 30 kg) = 97,200 kg = 97.2 t. Capacity (§1.4, p. 76): the unit of capacity is the litre (l); 1 l has a volume of 1 dm³; a container of 3 dm³ holds 1 dm³ … (example as printed — the second reader could not independently verify this sentence; it reads garbled and is worth a re-read before teaching). Time units section present (exact pages not pinned). Time conversions (p. 79): 259,200 s = 4,320 min = 72 h. Area, volume and mass units follow the length pattern (multiply down, divide up).

#### Lesson 8 — Algebraic expressions (កន្សោមពិជគណិត), pp. 81–90
y − 3x; a rectangle's perimeter P = (x + 15) × 2 = 2x + 30. Notation: 2x = 2 × x, xy = x × y. Table of terms: 7x (1 term), 3/2 a + 5b (2 terms), 4n² − 7n + 1 (3 terms), 2x³y − x² + 5z − 3 (4 terms).

#### Lesson 9 — First-degree equations in one unknown (សមីការដឺក្រេទីមួយមានមួយអញ្ញាត), pp. 91–100
5 + x = 8; x + 3 = 5, with a balance model. Definition: an equation of the form ax + b = c, a ≠ 0. §3 solving equations (p. 93): 3.1 by adding/subtracting — Ex. 1: 5 = 5, so 5 + 2 = 5 + 2 (add 2 to both sides) → 7 = 7; Ex. 2: 5 − 2 = 5 − 2 → 3 = 3. Rule box: adding or subtracting the same number from both sides keeps the equation true; if a = b then a ± c = b ± c. Ex. 3: solve x − 2 = 1 with a balance model (cylinder = x, ⊖⊖ = −2, ⊕ = +1): add 2 to both sides, x − 2 + 2 = 1 + 2, the ⊕⊖ pair cancels, x = 3.

#### Lesson 10 — Inequalities (វិសមភាព), pp. 101–106
2 > 1, −3 > −5, −2 < 1; 3/4 < 1, 5/2 > 1, −1/3 > −2/3. a > b or b < a; c ≤ d or d ≥ c. Definition: an expression with >, ≥, < or ≤. Same-direction and opposite-direction pairs. Exercise: triangle sides 3, 5, 7 with inequalities.

#### Lesson 11 — Ratio and proportion (ផលធៀបនិងសមាមាត្រ), pp. 107–116
A dog 2 cm : a cat 3 cm = 2:3 = 2/3. Ratio a:b or a/b (b ≠ 0). A class of 45 pupils with 15 girls has 30 boys; ratio boys:girls = 30:15 = 2/1. Exercises: 50 g / 200 g; 700 g / 1 kg.

#### Lesson 12 — First notions of geometry (សញ្ញាណដំបូងនៃរូបធរណីមាត្រ), pp. 117–126
Solids (cuboid, cylinder, pyramid); points (no dimension, named with a capital letter); lines, rays, segments. Rays (p. 120): a ray is named by its origin; rays Mx and My on line xy are opposite rays (កន្លះបន្ទាត់ឈមគ្នា). Exercises: name the rays on line xy through points A, D (Ax, Ay, Dx, Dy); lines AB and EF meet at O — the rays are OA, OB, OE, OF. §1.5 plane (ប្លង់, p. 120): a book page as a plane; if line d lies in plane P, every point of d is in P (M ∈ d ⇒ M ∈ P); line xy divides plane P into two half-planes (I) and (II).

#### Lesson 13 — Angles (មុំ), pp. 127–138
Two rays Ox, Oy make region (I), the angle, and region (II), the reflex angle. Angle AOB = ∠AOB or ∠BOA or ∠O; sides, vertex. Definition: a figure formed by two rays with a common origin. Objectives: four angle types, protractor use, operations on angles, bisector construction.

#### Lesson 14 — Parallel and perpendicular lines (បន្ទាត់ស្រប បន្ទាត់កែង), pp. 139–150
d ∥ d′ if they are in the same plane and have no common point; d ∩ d′ = {O} if they meet. Objectives: transversal angles, constructions, angles formed with parallel lines.

#### Lesson 15 — Two-dimensional figures (រូបធរណីមាត្រដែលមានវិមាត្រពីរ), pp. 151–166
Polygon definition; convex hexagon ABCDEF (vertices, sides AB…FA, diagonals AC, AD, AE, …, FD, angles); concave polygon example. Objectives: triangle properties, perimeter and area of triangles, rectangle and square properties, constructions.

#### Lesson 16 — Perimeter and area of polygons (បរិមាត្រនិងផ្ទៃក្រឡាពហុកោណ), pp. 167–176
Broken line: L = AB + BC + CD + DE. Polygon perimeter = sum of its sides; hexagon 12 + 7 + 5 + 10 + 14 + 23 = 71 cm. Triangle perimeter (p. 170): P_ABC = a + b + c (rule box). Exercise: isosceles triangle ABC, AB = AC = 27 cm, BC = 21 cm → P = 2 × 27 + 21 = 75 cm. Practice: triangle with sides 13 dm, 17 dm, 19 dm. §2 area of polygons (p. 170): 2.1 notion of area — a closed curve L bounds a region (S); figures (1) and (2) each have area 10 square units, so they have equal area; figure (3) has area 1 square unit (counting unit squares).

#### Lesson 17 — Circle (រង្វង់), pp. 177–184
Centre O, radius 2 cm; a circle is the set of points at a fixed distance from the centre; diameter AB, radius OC, arc BC, chord BC. Objectives: circumference, area, arc length, sector area. Circle area (p. 180): rule box — area of a circle of radius R is S = πR²; since d = 2R, R = d/2, so S = πd²/4. Ex. 1: R = 10 cm, π ≈ 3.141 → S = 3.141 × 10² = 314.1 cm². Ex. 2: d = 14 cm, π ≈ 22/7 → S = 22/7 × 14²/4 = 154 cm². Exercise 1 (p. 180): composite figure — rectangle 14 × 20 cm plus a semicircle of diameter 14 cm: p₁ = 20 + 20 + 14 = 54 cm; p₂ = ½πd = ½ × 22/7 × 14 = 22 cm; total perimeter p = 76 cm; S₁ = 20 × 14 = 280 cm²; S₂ = ½ × ¼ × 22/7 × 14² = 77 cm²; total S = 357 cm². Exercise 3: annulus, outer R = 5 cm, inner r = 3 cm, π = 3.14: S₁ = 3.14 × 25 ≈ 78.5 cm²; S₂ = 3.14 × 9 ≈ 28.26 cm²; annulus S ≈ 50.24 cm².

#### Lesson 18 — Volume and lateral surface area of solids (មាឌនិងផ្ទៃក្រឡាខាងនៃសូលីត), pp. 185–192
Cylinder, cube and cuboid parts (bases, lateral faces, height, edges). Cuboid 7 × 4 × 6: base area 28 cm², volume 168 cm³ (p. 186, as read at lesson opening). Cube volume (p. 187): rule box — volume of a cube of edge a is V = a³; the edge from the volume is a = ∛V. Volume units: 1 dm³ = 0.001 m³; 1 m³ = 1,000 dm³ = 1,000,000 cm³. Exercise: a cube of edge 2.5 dam → V = 2.5 × 2.5 × 2.5 = 15.625 dam³. §2.3 cylinder volume (p. 187): a prism with a circular base; V = Sh where S = πR²; rule box — cylinder volume V = πR²h. Exercise 1: a cylinder 8 m long with diameter 30 mm, find its volume in dm³ (π = 3.14).

#### Lesson 19 — Symmetry (ភាពឆ្លុះ), pp. 193–200
Objectives: determine symmetry and symmetric figures about a point; about a line; determine axes of symmetry of a geometric figure. §1 symmetry about a point (p. 193): 1.1 — M′ is the symmetric of M with respect to point O iff O is the midpoint of MM′ (OM′ = OM); rule box as printed. 1.2 constructing M′ from M with respect to O: with a ruler, extend ray MO and mark M′ so that OM′ = MO. Later sections: symmetry about a line, axes of symmetry.

#### Lesson 20 — Probability (ប្រូបាប), pp. 201–208
Experiments: a coin (2 outcomes), a die (6 outcomes), drawing a ball numbered 1–10, an aspirin tablet, a car travelling 30 km. Definition: an experiment is an action whose result cannot be predicted in advance. Objectives: probability of an event; probability as a decimal and as a percent.

#### Lesson 21 — Bar charts (ក្រាបសសរ), pp. 209–216
40 pupils' ages 11–14. Frequency table (checker-corrected from the book's own bar chart and text, p. 210): age 11: 9, 12: 14, 13: 11, 14: 6 — total 40. Most pupils are 12, fewest are 14. (The second read's recount "11: 8, 14: 7" was wrong.)

#### Lesson 22 — Pie charts (ក្រាបផ្អិត), pp. 217–220
Worked example: 60 + 40 + 20 = 120; 60/120 = 1/2 → 180°; 40/120 = 1/3 → 120°; 20/120 = 1/6 → 60°. Exercise 2 (p. 220): a pie chart of 450 hectares of crops with sectors 120°, 80°, 105°, 50° — see Errors (the sectors do not sum to 360°). Exercise 3 (p. 220): a pie chart with sectors A 25°, B 110°, D 75° — sector C = 360 − (25 + 110 + 75) = 150°. Exercise 6: frequencies 35 + 20 + 53 + 12 = 120.

### Confirmed book errors and slips

1. **Printed p. 15, L2 exercise and answer: "9,018 + 1,457 = 91,575" — wrong.** Confirmed at 300 DPI. Correct sum: **9,018 + 1,457 = 10,475**. The exercise asks whether the sum is divisible by 2 or by 5 without dividing: the correct sum 10,475 ends in 5, so it IS divisible by 5 — the book's conclusion (divisible by 5, because the sum's last digit is 5) still holds for the correct sum, but the printed sum 91,575 is wrong. The companion item on the same page is correct: 1,995 + 1,329 = 3,324, divisible by 2 because the sum is even. Teach the first sum corrected as 10,475.
2. **Printed p. 220, L22 Exercise 2 pie chart: the four sector angles sum to 355°, not 360°.** Confirmed at 300 DPI: sectors are 120°, 80°, 105° and 50° (120 + 80 + 105 + 50 = 355). The chart is therefore inconsistent with the stated total of 450 hectares — areas computed from these angles would total 450 × 355/360 ≈ 443.75 ha, not 450 ha. The page gives no data from which to say which sector is 5° short, so the correction is: the angles as printed do not make a whole circle; one sector should be 5° larger. Do not teach the chart as printed.
3. No other worked example or answer checked in this book was wrong.

### Honest negatives

Topics a Grade 7 book might be expected to teach that this one does NOT:
- ~~No powers, roots or scientific notation (they arrive in Grade 8)~~ — **REMOVED (false): L1 §8.3 (pp. 12–13) teaches powers, square roots and cube roots** (see L1 above). **Powers and roots ARE taught.** (Scientific notation is still not taught.)
- No rational-number arithmetic as a system (fractions and decimals are treated separately; negative fractions appear but the rational-number definition comes in Grade 8).
- No simultaneous equations; no functions or graphs of functions (coordinate systems come in Grade 8).
- No congruence, no Pythagoras, no trigonometry.
- No mean/median/mode beyond the pie/bar chart work (statistics proper is Grade 8); no combinatorics beyond simple experiments.
- No area of a trapezoid or rhombus as formulas in L16 as read (perimeter and triangle area are the formulas given; polygon area is introduced by counting unit squares).

### Additions from the second independent read (verified, with pages)

- **L2 — Divisors and multiples (pp. 15–24):** prime factorisation (p. 20): 30 = 2 × 3 × 5 by division ladder and by factor tree. Exercise: 180 = 2 × 2 × 3 × 3 × 5 = 2² × 3² × 5; 360 = 2 × 2 × 2 × 3 × 3 × 5 = 2³ × 3² × 5; x + y = 180 + 360 = 540 = 2² × 3³ × 5. GCD (p. 22): 90 = 2 × 3² × 5; 126 = 2 × 3² × 7; PGCD(90, 126) = 2 × 3² = 18. A 90 cm × 126 cm rectangle tiles with 18 cm squares, 5 × 7 = 35 squares. LCM (p. 22): PPCM(6, 8) = 24 (multiples listed; also by prime factorisation 6 = 2 × 3, 8 = 2³ → 2³ × 3 = 24). LCM(18, 24, 36) = 72 (p. 23). The book uses the French abbreviations PGCD and PPCM ("PPCM ឬ LCM").
- **L4 — Fractions (pp. 41–52):** work-rate problem (p. 50): two workers do 1/5 + 1/6 = 6/30 + 5/30 = 11/30 of a job per hour; the whole job takes 30/11 = 2 8/11 hours. End-of-lesson exercises (pp. 50–51): equivalent fractions (3/5 = 12/20; 3/4 = −6/−8 = −24/−32 = 21/28); simplifying (6/9, 18/33, −84/196, −625/−1000, 2232/4464); find x, y (x/7 = 6/21 → x = 2; −5/y = 20/28 → y = −7; x · 3/7 = 2/3 → x = 14/9; x ÷ 8/11 = 11/3 → x = 8/3); comparing fractions; sums including −(1/4)² + (2/3)³; comparing 3/2 h vs 3/4 h, 7/10 m vs 3/4 m, 7/8 kg vs 9/10 kg, 5/6 km/h vs 7/9 km/h. Telescoping series (p. 51): 1/(1×2) = 1 − 1/2; 1/(2×3) = 1/2 − 1/3; 1/(3×4) = 1/3 − 1/4; compute 1/(1×2) + 1/(2×3) + … + 1/(1995×1996). Pattern (p. 51): 1/1 = 1; 1/2 = 2/(1+3); 1/3 = 3/(1+3+5); 1/4 = 4/(1+3+5+7); write 1/5, 1/6, 1/7, 1/8 the same way. Word problems (p. 51): a tank, fractions 7/24, 1/4, 1/3 used over three days; two cars covering 3/8 and 3/5 of an 80 km route.
- **L5 — Decimals (pp. 53–62):** 5/1000 = 0.005; 2/100 = 0.02; number line 3.0–3.1; comparing 45.73 vs 36.76; 0.28 = 28/100 = 7/25; 2.56 × 8 = 20.48; 0.4 ÷ 2 = 0.2; 1.42 ÷ 5/6 = 1.42 × 6/5 = 1.704; estimating sums by rounding (29.8 → 30, 39.9 → 40).
- **L9 — First-degree equations (pp. 91–100):** 198 + 7 = 205 (p. 95); 28 × 1.4 = 39.2 (p. 96) — equations solved by the inverse-operation rules from L1.
- **L10 — Inequalities (pp. 101–106):** adding/subtracting the same number keeps the direction (p. 102): 25 > 10, add 5 or subtract 5 from both sides. Exercise: a is negative, a ≠ −1 → a + 1 < 1; a is positive → compare a² + 1 and 1.
- **L11 — Ratio and proportion (pp. 107–116):** equivalent ratios 2/5 = 4/10 = 6/15 = 8/20 (p. 108). Speed conversion 18 km/h = 18 × 1000 m / 3600 s = 5 m/s (p. 110). Exercise: a man earns $1200 and spends $450 — spending:earnings = 450:1200 = 3:8.
- **L13 — Angles (pp. 127–138):** angle measures 90°, 60°, 70°, 80°, 100°, 30°; angles summing to 180° (p. 137).
- **L14 — Parallel and perpendicular lines (pp. 139–150):** straight angles 180° (pp. 148–149); triangle angle sum 180° and quadrilateral angle sum 360° (per the lesson objectives).
- **L15 — Two-dimensional figures (pp. 151–166):** triangle angles 30°, 45°, 60°, 90°; e.g. angles 70° and 47° in one triangle; 99° and 39° (sum 138°) in another.
- **L16 — Perimeter and area of polygons (pp. 167–176):** §2.5 triangle area (p. 174): grid triangle 8 + 6 = 14 square units (12/2 = 6); triangle ABC in rectangle ABCD: S_ABCD = 4 × 7 = 28, so S_ABC = 1/2 × 4 × 7 = 14. Formula box: S = 1/2 bh. Exercise 1: S_ABC = 1/2 × 24 cm × 17 cm = 204 cm². Exercise 2: given S = 132 dm² and base a = 34 dm, height h = 2S/a = 2 × 132/34 = 7.76 dm.
- **L17 — Circle (pp. 177–184):** circumference by measurement (p. 178): diameters 4, 6, 8, 10, 12 cm → C = 12.5, 19, 25.1, 31.4, 37.8 cm; ratios ≈ 3.12–3.16, so π ≈ 3.14. C = 25 cm → d = 25/3.14 = 7.96 cm (p. 179). π ≈ 22/7; sector angles 45°/360° (p. 181). Circle areas with π = 22/7 (p. 184): r = 28 → 2464; r = 35 → 3850; r = 42 → 5544 (cm²).
- **L18 — Volume and lateral surface area (pp. 185–192):** cylinder volume exercise (p. 188): 706.5 × 8000 = 5,652,000 mm³ (= 5.652 dm³) for the 8 m cylinder of 30 mm diameter. Cuboid 4 × 4 × 18 = 288 (p. 189).
- **L19 — Symmetry (pp. 193–200):** reflection in a line d (p. 198): OR = O′R′, RE = R′E′, OF = O′F′; C′ is the midpoint of R′E′; O, C, F collinear implies O′, C′, F′ collinear — reflection preserves lengths and angles. Activity: line OO′ as the perpendicular bisector of AB; compare ∠OAB with ∠O′AB, ∠OAO′ with ∠OBO′.
- **L20 — Probability (pp. 201–208):** dice (p. 203): even numbers 2, 4, 6 → 3/6 = 1/2. 85% + 15% = 100%; 85/100 = 17/20 (p. 204). Probability scale 0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1 (p. 206).
- **L21 — Bar charts (pp. 209–216):** axis 20%–80% (p. 211); 2/8 × 100% = 25% (p. 212); values 400/350/300/250/200/150 (p. 213); percentages 57.60%, 61%, 53.70%, 52%, 48%, 46.30%, 42.40%, 39% (p. 214).
- **L22 — Pie charts (pp. 217–220):** Exercise 4 (p. 220): 30 pupils by favourite sport — table 8, 6, 12, 4 (total 30); draw the pie chart and the bar chart. Exercise 5 (p. 220): pie-chart sectors 68°, 81°, remainder 211°. Exercise 6 (p. 220): 120 pupils — 35 + 20 + 53 + 12 = 120; draw the pie chart and the bar chart; which group is largest?

### Held-copy defects

- None found: all 224 PDF pages present, in order, legible; no missing, duplicated or truncated pages.
- Quirk, not a defect: a diagonal watermark ("facebook.com/krou.moeys.gov.kh", "youtube.com/moeys") runs across many pages; text remains readable.

### Notes for the lessons builder

- L3 is the book's first treatment of negative numbers — it is motivated entirely by subtraction that "cannot be done" with positive integers only (7 − 8 = −1), plus the −60°C example. Keep that motivation.
- L7's traditional Khmer mass units (hap 60 kg, thang 30 kg, taong 15 kg, tamleung 37.5 g, chi 3.75 g, hun 0.375 g) are taught alongside the metric units — keep them; they are not in the Grade 5/6 books.
- L9 solves equations with a balance model and ⊕/⊖ zero pairs before the a ± c rule — keep the model, it is the book's own route to the rule.
- L17's circle-area lesson gives both forms (S = πR² and S = πd²/4) with π ≈ 3.141 and π ≈ 22/7 used in different examples — keep both approximations as the book uses them.
- The G8 teacher guide cites G7 L11 (ratio/proportion) and L6 (percent: profit/loss, discount, commission, interest) as prior learning for G8 L3.


## 4. `g08.pdf` — Grade 8 student, Mathematics (MoEYS)

- **File:** `g08.pdf` — 241 PDF pages, 32,871,606 bytes, SHA-256 `d84471c9dc228c9004462dd6b034adc6b56ffe8602e8d926757d1a92e897a8d0`.
- **How read:** MIXED. The text layer is a legacy-font encoding that extracts as mojibake — Khmer words unreadable, but Western digits, math symbols and page layout survive, so lesson boundaries, section numbers and page numbers were established from the text layer and all Khmer wording was read from renders. All 18 lesson-opening pages read visually at 150 DPI (PDF pp. 6, 18, 32, 48, 56, 80, 94, 108, 120, 128, 146, 152, 174, 192, 202, 210, 220, 236), plus 39 section-start pages at 150 DPI; the eight error checks below were verified from 300 DPI cropped renders (PDF pp. 3, 6, 18, 48, 83, 97, 116, 122, 161, 241). Interior drill pages are described from their text-layer headers + digit content, not read word-by-word.
- **Printed-page offset:** printed = PDF − 5, verified at all 18 lesson starts. Printed pages run 1–236. Front matter: PDF p. 1 pictorial cover, p. 2 title page, p. 3 imprint page, p. 4 foreword, p. 5 table of contents; content starts PDF p. 6 = printed p. 1. Last content PDF p. 241 = printed p. 236 (L18 exercises).
- **Held-copy defects:** NONE in content. PDF p. 3 (imprint) is an image page (0 characters in the text layer) but fully legible visually. Pages carry diagonal watermarks "facebook.com/moeys.gov.kh", "krou.moeys.gov.kh", "youtube.com/moeys" — a held-copy feature, not a defect.

### Identity

- **Title as printed:** គណិតវិទ្យា — *Kanitvityea* (Mathematics).
- **Grade:** ថ្នាក់ទី ៨ (Grade 8), in a box on the cover.
- **Publisher:** ក្រសួងអប់រំ យុវជន និងកីឡា (Ministry of Education, Youth and Sport, MoEYS); published by គ្រឹះស្ថានបោះពុម្ពនិងចែកផ្សាយ (Publishing and Distribution House), #148 Preah Norodom Blvd, Phnom Penh.
- **Imprint (PDF p. 3):** writing committee (គណៈកម្មការនិពន្ធ) named in Khmer by role — chair លោកស្រី អ៊ុកសុមនី, members លោក ប៊ូ សន, លោក ព្រំ ឌុន, លោក ឌូ វ៉េត, លោក ដុំ សត្យា, លោក អ៊ិន គីមស៊ុន, លោក ហាក់ រណ្តាថា, លោក ជា អម្ររ, លោក ឌួង សុជាង, លោក សំ សុភក្តិ (editors, illustrators, proofing). Approved for school use by MoEYS under Prakas No. 1582, dated 2 June 2010. "ហាមចតចម្លងសៀវភៅនេះ" (Do not photocopy this book). Copyright © Publishing and Distribution House. **11th printing 2020, 90,000 copies** (បោះពុម្ពលើកទី១១ ឆ្នាំ២០២០ ចំនួន ៩០ ០០០ច្បាប់) — verified at 300 DPI; an earlier 150 DPI read of this page misread it as "first printing, 50,000 copies". Footer on printed p. 51 also reads "បោះពុម្ពផ្សាយឆ្នាំ ២០២០" (published 2020).
- **ISBN as printed:** **ISBN 9-789-995-001-223** (i.e. 978-9995-001-22-3).
- **Foreword (PDF p. 4, អារម្ភកថា):** the book is for Grade 8 students; each lesson has definitions (ការផ្តល់បញ្ញត្តិ), model exercises (លំហាត់គំរូ), practice (ប្រតិបត្តិ) and exercises (លំហាត់). Signed គណៈកម្មការនិពន្ធ (writing committee). (Gist from the visual read.)
- **Structure:** 18 lessons (មេរៀនទី ១–១៨), flat list, no units. Each lesson opens with objectives (គោលបំណង), then numbered sections with boxed rules, model exercises, practice and end-of-lesson exercises. Lesson numbers are in Khmer numerals; most numbers in the text are Western digits.
