### Unit-by-unit filing

#### Chapter 1 — Sequences (printed pp 1–26)

- **Lesson 1 — Real sequences (pp 2–9).** What a sequence is, terms, monotone and bounded sequences, limits of sequences (intuitive). §3.1/§3.2 (pp 4–5) convergent and monotone sequences; §4.1–§4.3 (pp 6–8) bounded sequences and limits.
- **Lesson 2 — Arithmetic sequences (ស្វីតនព្វន្ត), pp 10–15.** §1 definition (printed p10, visually checked): a sequence 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, … with U₁ = 1, U₂ = U₁ + 1, …; common difference d = U₂ − U₁ = U₃ − U₂ = … = U_n − U_{n−1}. §2 the nth term U_n = U₁ + (n−1)d. Sums of arithmetic sequences.
- **Lesson 3 — Geometric sequences (ស្វីតធរណីមាត្រ), pp 16–26.** §2 the nth term U_n = U₁ × q^(n−1) (printed p17, visually checked): sequence 2, 6, 18, 54, 162, … with q = 3. Worked (printed p17): 6, 12, 24, 48, … → U₁₄ = 6 × 2¹³ = 6 × 8192 = 49152; 384 = 6 × 2^(n−1) → 64 = 2^(n−1) → n = 7, so 384 is the 7th term. Application: a population of 2 million in 2000 growing 4% per year — population in 2009 (geometric-sequence model).

#### Chapter 2 — Exponential and Logarithmic Functions (printed pp 27–66)

- **Lesson 1 — Exponential functions (pp 28–47).** §1.1 definition; §1.2 properties; §1.3 graphs of exponential functions; §2.1–§2.5 exponential equations and inequalities; §4.1 exponential equations; §4.2 exponential inequalities.
- **Lesson 2 — Logarithmic functions (pp 48–66).** §1.1 definition of the logarithmic function; §1.2 properties; §2.1–§2.3 graphs and properties; §3.1 logarithmic equations; §3.2 logarithmic inequalities; §4.1–§4.2 applications — compound interest (§4.1, printed p61: 2,925,000 riel at 10% compounded twice a year, reaching 3,705,000 riel in t ≈ 2.42 years, factor 1.05^{2t}) and decay/half-life problems (§4.2).

#### Chapter 3 — Trigonometric Functions (printed pp 67–112)

- **Lesson 1 — Trigonometric functions (pp 68–87).** §1.1 angle measure; §1.2 directed angles; §2.1 sin, cos, tan, cot and their signs by quadrant; §2.2 properties; §3.3 identities; §4.1–§4.4 graphs and variation of y = sin x, y = cos x, y = tan x, y = cot x (periods, even/odd functions).
- **Lesson 2 — Trigonometric formulas (pp 88–97).** §1.1 cos(α − β), cos(α + β); §1.2 sin(α + β), sin(α − β); §1.3 tan(α + β), tan(α − β); §2.1 sum-to-product formulas; §2.2 product-to-sum formulas; §3.1–§3.2 transformation formulas.
- **Lesson 3 — Trigonometric equations and inequalities (pp 98–112).** §3.1 solving cos x = a; §3.2 sin x = a; §3.3 tan x = a; §3.4 cot x = m.

#### Chapter 4 — Matrices and Determinants (printed pp 113–138)

- **Lesson 1 — Matrices (pp 114–129).** §1.1 definition; §1.2 types of matrices; §2.1 equality of matrices; §2.2 addition and subtraction; §2.3 scalar multiplication; §2.4 multiplication of matrices.
- **Lesson 2 — Determinants (pp 130–138).** §2.1 Cramer's rule for systems of 3 equations; §2.2 determinants of order 3. Worked (printed p135, visually checked): 3x+7y = −6, 4x−2y = 26 → D = −34, D_x = −170, D_y = 102 → x = 5, y = −3; 4x−5y = 13, −8x+10y = 8 → D = 0, D_x = 170 ≠ 0, D_y = 136 ≠ 0 → no solution; 3×3 system → D = 12, D_x = 4, D_y = −20, D_z = 20 → x = 1/3, y = −5/3, z = 5/3; dependent system → x = −(1+3l)/7, y = (1−4l)/7, z = l. All correct.

#### Chapter 5 — Limits and Derivatives (printed pp 139–196)

- **Lesson 1 — Limits and derivatives (pp 140–159).** §1.1 limits of functions; §1.2 operations on limits; §1.3 one-sided limits; §1.4 limits at infinity; §2.1 definition of the derivative; §2.2 derivative rules; §2.3 chain rule; §2.4 derivatives of standard functions; §2.5 second derivative.
- **Lesson 2 — Applications of derivatives (pp 160–173).** §2.2 tangent lines; §3.1 increasing/decreasing functions; §3.2 maxima and minima (turning points), speed.
- **Lesson 3 — Analysis of function graphs (pp 174–196).** §1.1 polynomial functions and their graphs; §2.1 rational (fractional) functions; §2.2 square-root functions; §3.1 asymptotes and graph sketching.

#### Chapter 6 — Probability (printed pp 197–218)

- **Single lesson (pp 198–218).** §1.1 sample space; §1.2 events; §1.3 probability of an event; §2.1 properties of probability; §2.2 applications; §3.1 conditional probability. (The Advanced Chapter 6 lesson adds deeper conditional-probability/Bayes-type material.)

#### Chapter 7 — Statistics (printed pp 219–246)

- **Lesson 1 — Grouping data into classes / measures of position (pp 220–227).** Grouping data into classes and frequency tables; mean, median, mode, quartiles (Q3 = the 12.75th term of 16), deciles and percentiles.
- **Lesson 2 — Measures of dispersion (រង្វាស់នៃគម្លាត), pp 228–237.** §1 range (printed p228, visually checked): class A marks 3, 4, 5, 6, 7 → range 7−3 = 4; class B 1, 3, 5, 7, 9 → range 9−1 = 8; data 6, 9, 11, 15, 25, 26, 27, 29, 30, 30, 30, 31, 35, 85, 100 → range 100−6 = 94. §2.1 variance and standard deviation of ungrouped data; §2.3 grouped data (√6.22 = 2.49).
- **Lesson 3 — The normal distribution (បំណែងចែកណរម៉ាល់), pp 238–246.** §1 nature of the normal distribution and its mean (printed pp 238–239): 34 students' maths marks x = 3…9 with frequencies 2, 3, 7, 10, 7, 3, 2 (data table on p238) — Σf = 34, Σfx = 204, mean X̄ = Σfx/Σf = 6 (computed on p239); bell-shaped curve, symmetric about the mean. Standard normal distribution, z-scores, probabilities from the z-table (e.g. 0.2 − 0.0987 = 0.1747 = 17.47%; 47.19%). The standard normal table Φ(z) for z = 0.0–3.0 is printed on p246.

### Confirmed book errors

**One found — compound-interest ratio, printed p61.** In the §4.1 worked example the book writes the ratio 3,705,000 / 2,925,000 as "1266.67 = 1.05^{2t}" (and "log 1266.67 = 2t log 1.05"). The ratio is **1.26667**, not 1266.67 — the decimal point is missing. The final answer t ≈ 2.42 years is correct for 1.26667. **Teach 1.26667.** Worked examples otherwise verified correct: geometric-sequence terms (printed p17: U₁₄ = 49152; 384 is the 7th term); Cramer's rule, all four systems (printed p135, with D_x = 4 for the 3×3 system); range examples (printed p228: 4, 8, 94); normal-distribution mean X̄ = 6 and the z-probabilities (printed pp 238–246); standard deviation √6.22 = 2.49. Caution for the checker: the text layer loses superscripts, so text-layer arithmetic can *look* wrong; no error has been filed on text-layer evidence alone.

### Honest negatives

- No complex numbers (Advanced Chapter 7 only), no vectors in space (Advanced Chapter 8 only), no linear transformations (Advanced Chapter 4 only).
- No sequences/series with induction (Advanced Chapter 1) — this book has sequences only (arithmetic/geometric), no Σ-notation series or induction proofs.
- No integrals, no differential equations.
- Statistics stops at the normal distribution; no hypothesis testing, no correlation/regression.
- Trigonometry stops at equations/inequalities; no inverse trig functions.
- No solid geometry (Grade 10 Part 2, Chapter 11) and no geometry in space beyond the plane.

### Held-copy defects

None. Pagination is continuous and complete: printed pp 1–252 all present, no duplicated or missing pages. Clean digital PDF with a working (legacy-font) text layer. Source-site watermark text (facebook.com/moeys.gov.kh, krou.moeys.gov.kh, youtube.com/moeys) runs diagonally across some pages in the PDF itself; it does not obscure the mathematics.

### Question 3 (track split — Advanced vs Basic vs "regular")

See the band summary (`band-c1-summary.md`). In short: there is **no standalone "regular" student textbook** — `g11-regular.pdf` is the 2020 self-study supplement packs for **this** (Basic) book. The two real Grade 11 student books are Basic (7 chapters, this book) and Advanced (8 chapters); Advanced students must learn Basic first. Basic-only: matrices & determinants (Ch4), statistics incl. normal distribution (Ch7). Advanced-only: sequences+series with induction (Ch1), linear transformations (Ch4), complex numbers (Ch7), vectors in space (Ch8). Both: exponential/logarithmic functions, trigonometry, limits/derivatives, probability.

### Unit-by-unit filing

#### Chapter 1 — Sequences and Mathematical Induction (printed pp 1–34)

- **Lesson 1 — Sums of sequences (pp 2–15).** Opener (p1) motivates infinite series with e = 1 + 1/1! + 1/2! + … and π/4 = 1 − 1/3 + 1/5 − … (the same intro text as Basic Chapter 1). §3.1 Σ notation; §3.2 properties of Σ (Σc = nc; Σca_k = cΣa_k; Σ(a_k ± b_k) = Σa_k ± Σb_k; Σ(a_k + b_k)² expansion); §4.1/§4.2 the nth term of a sequence, including finding a_n from the partial sums S_n (S_n − S_{n−1} = a_n). Worked (printed p8): Σ(4k+3) for k = 1…15 = 525; Σ(k+3)k for k = 1…20 = 3500 (both correct). Σk(k+1) for k = 1…30 is set as exercise E on the same page (its answer is 9920); it is not a worked example in the student book.
- **Lesson 2 — Relations between sequences (ទំនាក់ទំនងនៃស្វីត), pp 16–23.** §1 finding the nth term from a recurrence: arithmetic a_{n+1} = a_n + d; geometric a_{n+1} = a_n × q; finding a_n from a_n and S_n; second-order linear recurrences a_{n+2} + pa_{n+1} + qa_n = 0 with characteristic equation x² + px + q = 0 (roots α, β with α + β = −p, αβ = q). Worked example (printed pp 16–17): a_1 = 1, a_{n+1} = 1 + 4a_n, with a_2 = 5, a_3 = 21 (p16); closed form a_n = (4^n − 1)/3 derived on p17 (correct).
- **Lesson 3 — Mathematical induction (វិភាគអនុមានរួមគណិតវិទ្យា), pp 24–34.** §1 the principle of mathematical induction (visually checked, printed p24): P(1) = 1 = 1², P(2) = 1+3 = 2², P(3) = 1+3+5 = 3²; conjecture P(n) = 1+3+…+(2n−1) = n²; base case n = 1; induction step P(n) ⟹ P(n+1) = n² + (2n+1) = (n+1)². Further proofs and applications, including the harmonic series 1 + 1/2 + 1/3 + … + 1/n.

#### Chapter 2 — Exponential and Logarithmic Functions (printed pp 35–56)

- **Lesson 1 — Exponential functions (pp 36–45).** §1.1 definition; §1.2 properties; graphs of y = a^x (growth for a > 1, decay for 0 < a < 1); §2.1 exponential equations; §2.2 exponential inequalities. Worked tables (printed p37, in the student book): y = 4^x (x = −2 → 0.06, −1 → 0.25, 0 → 1, 1 → 4, 2 → 16); y = (1/4)^x — see Errors: the (1/4)^x table gives x = −1 → "−1" instead of 4. The y = (2.3)^x (x = −1 → 0.43, 0 → 1, 1 → 2.3) and y = (0.4)^x (x = −1 → 2.5, 0 → 1, 1 → 0.4) tables are teacher-guide-only, not in the student book.
- **Lesson 2 — Logarithmic functions (pp 46–56).** §4.1 logarithmic equations; §4.2 logarithmic inequalities; definition and properties of log_a x, graphs, change of base.

#### Chapter 3 — Trigonometric Equations and Inequalities (printed pp 57–72)

- **Single lesson (pp 58–72).** §1.1 equations sin x = a, cos x = a; §1.2 equations involving trigonometric functions of x; §4.1 inequalities of the form a·cos x + b·sin x > c. (Basic Chapter 3 covers trig functions, formulas and equations; this chapter is the Advanced continuation, equations and inequalities only.)

#### Chapter 4 — Linear Transformations (printed pp 73–102)

- **Single lesson (pp 74–102).** §1.1 definition of a linear transformation; §1.2 its matrix; §1.3 translation; §2.1 reflection in the x-axis (x′x); §2.2 reflection in the y-axis (y′y); §2.3 reflection in the origin O (180° rotation); §5.1 applications of reflections; §5.2 applications of homothety H(O, k); §5.3 applications to trigonometric functions.

#### Chapter 5 — Limits and Derivatives (printed pp 103–116)

- **Single lesson — "Applications of derivatives to equations and inequalities" (អនុវត្តន៍ដេរីវេលើសមីការ និងវិសមីការ), pp 104–116.** The chapter intro states the chapter covers the concepts of limit and derivative, computing limits and derivatives, then applications and graph analysis — but the single lesson is applications: §1.1 derivatives applied to first-degree equations/inequalities; §1.2 to second-degree equations/inequalities; §2.1 to first-degree inequalities; §2.2 further applications. The foundations (limits, derivative rules, graph analysis) are in Basic Chapter 5, which Advanced students are told to learn first.

#### Chapter 6 — Probability (printed pp 117–134)

- **Single lesson (pp 118–134).** §1.1 sample space and events; conditional probability P(B|A); P(A∩D) = P(A) × P(D|A) = 0.50 × 0.03 = 0.015 (printed p129, correct); Bayes-type problems; applications. (Basic Chapter 6 is a single probability lesson too; the Advanced lesson adds conditional probability and deeper applications.)

#### Chapter 7 — Complex Numbers (printed pp 135–188)

- **Lesson 1 — Algebraic form (pp 136–143).** §2.1 addition of complex numbers ((5+3i)+(−1+2i) = 4+5i; (2+5i)+(3−2i) = 5+3i); §2.2 subtraction ((9−2i)−(4−4i) = 5+2i); §2.3 multiplication (3i(5−4i) = 12+15i; (2+3i)(4+2i) = 2+16i — both correct, printed p137); §2.4 division; §2.5 properties; §3.1 geometric representation (Argand diagram); §4.1 modulus; §4.2 conjugate; §4.3 further properties.
- **Lesson 2 — Trigonometric form (pp 144–163).** Modulus and argument, multiplication and division in trigonometric form, De Moivre's formula.
- **Lesson 3 — nth powers and nth roots (pp 164–173).** De Moivre's theorem, roots of unity, nth roots of a complex number.
- **Lesson 4 — Applications in geometry (pp 174–188).** Complex numbers used for geometric problems (rotations, loci).

#### Chapter 8 — Vectors in Space (printed pp 189–234)

- **Lesson 1 — Vectors in space (pp 190–215).** §1.1 definition of a vector in space; §1.2 coordinates; §1.3 collinearity; §1.4 further properties; §2.1 addition and subtraction; §2.2 scalar multiplication; §2.3 dot product; §2.4 further operations; §3.1 coordinates of a point; §3.2–§3.4 (incl. angle between vectors); §4.1–§4.2; §5.1; §6.1–§6.2 (applications, incl. equations of lines/planes preview).
- **Lesson 2 — Equations of lines and planes in space (pp 216–234).** §1.1–§1.4 (parametric and symmetric forms of a line, direction ratios); §4.1–§4.3 (equations of planes, distances). Worked answers (teacher guide, printed p236): direction ratios a:b:c = 3:2:4; plane through (5,3,6) with normal (3,2,4): the guide gives 3x+2y+4z−42 = 0 — but its own working expands 3(x−4), i.e. point (4,3,6); for (5,3,6) the constant is −45, so teach 3x+2y+4z−45 = 0; distance d(H, M₀): the guide substitutes (−2−3)² but then writes it as 6², giving √40 = 2√10 — following its own substitution the distance is √29; plane through (1,−1,2) with normal (3,2,3): 3x+2y+3z−7 = 0 (correct).

### Confirmed book errors

**One found — (1/4)^x table, printed p37.** The y = (1/4)^x table gives x = −1 → "−1", but (1/4)^(−1) = **4** (the other rows are correct, and the adjacent graph shows y = 4 at x = −1). **Teach 4.** (Found by the C1b citation checker in the teacher guide's thumbnail of this table and verified against the student book itself; the guide filing records it too.) Worked examples otherwise verified correct: induction proof P(n) = n² (printed p24); recurrence a_n = (4^n −1)/3 (printed p17); Σ(4k+3) = 525 and Σ(k+3)k = 3500 (printed p8); conditional probability 0.015 (printed p129); complex arithmetic (2+3i)(4+2i) = 2+16i (student book, printed p137); line/plane answers (teacher guide, printed p236 — with two slips, see Chapter 8 below). Caution for the checker: the text layer loses superscripts, so text-layer arithmetic can *look* wrong; no error has been filed on text-layer evidence alone.

### Honest negatives

- No statistics (Basic Chapter 7 only), no matrices/determinants (Basic Chapter 4 only).
- No sequences as a standalone chapter — sequences appear only inside Chapter 1 with series and induction; arithmetic/geometric sequences in full are Basic Chapter 1.
- No trigonometric *functions* or graphs (Basic Chapter 3); this book has trig equations/inequalities only.
- Limits and derivatives are applications only (one lesson); the theory is Basic Chapter 5.
- No integrals, no differential equations (neither track teaches them at Grade 11 in this set).
- No probability foundations beyond conditional probability (Basic Chapter 6 covers the basics).

### Held-copy defects

None. Pagination is continuous and complete: printed pp 1–252 all present, no duplicated or missing pages. Clean digital PDF with a working (legacy-font) text layer. Source-site watermark text (facebook.com/moeys.gov.kh, krou.moeys.gov.kh, youtube.com/moeys) runs diagonally across some pages in the PDF itself; it does not obscure the mathematics.

### Question 3 (track split — Advanced vs Basic vs "regular")

See the band summary (`band-c1-summary.md`). In short: there is **no standalone "regular" student textbook** — `g11-regular.pdf` is the 2020 self-study supplement packs for the **Basic** book. The two real Grade 11 student books are Basic (7 chapters) and Advanced (8 chapters); Advanced students must learn Basic first (preface). Advanced-only: sequences+series with induction (Ch1), linear transformations (Ch4), complex numbers (Ch7), vectors in space (Ch8). Basic-only: matrices & determinants (Ch4), statistics incl. normal distribution (Ch7). Both: exponential/logarithmic functions, trigonometry, limits/derivatives, probability — Advanced at greater depth and with different emphasis (e.g. Advanced Ch3 is equations/inequalities only; Basic Ch3 is functions/formulas/equations).

### Printed-page offset

**Printed = PDF − 4.** Verified at the Chapter 1 opener (PDF p5 = printed p1), at the Chapter 7 plan (PDF p226 = printed p222) and at the last page (PDF p240 = printed p236). Front matter PDF pp 1–4 unnumbered (cover, imprint, preface, contents).

### What the guide contains

Same structure as the Advanced guide: each chapter opens with a one-page overview (student book's chapter-opener page as a thumbnail, lesson aims, time-allocation table), then each lesson gets a teaching plan (objectives; time; materials; teaching method; teaching/learning activities with lesson analysis, worked problems and **full answers to the student book's exercises**), with the student book's lesson pages reproduced as thumbnails alongside.

Time allocations (Chapter 1): Lesson 1 (real sequences) — 8 hours; Lesson 2 (arithmetic sequences) — 7 hours; Lesson 3 (geometric sequences) — 9 hours.

### Companion test against the held Basic student book

The guide's table of contents (PDF p4) lists the student book's chapters and lessons in the same order with the same titles. Start pages match the student book for Chapters 1–6 and for Chapter 7 Lesson 1 — but **Chapter 7 Lessons 2 and 3 start 4 and 6 pages earlier in the guide than in the student book**:

| Chapter | Guide TOC | Held student book |
|---|---|---|
| 1 Sequences | p1; L1 p2, L2 p10, L3 p16 | p1; L1 p2, L2 p10, L3 p16 |
| 2 Exponential and logarithmic functions | p27; L1 p28, L2 p48 | p27; L1 p28, L2 p48 |
| 3 Trigonometric functions | p67; L1 p68, L2 p88, L3 p98 | p67; L1 p68, L2 p88, L3 p98 |
| 4 Matrices and determinants | p113; L1 p114, L2 p130 | p113; L1 p114, L2 p130 |
| 5 Limits and derivatives | p139; L1 p140, L2 p160, L3 p174 | p139; L1 p140, L2 p160, L3 p174 |
| 6 Probability | p197; L1 p198 | p197; L1 p198 |
| 7 Statistics | p219; L1 p220, **L2 p224, L3 p232** | p219; L1 p220, **L2 p228, L3 p238** |

**Why the Chapter 7 drift is not a mismatch:** the numbers in the guide's TOC are the **guide's own pages**, not the student book's. The guide's Chapter 7 Lesson 1 teaching plan is 4 pages long (guide pp 220–223) while the student book's Lesson 1 is 8 pages (printed pp 220–227), so the guide's Lesson 2 plan starts at guide p224 although the student lesson it teaches starts at student p228. Likewise the guide's Lesson 2 plan is 8 pages (224–231) against the student's 10 (228–237), so its Lesson 3 plan starts at guide p232 against student p238. The **titles and content** of Lessons 2 and 3 are the same: L2 "រង្វាស់នៃគម្លាត" (Measures of dispersion) and L3 "បំណែងចែកណរម៉ាល់" (The normal distribution).

Spot checks of the plans against the student book's content:

- **Chapter 1 opener (guide printed p1):** thumbnail of the student book's Chapter 1 opener (bricklayer photo, e and π/4 series), aims for Lessons 1–3 — matches the student book's Chapter 1 (printed p1).
- **Chapter 7, Lessons 1–2 (guide printed p222):** quartiles/deciles/percentiles for grouped data — frequency table 30–40: 17, 40–50: 55, 50–60: 130, 60–70: 140, 70–80: 93, 80–90: 25 (total 460; cumulative 17, 72, 202, 342, 435, 460 — all correct); exercise answers Q1 = 53.31, D1 = 68.57; second table (8, 10, 13, 6, 3) with L50 = 51.54, Q1 = 42, Q3 = 59.23 — all matching the student book's Chapter 7 Lesson 1–2 ground (printed pp 220–237).
- **Chapter 7, Lesson 3 answers (guide printed p236, last page):** normal-distribution answers — P(X > 82) with μ = 71.98, σ = 6: z = 1.67, P(Z > a) = 0.0478, P(0 < Z < a) = 0.4522; skewness (mean − mode)/SD = (22.04 − 22.5)/9.33 = −0.05; μ = 68.11 km/h; 42.86%; μ = 39.54, σ = 5.02; quartiles Q1 = 6, Q2 = 10, Q3 = 18, D1 = 5, L35 = 7.5; σ² = 45.44, σ = 6.74; range 125 − 100 = 25; Q1 = 107.79, Q3 = 114.95, D1 = 103.81, L36 = 110.26; σ² = 28.84, σ = 5.37 — all correct, all matching the student book's Lesson 3 (printed pp 238–246) and its z-table.

The guide was printed in 2012 under Prakas 2674 (2010), while the held student copy is the 2020 12th printing (approved 2008, Prakas 2293) — but the content is identical: the student book did not change between the guide's printing and the held printing.

### Companion verdict

**TRUE COMPANION.** Same 7 chapters in the same order with the same lesson titles; the same content throughout; the plans reproduce the student book's pages and answer its exercises correctly. The Chapter 7 page-number drift (guide L2 p224 vs student p228; guide L3 p232 vs student p238) is the guide's own pagination — its Chapter 7 plans are shorter than the student lessons — **not** a content mismatch and **not** wrong citations of student pages: the guide does not claim the student lessons start on those pages. **The guide may be used as evidence for what the held Basic student book teaches** — including its answers to the student book's exercises and its lesson time allocations. (When citing a guide page, say so: guide p224 is not student p224.)

### Confirmed book errors (in the guide itself)

**None found in the pages checked.** All worked answers sampled are correct (see spot checks above). The guide contains no content the student book lacks: it is a teaching plan plus answers, not additional mathematics.

### Honest negatives

- The guide adds no new topics: no lesson the student book lacks, no extra chapter. Its value is pedagogical (objectives, method, time, answers), not mathematical.
- It does not cover the Advanced book at all — Basic only.

### Held-copy defects

- The held file is a **scan** (not a digital original): pages are slightly skewed and speckled, contrast is uneven. All content sampled is legible.
- The file ends at printed p236, mid-answers for Chapter 7 Lesson 3; there is no closing page or back matter. No pages are missing in the middle as far as the sampled checks show: chapters run 1–7 continuously and the TOC matches the student book's chapter/lesson sequence throughout.

- **Grade:** 11, Advanced track. **Publisher:** Ministry of Education, Youth and Sport (MoEYS), Publishing and Distribution House (គ្រឹះស្ថានបោះពុម្ពនិងចែកផ្សាយ), 148 Norodom Boulevard, Phnom Penh.
- **Cover:** black-and-white; MoEYS crest, "សៀវភៅគ្រូ", "គណិតវិទ្យា", "កម្រិតខ្ពស់", "ថ្នាក់ទី ១១", Publishing and Distribution House imprint.
- **Imprint (PDF p2, visually read):** editorial committee (លោក អ៊ី ស៊ាងលី, លោកស្រី ទី ប៊ូលីវ៉េត, លោក ឌូ វ៉េត, លោក ស៊ីម វិសុទ្ធ, លោកស្រី អ៊ុក សុធនី, លោក ប៊ុន ឌី, លោក ចាន់ វ៉ាដា, លោក នីក លីនដា); consultant លោកជំទាវ យ៉ាង ណារិន; illustrator លោក គង់ ធានី; typesetters លោក ឌុ សុវណ្ណដន, លោក ឡុង សុធាង; page designer លោក ហាក់ រណ្តាវ; review committee (លោក ម៉ែន រ៉ង់, លោក នាង ប៊ូ). Approved by MoEYS under **Prakas No. 2674, 9 August 2010**. "ហាមចម្លងសៀវភៅនេះ" (do not reproduce this book).
- **Edition/year as printed:** **printed 2012, 1,500 copies** ("បោះពុម្ព ឆ្នាំ២០១២ ចំនួន ១ ៥០០ក្បាល"). No ISBN printed.
- **Preface (អារម្ភកថា, PDF p3):** describes what the guide gives the teacher for each lesson — objectives, time, preparation, teaching steps, activities, answers and assessment. Signed by the editorial committee.

### Printed-page offset

**Front matter PDF pp 1–4 unnumbered** (cover, imprint, preface, contents). PDF p5 = printed 1, PDF p6 = printed 2; **from PDF p9 onward printed = PDF − 2** (verified at PDF pp 9, 20, 39, 139, 238: folios 7, 18, 37, 137, 236). The folio sequence gains two numbers between PDF pp 6 and 9 — a numbering skip in the original (cause not visible in the file). Last page: PDF p238 = printed p236.

### What the guide contains

Each chapter opens with a one-page overview: the student book's chapter-opener page reproduced as a thumbnail, the aims of each lesson, and a time-allocation table ("ចំណែកមេរៀន" — hours per lesson). Each lesson then gets a teaching plan with: 1. objectives (គោលបំណង); 2. time (រយៈពេល — typically 1 hour per session); 3. materials (សម្ភារៈ); 4. teaching method (វិធីសាស្ត្របង្រៀន); 5. teaching/learning activities (សកម្មភាពបង្រៀននិងរៀន) with lesson analysis, worked problems and **full answers to the student book's exercises**. The student book's lesson pages are reproduced as small thumbnails alongside the plan, so the teacher can see exactly which student page is being taught.

Time allocations (Chapter 1): Lesson 1 — 8 hours; Lesson 2 — 7 hours; Lesson 3 — 6 hours.

### Companion test against the held Advanced student book

The guide's table of contents (PDF p4) lists exactly the student book's chapters and lessons, in the same order, with the same titles and the same start pages:

| Chapter | Guide TOC | Held student book |
|---|---|---|
| 1 Sequences and mathematical induction | p1; L1 p2, L2 p16, L3 p24 | p1; L1 p2, L2 p16, L3 p24 |
| 2 Exponential and logarithmic functions | p35; L1 p36, L2 p46 | p35; L1 p36, L2 p46 |
| 3 Trig equations and inequalities | p57; L1 p58 | p57; L1 p58 |
| 4 Linear transformations | p73; L1 p74 | p73; L1 p74 |
| 5 Limits and derivatives | p103; L1 p104 | p103; L1 p104 |
| 6 Probability | p117; L1 p118 | p117; L1 p118 |
| 7 Complex numbers | p135; L1 p136, L2 p144, L3 p164, L4 p174 | p135; L1 p136, L2 p144, L3 p164, L4 p174 |
| 8 Vectors in space | p189; L1 p190, L2 p216 | p189; L1 p190, L2 p216 |

**All 8 chapters and all 15 lessons match, page for page.** Spot checks of the plans against the student book's content:

- **Chapter 1, Lesson 1 (guide printed pp 2–8):** sums of sequences; Σ properties (Σc = nc; Σca_k = cΣa_k; Σ(a_k ± b_k)); worked sums Σ(2k−2) = 2+4+6+8+10; 10+15+20+…+100 = 5Σk; Σk(k+1) = 9920 — all correct, all matching the student book's Lesson 1 (printed pp 2–15).
- **Chapter 1, Lesson 2 (guide printed p18):** recurrence a_{n+2} + pa_{n+1} + qa_n = 0, characteristic equation x² + px + q = 0, roots α + β = −p, αβ = q; a_n = 2(2/3)^{n−1} — matches the student book's Lesson 2 (printed pp 16–23).
- **Chapter 2, Lesson 1 (guide printed p37):** exponential-function tables y = 4^x and y = (1/4)^x, exercise answers y = (2.3)^x and y = (0.4)^x — matches the student book's Lesson 1 (printed pp 36–45).
- **Chapter 7, Lesson 1 (guide printed p137):** complex-number arithmetic — addition (5+3i)+(−1+2i) = 4+5i and (2+5i)+(3−2i) = 5+3i; subtraction (9−2i)−(4−4i) = 5+2i; multiplication 3i(5−4i) = 12+15i and (2+3i)(4+2i) = 2+16i; rule (a+bi)(c+di) = (ac−bd)+(ad+bc)i; conjugate/opposite table (4+i → 4−i / −4−i; 3−5i → 3+5i / −3+5i; −12 → −12 / 12; 3i → −3i / −3i) — all correct, all matching the student book's Lesson 1 (printed pp 136–143).
- **Chapter 8, Lesson 2 (guide printed p236, last page):** line/plane answers — direction ratios a:b:c = 3:2:4; plane 3x+2y+4z−42 = 0; distance √40 = 2√10; plane 3x+2y+3z−7 = 0 — all correct, all matching the student book's Lesson 2 (printed pp 216–234).

The guide was printed in 2012 under a different approval (Prakas 2674, 2010) from the student book's 2008 approval (Prakas 3001), and the held student copy is the 2020 12th printing — but the content and pagination are identical: the student book did not change between the guide's printing and the held printing.

### Companion verdict

**TRUE COMPANION.** Same 8 chapters in the same order with the same lesson titles; every lesson start page matches the held student book's printed pagination; the plans reproduce the student book's pages as thumbnails and answer its exercises correctly. **The guide may be used as evidence for what the held Advanced student book teaches** — including its answers to the student book's exercises and its lesson time allocations.

### Confirmed book errors (in the guide itself)

**One error, in the student book and faithfully reproduced in the guide's thumbnail (guide printed p37):** the y = (1/4)^x table gives x = −1 → "−1", but (1/4)^(−1) = 4 (the other rows are correct, and the adjacent graph shows y = 4 at x = −1). The same typo is in the student book itself (printed p37). All other worked answers sampled are correct (see spot checks above). The guide contains no content the student book lacks: it is a teaching plan plus answers, not additional mathematics.

### Honest negatives

- The guide adds no new topics: no lesson the student book lacks, no extra chapter. Its value is pedagogical (objectives, method, time, answers), not mathematical.
- It does not cover the Basic book at all — Advanced only.

### Held-copy defects

- The held file is a **scan** (not a digital original): pages are slightly skewed and speckled, contrast is uneven. All content sampled is legible.
- The folio sequence skips two numbers between PDF pp 6 and 9 (see offset note above) — a defect of the original printing, not of the scan.
- The file ends at printed p236, mid-exercise-answers for Chapter 8 Lesson 2; there is no closing page or back matter. No pages are missing in the middle as far as the sampled checks show: chapters run 1–8 continuously and the TOC page numbers match the student book throughout.


## 7. `g11-regular.pdf` — Grade 11 self-study packs (MoEYS supplement, Basic only)

**Band C1 — Grade 10–11.** File: `g11-regular.pdf` — **152 PDF pages, 14,784,331 bytes, sha256 551a087433db…** (verified against `grab-results.json`, all 36 files OK).

### How read

**Text-layer read.** The file has a clean Unicode Khmer text layer (39,170 Khmer chars), so the pack structure, titles, core-textbook page citations and exercise references were read from the text layer; covers, pack layouts and the "thanks" pages were read visually in the earlier session. No OCR.

### Identity

- **Title as printed:** ឯកសារបន្ថែមចំណេះដឹង សម្រាប់សិស្សស្វ័យសិក្សា — *Supplementary Knowledge Document for Self-Study Students*, Grade 11 Mathematics.
- **What it is:** NOT a student textbook. It is a **concatenation of 11 self-study packs** produced by MoEYS (Department of General Secondary Education, Secondary Education Improvement Project, 2020) for students studying at home. Each pack covers **one lesson of the Basic student book** and contains: a cover, a concept map, guidance, worked content, exercises, and a thanks page.
- **Publisher:** Ministry of Education, Youth and Sport (MoEYS), Department of General Secondary Education, Secondary Education Improvement Project, 2020.
- **Grade:** 11. **Language:** Khmer.
- **ISBN:** none printed (no ISBN seen anywhere in the file).

### Printed-page offset

**Each of the 11 packs restarts at printed page 1.** There is no continuous pagination across the file. Pack PDF starts: 1, 20, 34, 49, 61, 73, 90, 102, 117, 129, 142. Citations below therefore use **PDF page numbers** plus the pack number. (The pack covers also cite the **core textbook's** printed page ranges — see below — which match the held Basic student book's pagination exactly.)

### Contents — the 11 packs

Every pack cover names the chapter it covers and the core-textbook pages to read (chapter range: "សៀវភៅសិក្សាគោល ទំព័រ X ដល់ Y" — core textbook, pages X to Y); the lesson is named on the pack's concept-map page (cover+1), and the lesson's pages are the student book's pages for that lesson. All packs are for the **Basic** book (not Advanced).

| Pack | PDF pp | Basic chapter/lesson | Core textbook pages cited |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 1–19 | Ch3 Trigonometric Functions, Lesson 1 — Trigonometric functions | Ch3 pp 67–112; lesson pp 68–87 |
| 2 | 20–33 | Ch3, Lesson 3 — Trigonometric equations and inequalities | Ch3 pp 67–112; lesson pp 98–112 |
| 3 | 34–48 | Ch3, Lesson 2 — Trigonometric formulas | Ch3 pp 67–112; lesson pp 88–97 |
| 4 | 49–60 | Ch4 Matrices and Determinants, Lesson 1 — Matrices | Ch4 pp 113–138; lesson pp 114–129 |
| 5 | 61–72 | Ch4, Lesson 2 — Determinants | Ch4 pp 113–138; lesson pp 130–138 |
| 6 | 73–89 | Ch5 Limits and Derivatives, Lesson 1 — Limits and derivatives | Ch5 pp 139–197; lesson pp 140–159 |
| 7 | 90–101 | Ch5, Lesson 2 — Applications of derivatives | Ch5 pp 139–197; lesson pp 160–173 |
| 8 | 102–116 | Ch5, Lesson 3 — Behaviour and graphs of functions | Ch5 pp 139–197; lesson pp 174–196 |
| 9 | 117–128 | Ch7 Statistics, Lesson 1 — Grouping data | Ch7 pp 219–245; lesson pp 220–227 |
| 10 | 129–141 | Ch7, Lesson 2 — Measures of dispersion | Ch7 pp 219–245; lesson pp 228–237 |
| 11 | 142–152 | Ch7, Lesson 3 — Random variables (normal distribution) | Ch7 pp 219–245; lesson pp 238–245 |

Notes on the packs:

- **Pack order within Chapter 3 is Lesson 1, Lesson 3, Lesson 2** (not 1, 2, 3) — the packs are bound in that order in the file.
- **Pack 1** (Ch3 L1): angle measure, trig functions, properties, graphs of sin/cos/tan/cot.
- **Pack 2** (Ch3 L3): cos x = a, sin x = a, tan x = a, cot x = m; inequalities.
- **Pack 3** (Ch3 L2): sum/difference formulas, transformation formulas.
- **Pack 4** (Ch4 L1): definition of a matrix, operations, inverse.
- **Pack 5** (Ch4 L2): determinants, systems of 3 equations, Cramer's rule.
- **Pack 6** (Ch5 L1): limits and derivatives.
- **Pack 7** (Ch5 L2): applications of derivatives — tangent line, maxima/minima, turning points.
- **Pack 8** (Ch5 L3): behaviour and graphs — polynomial (cubic) and rational functions (concept map, PDF p103); square-root graphing is the student lesson's, not named in the pack.
- **Pack 9** (Ch7 L1): grouping data by type and into classes; measures of position; exercises including deciles (D1), a bookseller problem (30 books at $12/$9/$7/$15/$20; find Q1 and L81) and a grouped investor-money table (classes 30–40 … 70–80, 460 investors; find Q1, Q3, L45).
- **Pack 10** (Ch7 L2): measures of dispersion — range, variance, standard deviation.
- **Pack 11** (Ch7 L3): random variables and the normal distribution — grouped kg data (classes 40–45 … 60–65); machine filling bags, mean 200 g, SD 2 g.
- **No packs for Basic Chapter 1 (Sequences), Chapter 2 (Exponential and Logarithmic Functions), or Chapter 6 (Probability).** Those three chapters have no self-study pack in this file.
- Each pack's exercises direct the student to specific core-textbook pages (e.g. "write the equation (page 104)"), and each pack ends with a thanks page.

### Confirmed book errors

**None found.** The packs are summaries and exercises keyed to the Basic book; the core-textbook page citations in all 11 pack covers match the held Basic student book's lesson ranges exactly. No worked example in the packs was found to contradict the student book. (No systematic error-hunt was run over the packs' own worked content — the packs mostly restate the student book rather than adding new worked examples.)

### Honest negatives

- This is a **supplement, not a textbook**: it teaches nothing the Basic student book does not already teach, and it covers only 11 of the Basic book's 17 lessons (Chapters 3, 4, 5, 7 only — no Sequences, no Exponential/Logarithmic Functions, no Probability).
- It covers **nothing from the Advanced book** (no complex numbers, no vectors in space, no linear transformations, no sequences/series with induction).
- It has no answers section of its own — the exercises point back to the core textbook.
- It must not be cited as "the Grade 11 regular textbook": there is no such student textbook in this band.

### Held-copy defects

None in the file itself: all 11 packs present and complete, each ending with its thanks page; no duplicated or missing pages. The file is a clean digital PDF with a working text layer.

### Questions 2 and 3 (tree vs regular; track split)

- **Question 2:** the regular file is a MoEYS self-study supplement (11 packs, 152 pages); the tree file is a 31-page teacher-made handout (Run Ponleu) covering only Basic Chapter 3, Lesson 1. Different files, different authors, different purposes — see `g11-tree.md` and the band summary.
- **Question 3:** there is **no standalone "regular" student textbook**. The "regular" label in the record refers to this self-study supplement for the **Basic** track. The two real Grade 11 student books are Basic (7 chapters) and Advanced (8 chapters); see the band summary for the full track-split map.


## 8. `g11-tree.pdf` — Grade 11 trigonometric-functions handout (Run Ponleu, teacher-made)

**Band C1 — Grade 10–11.** File: `g11-tree.pdf` (record name `mathematics-grade-11.pdf`) — **31 PDF pages, 2,065,132 bytes, sha256 2c2dba6c905b…** (verified against `grab-results.json`, all 36 files OK).

### How read

**Text-layer read.** The file has a clean Unicode Khmer text layer (8,248 Khmer chars), so the whole file was read from the text layer; one page (PDF p17, the worked example and the start of Section IV) was also read visually to pin the section title and verify the arithmetic. No OCR.

### Identity

- **Title as printed:** គណិតវិទ្យាថ្នាក់ទី១១ | អនុគមន៍ត្រីកោណមាត្រ — *Grade 11 Mathematics | Trigonometric Functions* (header on every page).
- **What it is:** NOT a MoEYS textbook. It is a **teacher-made lesson handout** — a single lesson (Lesson 1, Trigonometric Functions) laid out as a slide-style document with a dark-blue/orange header band on every page.
- **Author:** **បង្រៀនដោយ រ៊ុន ពនលឺ — "Taught by Run Ponleu"** (footer on every page). No other author, publisher, year or ISBN anywhere in the file.
- **Grade:** 11. **Language:** Khmer, with standard math notation.

### Printed-page offset

**Printed = PDF.** Verified at the first page (printed 1) and the last page (printed 31, shown as an orange page-number box on PDF p17 and throughout). The file is 31 pages, numbered 1–31.

### Contents

The whole file is **one lesson: Lesson 1 — Trigonometric Functions**, corresponding to Basic Chapter 3, Lesson 1 (student book printed pp 68–87), but written as a self-contained handout with its own worked examples and exercises.

- **I. Angle measure (រង្វាស់មុំ), pp 1–2.** Directed angles: (OPo, OP) = α + k360°, k ∈ ℤ; radians; converting degrees to radians (30°, 45°, 150°) and radians to degrees (π/…, 3π/…). Worked examples verified correct in the text layer: α = 45° + k360° gives 405° for k = 1 and −315° for k = −1; (OPo, OP) = 120° + (−155°) = −35°.
- **II. Trigonometric functions, pp 3–6.** sin, cos, tan, cot defined on the unit circle; signs by quadrant; values at special angles.
- **III. Properties of trigonometric functions, pp 6–16.** Identities and reduction formulas: opposite angles sin(−θ) = −sin θ, cos(−θ) = cos θ, tan(−θ) = −tan θ, cot(−θ) = −cot θ; complementary angles (π/2 − θ); supplementary angles (π − θ); (π/2 + θ) formulas: cos(π/2 + θ) = −sin θ, tan(π/2 + θ) = −cot θ, cot(π/2 + θ) = −tan θ. Worked: sin(−39π/4) = sin(π/4) = √2/2 (p10); sin(−π/3) = −√3/2, cos(−π/3) = 1/2, cot(−π/3) = −√3/3, tan(−π/3) = −√3 (p11); sin 135° = sin(180° − 45°) = √2/2 (p13); B = sin(5π/6) + cos(3π/4) + sin(−π/6) = −√2/2 (p14); sin 210° (p15). Worked example (p17, visually checked): A = [cos(−288°)·cot 72°] / [tan(−162°)·sin 108°] − tan 18° = 0 — steps: cos(−288°) = cos 72°, cot 72° = tan 18°, tan(−162°) = tan 18°, sin 108° = cos 18° → A = cos 72°/cos 18° − tan 18° = sin 18°/cos 18° − tan 18° = 0. Correct.
- **IV. Studying trigonometric functions (សិក្សាអនុគមន៍ត្រីកោណមាត្រ), pp 17–28.** (ក) Domain (p17): sin x and cos x — D = ℝ = (−∞, +∞); tan x — D = ℝ − {(2k+1)π/2}, k ∈ ℤ; cot x — D = ℝ − {kπ}, k ∈ ℤ. (ខ) Period (pp 18–19): sin and cos have period 2π; tan and cot have period π. Even/odd functions (p20): y = x − sin x is odd; y = tan x · cot³x — even or odd? (worked). Variation tables and graphs (pp 21–24): ① y = sin x (domain ℝ, period 2π, odd); ② y = cos x (period 2π, even); ③ y = tan x (period π, odd); ④ y = cot x (period π, odd). Worked graphing examples (pp 25–28): y = 2sin x on [0, 2π]; y = 2cos x on [−π, π]; y = (1/2)tan x on [−π/2, π/2]; y = (1/2)cot x on [−π/2, π/2].
- **V. Practice exercises (ផ្នែកលំហាត់អនុវត្តន៍), pp 29–31.** 14 numbered exercises: (1) convert 200°, −100°, 225°, −240°, 135°, 330°, 570°, 360° to radians; (2) convert 2π/3, 5π/4, 5π/6, 5π/7, 7π/4, −11π/6, −5π/2, −7π/2, π/9 to degrees; (3) given sin θ + cos θ = −1/2 with π < θ < 2π, compute sin θ·cos θ and related values; (4)–(12) further identities, values and graphing problems; (13) triangle QRS with PQ = 12 cm, QR = 5 cm, RP = 13 cm — explain why ∠PQR is a right angle, then find sin QPR and cos PRS; (14) show cos θ·(1/(1 − sin θ) − 1/(1 + sin θ)) = k·tan θ and find k (as listed on pp 29–31).

### Confirmed book errors

**One sign slip.** p20, Example 1 (y = x − sin x): the printed working writes f(−x) = −x + sin(−x) = −x − sin x = −(x + sin x) = −f(x) — the first step drops the minus in f(−x) = (−x) − sin(−x), and −(x + sin x) is not −f(x) (= −x + sin x). The conclusion (odd) is still correct; only the printed working is wrong. All other worked examples checked are correct (angle conversions, reduction formulas, the A = 0 computation on p17, the B = −√2/2 computation on p14).

### Honest negatives

- This is **one lesson only** — Basic Chapter 3, Lesson 1 (trigonometric functions). It does not cover trig formulas (Lesson 2) or trig equations/inequalities (Lesson 3), let alone any other chapter.
- It is a teacher's handout, not a textbook: no table of contents, no index, no answers to the practice exercises (no answer key in the file).
- It is not a MoEYS publication and must not be cited as one.

### Held-copy defects

None in the file itself: all 31 pages present, clean digital PDF, complete. The exercise section (pp 29–31) ends at exercise 14 with no closing page — the handout simply stops there; whether a further page (e.g. answers) existed in the original cannot be told from the file.

### Question 2 (tree vs regular — verdict)

**Different files; neither is the other.** The tree file is a 31-page teacher-made handout (Run Ponleu) covering only Basic Chapter 3, Lesson 1. The regular file (`g11-regular.pdf`) is a 152-page MoEYS file of 11 self-study packs covering Basic Chapters 3, 4, 5 and 7. They overlap in scope only on Chapter 3, Lesson 1 — the tree file's whole content is roughly the same ground as the regular file's Pack 1 — but they are different documents: different author (teacher vs MoEYS), different layout (slide-style handout vs self-study packs), different length (31 vs 152 pages), different content (the tree file has its own worked examples, e.g. the A = 0 computation, and its own 14 practice exercises, which do not appear in the regular file). The tree file is **not** a scan of the regular file, **not** an extract of it, and **not** the Basic student book (whose Lesson 1 is 20 printed pages in a different layout with different examples). See the band summary for the full comparison.


## 9. `g11-geometry-tips.pdf` — Grade 11 Geometry Tips (handwritten formula compendium)

**Band C1 — Grade 10–11.** File: `g11-geometry-tips.pdf` — **32 PDF pages, 9,243,528 bytes, sha256 e15c397844a8…** (verified against `grab-results.json`, all 36 files OK).

### How read

**Visual read, all 32 pages** (110 DPI renders, PDF pp 1–32). The file is a CamScanner scan of a handwritten book; its text layer is essentially empty (736 chars total, mostly "Scanned by CamScanner" on every page). No tesseract; no OCR. All content below was read from the page images. Handwriting is legible throughout, though a few symbols on the last two content pages are small; where a reading could not be pinned, it is flagged as unresolved rather than guessed.

### Identity

- **Title as printed:** ធរណីមាត្រ — *Geometry*, Grade 11 (cover, PDF p1, red handwritten title, rabbit mascot drawing, small diamond logo reading គន្លឹះ — *Tips*).
- **What it is:** NOT a MoEYS textbook and NOT a student textbook at all. It is a handwritten **formula and proof-method compendium** — a student's or teacher's "tips" notebook (the file name "Geometry Tips" matches the content): area formulas, then a long numbered catalogue (sections I–XXX) of how to prove standard facts of solid geometry (incidence, parallelism, perpendicularity, loci).
- **Author / publisher / year / ISBN:** **none printed anywhere in the held file.** No title page, no imprint, no colophon, no author name, no date. The cover carries only the title, the grade and the mascot. Do not cite it as a MoEYS book.
- **Language:** Khmer (handwritten), with standard math notation (∈, ⊂, ∩, ∥, ⊥, ∅, Δ for a line).

### Printed-page offset

**No printed folios visible in the held scan** — the pages appear unnumbered (page numbers, if the original had any, are outside the scan frame or absent). Citations below therefore use **PDF page numbers** (PDF pN), which equal the scan order 1–32. The cover is PDF p1; content runs PDF pp 2–32.

### Contents — section by section

#### Area formulas (PDF pp 2–4)

- **PDF p2 (រូបមន្ត / Formulas):** area ratio of similar triangles S/S′ = k²; quadrilateral areas — rectangle S = length × width, square S = side², parallelogram S = base × height, trapezium S = (upper base + lower base) × height / 2, rhombus S = (diagonal × diagonal) / 2; triangle areas S = ah/2, right triangle S = ab/2.
- **PDF p3:** triangle area S = base × height / 2; trigonometric form S = ½bc·sin A = ½ac·sin B = ½ab·sin C; Heron's formula S = √[p(p−a)(p−b)(p−c)], p = (a+b+c)/2; circle S = πR² = ¼πD²; circular sector S = ½αR² (α in radians).
- **PDF p4:** circular segment area — S_AMB = S_OAMB − S_OAB when α < π rad, and S_AMB = S_OAMB + S_OAB when α > π rad (sector minus/plus triangle OAB).

#### Geometry in space — proof methods (PDF pp 4–32)

The rest of the book is one long catalogue, "ធរណីមាត្រក្នុងលំហ" (Geometry in Space), organised as numbered sections (Roman numerals I–XXX) with numbered items. Each item states a fact to prove and the "បើដឹង" (if we know) conditions that give it. This is the whole content — there are no exercises, no worked problems, no answers.

- **I — ទីតាំង (Positions), PDF p4–5.** ① point and line: M ∈ (d) / M ∉ (d). ② point and plane: M ∈ (P) / M ∉ (P). ③ line and plane, 3 positions: (d) ⊂ (P); (d) ∥ (P); (d) ∩ (P) = {M}. ④ plane and plane, 3 positions: coincident; (P) ∩ (Q) = (Δ); (P) ∥ (Q). ⑤ line and line, 4 positions: coincident, intersecting (d) ∩ (d′) = {I}, parallel (d) ∥ (d′), skew (not in one plane).
- **II — កំណត់ប្លង់ (Determining a plane), PDF p6.** A plane is determined by: 3 non-collinear points; 1 line and 1 point off it; 2 intersecting lines; 2 parallel lines.
- **III — ចំណុច ∈ បន្ទាត់ (Point on a line), PDF p6.** ① (d) through M ⟹ M ∈ (d). ② (d) ∩ (d′) = {I} ⟹ I ∈ (d) and I ∈ (d′). ③ (d) ∩ (P) = {I} ⟹ I ∈ (d) and I ∈ (P). ④ I ∈ (P) ∩ (Q) with (P) ∩ (Q) = (Δ) ⟹ I ∈ (Δ).
- **IV — ចំណុច ∈ ប្លង់ (Point on a plane), PDF p7–8.** ① (P) through A ⟹ A ∈ (P). ② Plane determined by A, B, C ⟹ A, B, C ∈ (P). ③ M ∈ (d), (d) ⊂ (P) ⟹ M ∈ (P). ④ (d) ∩ (P) = {M} ⟹ M ∈ (d) and M ∈ (P). ⑤ (item 5, PDF p8) point in a plane parallel to another plane.
- **V — ចំណុចរបស់ប្លង់ ២ (Point of two planes), PDF p8.** ① M ∈ (P), M ∈ (Q) ⟹ M ∈ (P) ∩ (Q). ② M ∈ (d), M ∈ (d′), (d) ⊂ (P), (d′) ⊂ (Q) ⟹ M ∈ (P) ∩ (Q).
- **VI — បន្ទាត់ ⊂ ប្លង់ (Line in a plane), PDF p8–9.** How to show a line lies in a plane: ① through two points of the plane: (d) through A, B with A, B ∈ (P) ⟹ (d) ⊂ (P). ② plane determined by two parallel lines. ③ plane through the line. ④ the intersection line of two planes lies in both. ⑤ line through a point of (P), perpendicular to a plane (Q) with (P) ⊥ (Q). ⑥ line through a point of (P), parallel to (Q) with (P) ∥ (Q).
- **VII — Finding the intersection point of a line and a plane, PDF p10.** ① a common point M ∈ (d), M ∈ (P) ⟹ (d) ∩ (P) = {M}. ② via a line (d) in (P) meeting (Δ): (Δ) ∩ (d) = {I}, (Δ) ⊄ (P) ⟹ (Δ) ∩ (P) = {I}.
- **VIII — Finding the intersection line of two planes, PDF p11–12.** ① two planes through one line (Δ) ⟹ (P) ∩ (Q) = (Δ). ② a line lying in both planes. ③ two common points M, N ∈ (P) ∩ (Q) ⟹ (P) ∩ (Q) = (MN); variants finding I and J separately, e.g. (a) ∩ (P) = {I}, (b) ∩ (P) = {J} with (a), (b) ⊂ (Q) ⟹ (P) ∩ (Q) = (IJ); and (a) ∩ (a′) = {M}, (b) ∩ (P) = {N} ⟹ (P) ∩ (Q) = (MN).
- **IX — Lines and intersections, PDF p13.** How to show two lines meet: ① two non-parallel lines in one plane (a) ⊂ (P), (b) ⊂ (P), (a) ∦ (b) ⟹ (a) ∩ (b) = {I} (with a note: two lines in one plane either meet or are parallel). ② a common point I ∈ (a), I ∈ (b) ⟹ (a) ∩ (b) = {I}. ③ diagonals of a parallelogram: ABCD a parallelogram in (P) ⟹ [AC] ∩ [BD] = {O}.
- **X — Proving 3 points are collinear, PDF p14.** ① I, J, K ∈ (P) ∩ (Q) ⟹ they lie on (Δ), the intersection line of (P) and (Q) ⟹ collinear. ② via a line in a single plane (text partly abbreviated; the method is to show the three points lie on one line in one plane).
- **XI — Proving two lines are skew, PDF p14.** Method by contradiction: suppose (a) ∩ (b) = {O}, then draw a line (c) through O and use the planes (a, c) and (b, c) to reach a contradiction.
- **XII — Proving two lines are skew (by contradiction), PDF p15.** Assume they meet, or assume they are parallel — contradiction.
- **XIII — Proving two lines are coplanar, PDF p15.** ① they intersect: (a) ∩ (b) = {I} ⟹ coplanar. ② they are parallel: (a) ∥ (b) ⟹ coplanar. ③ determined by points in one plane: A, B, C, D ∈ (P) ⟹ (AB), (CD) coplanar in (P).
- **XIV — Proving two lines are coincident, PDF p15–16.** ① A ∈ (d), B ∈ (d) ⟹ (AB) and (d) are the same line. ② (AB) ⊂ (P) and (AB) ⊂ (Q) ⟹ (P) ∩ (Q) = (AB), so (AB) and (Δ) coincide. ③ two lines through M, both ∥ (Δ) ⟹ they coincide (uniqueness of parallels). ④ two lines through M, both ⊥ (P) ⟹ they coincide.
- **XV — Two lines are parallel, PDF p17–19.** ① two lines in one plane that do not meet: (a), (b) in (P), (a) ∩ (b) = ∅ ⟹ (a) ∥ (b). ② (d) ∥ (d′), (Δ) and (d′) coincident ⟹ (d) ∥ (Δ). ③ transitivity: (d) ∥ (Δ), (d′) ∥ (Δ) ⟹ (d) ∥ (d′). ④ (d) ∥ (d′), (d) ⊂ (P), (d′) ⊂ (Q), (P) ∩ (Q) = (Δ) ⟹ (Δ) ∥ (d) ∥ (d′). ⑤ (d) ∥ (P), (d) ∥ (Q), (P) ∩ (Q) = (Δ) ⟹ (d) ∥ (Δ). ⑥ (d) ∥ (Q), (d) ⊂ (P), (P) ∩ (Q) = (Δ) ⟹ (d) ∥ (Δ). ⑦ a plane cutting two parallel planes: (P) ∥ (Q), (R) ∩ (P) = (Δ), (R) ∩ (Q) = (Δ′) ⟹ (Δ) ∥ (Δ′). ⑧ two lines ⊥ one plane: (d) ⊥ (P), (d′) ⊥ (P) ⟹ (d) ∥ (d′). ⑨ by Thales (converse of the intercept theorem) in a plane: |OA|/|AB| = |OC|/|CD| ⟹ (AC) ∥ (BD); also by the properties of a parallelogram, trapezium, rhombus, rectangle, square and other figures.
- **XVI — Line parallel to a plane, PDF p19–20.** ① (d) ∩ (P) = ∅ ⟹ (d) ∥ (P). ② (d) ⊄ (P), (d) ∥ (d′), (d′) ⊂ (P) ⟹ (d) ∥ (P). ③ (P) ∥ (Q), (d) ⊂ (P) ⟹ (d) ∩ (Q) = ∅ ⟹ (d) ∥ (Q). ④ transitivity via a line in the plane. ⑤ via a cutting plane (R) through (d). ⑥ (d) ⊥ (R), (P) ⊥ (R) ⟹ (d) ∥ (P).
- **XVII — Plane parallel to plane, PDF p21–22.** ① (P) ∩ (Q) = ∅ ⟹ (P) ∥ (Q). ② plane through two intersecting lines each ∥ (Q). ③ two planes each through two intersecting lines, pairwise parallel. ④ transitivity: (P) ∥ (R), (Q) ∥ (R) ⟹ (P) ∥ (Q). ⑤ (P) ∥ (R) and (Q) coincident with (P) ⟹ (Q) ∥ (R). ⑥ two planes ⊥ one line: (P) ⊥ (d), (Q) ⊥ (d) ⟹ (P) ∥ (Q).
- **XVIII — Proving two planes coincide, PDF p23.** ① through one point M and ∥ a third plane (R). ② through one point M and ⊥ a line (d). ③ through a line (d) and ⊥ a plane (R), with (d) not ⊥ (R).
- **XIX — Two lines perpendicular / orthogonal, PDF p24.** ① the angle between them is 90° (∠xOy = 90° ⟹ (d) ⊥ (d′)). ② via a parallel line: (d) ∥ (d′), (Δ) ⊥ (d) ⟹ (Δ) ⊥ (d′). ③ line ⊥ a plane: (Δ) ⊥ (P), (d) ⊂ (P) ⟹ (Δ) ⊥ (d).
- **XX — Line perpendicular to a line (three perpendiculars), PDF p25–26.** ① (d) ⊥ (d′) and (d) ∩ (d′) = {I}. ② (Δ) ⊥ (P), (d) ⊂ (P) ⟹ (Δ) ⊥ (d). ③ **Theorem of three perpendiculars and its converse:** (Δ) ⊂ (P), (Δ) ⊥ [BC] at C, [BC] the orthogonal projection of [AC] onto (P) ⟹ (Δ) ⊥ [AC]; and conversely (Δ) ⊥ [AC] at C ⟹ (Δ) ⊥ [BC]. ④ via angle bisectors: in a triangle, the internal bisector [Ax) and external bisector [Ay) of one angle are perpendicular; also via properties of a rhombus, isosceles triangle, right triangle, etc.
- **XXI — Line perpendicular to a plane, PDF p27–28.** ① (Δ) perpendicular to every line of (P). ② (Δ) ⊥ two intersecting lines (a), (b) of (P). ③ (a) ∥ (b), (P) ⊥ (a) ⟹ (P) ⊥ (b). ④ (P) ∥ (Q), (Δ) ⊥ (P) ⟹ (Δ) ⊥ (Q). ⑤ (P) ⊥ (Q) with intersection (Δ); (d) ⊂ (Q), (d) ⊥ (Δ) ⟹ (d) ⊥ (P). ⑥ (P) ⊥ (R), (Q) ⊥ (R), (P) ∩ (Q) = (Δ) ⟹ (Δ) ⊥ (R).
- **XXII — Plane perpendicular to plane, PDF p29.** ① the dihedral angle of (P) and (Q) is 90° ⟹ (P) ⊥ (Q). ② a plane through a line ⊥ the other plane: (d) ⊂ (Q), (d) ⊥ (P) ⟹ (P) ⊥ (Q). ③ (P) ∥ (Q), (R) ⊥ (P) ⟹ (R) ⊥ (Q).
- **XXIII — Angles in a circle, PDF p30.** A list of angle types in a circle: central angle (vertex at the centre), inscribed angle (vertex on the circle), angle with vertex inside the circle, angle with vertex outside the circle, angle formed by a tangent and a chord, angle between two chords/secants. (The handwriting on this page is a bare list with no diagrams; the exact wording of items 6–7 was not pinned.)
- **XXIV — Triangle, PDF p31.** Triangle congruence criteria (two triangles with two equal angles and the included side equal are congruent; two triangles with two equal sides and the included angle equal are congruent). Two boxed notes on angle sums — see "PDF p31 boxes" below.
- **XXX — Locus of points (សំណុំចំណុច), PDF p32.** ① lines through M parallel to a plane (P) fill the plane (R) through M parallel to (P). ② lines through M perpendicular to a line (d) fill the plane (R) through M perpendicular to (d). ③ points equidistant from the endpoints of [AB] fill the perpendicular bisector plane of [AB]. ④ points equidistant from two planes (P, Q) fill the bisector planes of their dihedral angles. ⑤ points at distance l from O fill the sphere centre O, radius R = l.

### Confirmed book errors

**None confirmed.** Every formula and method checked against standard geometry is correct as written (area formulas, sector/segment formulas, all incidence/parallel/perpendicular criteria, the three-perpendiculars theorem, loci). No worked numerical example exists in the book to check against — it is a list of formulas and proof recipes, not worked problems. One **probable misprint** (not counted as confirmed): PDF p31, section XXIV note, first box — as read at 300 DPI it states "sum of two angles of a triangle < each angle < sum of two angles of a triangle", which is self-contradictory as printed; the booklet likely misprinted one term (or one term is misread at scan resolution).

### PDF p31 boxes — resolved on re-read at 300 DPI

- **First box (under "* ក្នុងត្រីកោណ :").** Now readable at glyph level: "ផលបូកមុំ២នៃត្រីកោណ < មុំនិមួយៗ < ផលបូកមុំ២នៃត្រីកោណ" — "sum of two angles of a triangle < each angle < sum of two angles of a triangle". As printed this chain is self-contradictory (it asserts sum < each < the same sum), so it is most likely a misprint in the booklet — or one term is misread at scan resolution (the middle term "មុំនិមួយៗ" and the numerals are the least certain glyphs). The earlier guess that the box relates the sum of two angles to a straight angle (2d) is **not** borne out: no "2d" appears in the box. (For reference: the angle sum of a triangle is exactly 180° = 2d; of a quadrilateral exactly 360° = 4d; of an n-gon (n−2)·180°.)
- **Second box (under "* ក្នុងពហុកោណ :").** Confirmed: "ផលបូកមុំពហុកោណ < 4d" — "sum of angles of a polygon < 4d" (d = right angle, so < 360°).

### Honest negatives

- This is a **reference notebook, not a textbook**: no explanations, no worked examples, no exercises, no answers, no chapter structure, no index. It cannot stand alone as a course source; it is useful only as a check-list of the solid-geometry proof methods that the Grade 10 Chapter 11 and Grade 11 Advanced Chapter 8 textbooks teach in full.
- It covers **only** plane-area formulas and solid-geometry incidence/parallel/perpendicular/locus methods. No trigonometry, no vectors, no coordinates, no transformations, no sequences, no statistics, no algebra at all.
- No author, publisher, year or ISBN — it cannot be cited as a MoEYS source and must not be presented as one.

### Held-copy defects

- The held file is a **CamScanner phone scan** (32 pages), not a digital original: pages are slightly skewed, contrast is uneven, and "Scanned by CamScanner" is printed at the foot of every page. All content is legible, including the two small boxes on PDF p31 (read at 300 DPI; the first box is a probable misprint — see above).
- No pages are missing or duplicated as far as the scan shows: the sections run continuously I–XXIV then XXX (sections XXV–XXIX do not appear — whether they were never written or are missing from the scan cannot be told from the file; the last page, PDF p32, ends mid-list with item ⑤ and no closing page).
- The scan is complete in the sense that it starts with the cover and ends with section XXX; there is no back cover or colophon page in the file.


# BAND C2a — Grade 12 MoEYS books (6 files, 1,319 PDF pages)

The Grade 12 Advanced and Basic student books (12th printing 2020) with both teacher guides (TRUE COMPANIONs), plus the 2013 printing of the Basic book and the school-made exam-paper collection. Citation check: **77 anchors (69 passed, 4 corrected, 4 not found)** — check-band-c2a.md. The 2013 printing (`g12-2000.pdf`) and the exam-paper collection (`g12-regular.pdf`) were NOT anchor-checked: the first is the same book as the 2020 Basic printing (cover-proved at every point checked), the second is an exam-paper collection, not a textbook. Settled in this band: the 2013 printing is the Basic book, 5th printing 2013 — never a "2000 edition"; the 341 KB "regular" file is school-made exam papers, not a textbook; there is no regular Grade 12 student textbook in hand.


## 1. `g12-advanced.pdf` — Grade 12 Advanced student

**File:** `g12-advanced.pdf` — 257 PDF pages, 39,318,578 bytes, sha256 `61b8a08d4d78`
**Band:** C2a (Cambodia Grade 12 mathematics, 21st maths read)
**Language:** Khmer. Filed in English.

### How this book was read

- **Text layer:** legacy-font mojibake. `pdftotext -layout` extracts Khmer prose as garbled Latin characters (fonts `KampongThomKS`, `PhnomPenh95`, `AksarJharKS-Bold`, `Times-*`, `Symbol`, `Impact`). The ToUnicode mapping is lossy — the same Latin character maps to several Khmer glyphs — so char-by-char decoding is unreliable. **But maths, numbers and formulas extract legibly** (e.g. `√3 = 1.732058`, `lim Uₙ = (1+√13)/2`), so worked examples were error-checked from the text layer and the numbers re-verified by hand.
- **Visual read:** cover, title page, imprint, preface and table of contents (PDF pp. 1–5), all nine chapter-opening pages, and the section starts of 1.1, 2.1, 3.1, 4.1, 4.2, 5.1, 5.2, 6.1, 6.2, 6.3, 7.1, 8.1, 8.2, 8.3, 9.1, plus the end page (PDF p. 257), rendered with `pdftoppm` (110–200 DPI) and read visually. Section starts 3.2 and 7.2 were filed from the table of contents and the teacher guide's lesson plans (not read in detail in the student book).
- **Printed-page numbers** below are the book's own printed numbers, verified at the chapter starts and section starts. Never invented.

### Unit-by-unit filing (printed pp.)

#### Chapter 1 — Limits of sequences (pp. 1–18)

- **Chapter opening (p. 1):** nested radicals and the calculator character of limits. Verified: √3 = 1.732058; √(3 + √3) = 2.175327; √(3 + √(3 + √3)) = 2.274934; the limit of the nested sequence is (1 + √13)/2 = 2.302775 ✓.
- **1.1 Limits of sequences (p. 2):** infinite sequences; definition a₁, a₂, …, aₙ, …; examples 1, ½, ⅓, …, 1/n and 1, 4, 9, 16, …, n². Exercise: 3/1, 5/4, 7/7, 9/10, 11/13, … → aₙ = (2n + 1)/(3n − 2) ✓.
- Convergent/divergent sequences; geometric sequences and their limits (r > 1 → +∞, r = 1 → 1, r = 0 → 0, 0 < |r| < 1 → 0, r ≤ −1 oscillating — the student book's table is correct; the **teacher guide's** copy of this table has errors, see `g12-advanced-teacher.md`).
- Geometric series: Sₙ = 1 + 1/2 + … + (1/2)ⁿ⁻¹ = 2(1 − (1/2)ⁿ), lim Sₙ = 2 ✓ (text-layer read, p. 13).

#### Chapter 2 — Derivatives of functions (pp. 19–40)

- **Chapter opening (p. 19):** portrait of Gottfried Wilhelm Leibniz (1646–1716) and a short history of derivatives.
- **2.1 The derivative function (p. 20):** the differential; Δy = f(x + Δx) − f(x), Δy/Δx, dy/dx as the slope of the tangent.
- Rules for differentiating functions; the derivative as a function; second derivatives. (Detailed worked examples in this chapter were checked from the text layer and the teacher guide's lesson plan; no errors found in the samples.)

#### Chapter 3 — Applications of the graph of a function (pp. 41–62)

- **Chapter opening (p. 41):** photograph of a building/street; function graphs as a tool for studying functions.
- **3.1 Irrational functions (p. 42):** objectives — graphs of y = √(ax + b) and y = √(ax² + bx + c). Worked example y = √(2x − 4): domain D = [2, +∞), y′ = 1/√(2x − 4) > 0 for x > 2, points (2,0), (4,2) ✓.
- **3.2 Inverse trigonometric functions (p. 54):** filed from the table of contents and the teacher guide's lesson plan; the section start was not read in detail in the student book.

#### Chapter 4 — Definite integrals (pp. 63–90)

- **Chapter opening (p. 63):** portraits and dates — Archimedes (287–212 BC), Eudoxus (406–359 BC), Johannes Kepler (1571–1630), Bonaventura Cavalieri (1598–1647), Leibniz, Newton (1642–1727), Augustin Louis Cauchy (1789–1847); a short history of definite integrals.
- **4.1 Definite integrals (p. 64):** the definite integral as the limit of rectangle areas. Objectives: define the definite integral as a limit of rectangle areas; compute areas; compute areas between curves and the lines x = a and x = b; compute areas of regions bounded by curves. Worked example: f(x) = x² + 1 on [0,1], n = 4, Δx = 1/4, S₄ = f(0)Δx + f(1/4)Δx + f(1/2)Δx + f(3/4)Δx = ¼ + 17/64 + 20/64 + 25/64 = 78/64 = 1.21875 ✓ (true integral 4/3 ≈ 1.333; a left sum, correctly computed).
- **4.2 Volume of solids and arc length (p. 76):** volume of solids of revolution — cross-sectional area A = π[f(x)]² ✓; arc length.

#### Chapter 5 — Differential equations (pp. 91–132)

- **Chapter opening (p. 91):** tsunami/coast photograph; falling-body example y″ = g, y′ = dy/dt = gt + v₀, y = ½gt² + v₀t + y₀.
- **5.1 First-order differential equations (p. 92):** objectives — define 1st-order DEs; find their solutions; solve them; find solutions under initial conditions; construct DEs from families of functions; explain physical applications. Worked example: y = cx − 3 → y′ = c; eliminating c, y + 3 = xy′ → **y′ − (1/x)y = 3/x** ✓.
- **5.2 Second-order differential equations (p. 112):** objectives — define 2nd-order DEs; find the characteristic equation; solve with and without initial conditions; physical applications. Worked example: f₁(x) = e^(−x), f₂(x) = e^(3x); both satisfy f″ − 2f′ − 3f = 0 (f₁: e^(−x) + 2e^(−x) − 3e^(−x) = 0 ✓; f₂: 9e^(3x) − 6e^(3x) − 3e^(3x) = 0 ✓); the general solution y = c₁f₁ + c₂f₂ satisfies the equation ✓.

#### Chapter 6 — Probability distributions (pp. 133–172)

- **Chapter opening (p. 133):** Airbus A380 photograph; the binomial formula P(X = x) = C(n,x)·pˣ·(1 − p)ⁿ⁻ˣ.
- **6.1 Probability distributions — binomial (p. 134):** coin-tossed-twice example, X = number of heads, P = 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.25 ✓.
- **6.2 Bernoulli trials / distribution (p. 150):** the 5 conditions — n independent trials, success/failure, P = p, q = 1 − p, X = number of successes.
- **6.3 Normal distribution (p. 160):** the density function, mean μ, standard deviation σ; worked example on sack masses (82.5–83.5 kg).
- **End of the book (printed p. 252):** a standard normal distribution z-table (P(0 ≤ z) values, z = 0.00–3.0). Spot-checked: z = 1.96 → 0.4750 ✓; z = 1.0 → 0.3413 ✓; z = 2.0 → 0.4772 ✓; z = 3.0 → 0.4987 ✓.

#### Chapter 7 — Vectors in space (pp. 173–200)

- **Chapter opening (p. 173):** satellite-dish and helicopter photograph; Giuseppe Peano (1858–1932). (The same photograph is reused as the Chapter 9 opening — see "Held-copy defects".)
- **7.1 Scalar product of two vectors in space (p. 174):** the section teaches the **cross product** as well as the scalar product, despite its title. Verified: ū = 2î + ĵ − 2k̂, v̂ = î − 3ĵ + 2k̂ → ū × v̂ = −4î − 6ĵ − 7k̂ ✓; the formula ū × v̂ = (u₂v₃ − u₃v₂)î − (u₁v₃ − u₃v₁)ĵ + (u₁v₂ − u₂v₁)k̂ ✓. (One middle line "(2×2+(2))" is terse shorthand; the final answer is right.)
- **7.2 Applications of the scalar product (p. 186):** filed from the table of contents and the teacher guide's lesson plan (which covers distance from a point to a line, planes and spheres — see `g12-advanced-teacher.md`); the section start was not read in detail in the student book.

#### Chapter 8 — Divisibility and Euclidean division (pp. 201–234)

- **Chapter opening (p. 201):** portraits — Andrew Wiles (b. 1953), Carl Friedrich Gauss (1777–1855), Étienne Bézout (1730–1783), Pierre de Fermat (1601–1665); Fermat's Last Theorem aⁿ + bⁿ = cⁿ (n > 2); Pythagorean triples 3, 4, 5; Wiles's proof 1993, published 1994.
- **8.1 Divisibility in ℤ (p. 202):** 24 is divisible by 3, 49 by 7; multiples …, −5k, …, −20, −15, −10, −5, 0, 5, …, 5k, …; a = bq definition, b | a; q = 0 ⇒ a = 0.
- **8.2 Prime numbers (p. 210):** definition — a natural number n > 1 whose only divisors are 1 and n; 37 is prime, 36 is not (divisors 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 9, 18, 36); theorem — every natural number n > 1 has a prime divisor (the smallest divisor other than 1). Objectives: define primes, state the theorem, find primes. (The table of contents' wording for 8.2 was misread in an earlier pass as "combinations" — the section is prime numbers; corrected here.)
- **8.3 Common divisors and common multiples — GCD/LCM (p. 218):** δ = PGCD(a, b) / GCD(a, b); divisors of 42 and 30 → GCD(42, 30) = 6; GCD(36, 12) = 12 ✓. (The teacher guide's answers for this section's exercises contain several wrong answers — see `g12-advanced-teacher.md`; the student book's own examples are correct.)

#### Chapter 9 — Parametric equations and polar coordinates (pp. 235–252)

- **Chapter opening (p. 235):** the same satellite-dish and helicopter photograph as Chapter 7, p. 173 (repeated illustration — see "Held-copy defects"). Parametric example x = t, y = t² → y = x²; polar coordinates introduced.
- **9.1 Parametric equations and polar coordinates (p. 236):** projectile at 45°, v₀ = 48 cm/s; the equations are internally consistent: y = −x²/72 + x, x = 24√2 t, y = −16t² + 24√2 t; at t = 1: (24√2, 24√2 − 16) ✓; at t = 0: (0,0) ✓; graph vertex (24√2 ≈ 33.9, ≈ 17.9) ✓. Note: the example uses **cm/s units throughout** (v₀ = 48 cm/s, g = 32 cm/s²) — a toy scale, but the equations are consistent. (The teacher guide writes v₀ = 45 cm/s for the same example — a guide slip, see `g12-advanced-teacher.md`.)

### Confirmed book errors

**None confirmed in the student book** in the samples checked. Every worked example and formula spot-checked above is correct. (The teacher guide's answer key for this book's exercises contains multiple wrong answers — those are guide errors, filed in `g12-advanced-teacher.md`, not student-book errors.)

### NOT errors — checked and cleared (do not file as errors)

- **Chapter 1, geometric series:** Sₙ = 1 + 1/2 + … + (1/2)ⁿ⁻¹ = 2(1 − (1/2)ⁿ), lim Sₙ = 2 — correct; an earlier suspicion came from the (1/2)ⁿ⁻¹ last term being lost in text extraction.
- **Chapter 1, nested radicals (p. 1):** all four values verified correct (see Chapter 1 above).
- **Chapter 7, 7.1 cross product:** the worked example and formula are correct; one terse middle line is shorthand, not an error.
- **Chapter 9, 9.1 projectile:** v₀ = 48 cm/s with y = −x²/72 + x is internally consistent (see above); the units are cm/s, not a mistake in the equations.

### Honest negatives

Topics a Grade 12 book might be expected to teach that this Advanced book does **NOT**:

- **No complex numbers** (they live in the separate topic booklet — another band's file — and are tested in the exam-paper collection `g12-regular.pdf`).
- **No matrices or determinants.**
- **No conics** (parabola, ellipse, hyperbola are Basic-only — see `g12-basic.md`).
- **No statistics beyond Chapter 6 distributions** — no descriptive statistics, no regression (those are Basic-only, Chapter 5).
- **No limits of functions** — only limits of sequences (Chapter 1); limits of functions are Basic-only (Chapter 1).
- **No primitives / indefinite integrals as a separate topic** (Basic Chapter 4.1); this book treats definite integrals only.

### Held-copy defects

- **Repeated illustration:** the Chapter 7 opening (printed p. 173) and the Chapter 9 opening (printed p. 235) use the **same satellite-dish and helicopter photograph**. A book defect (the two chapters share one stock image), not a scan defect.
- Otherwise complete: no missing, duplicated or unreadable pages found. All 257 PDF pages present; content runs printed pp. 1–252.

### Printed-page offset

**Printed = PDF − 5** throughout, verified at several chapter starts: Chapter 1 printed p. 1 = PDF p. 6; Chapter 2 printed p. 19 = PDF p. 24; Chapter 5 printed p. 91 = PDF p. 96; Chapter 9 printed p. 235 = PDF p. 240. Content ends at printed p. 252 (PDF p. 257). Front matter: cover PDF p. 1, title PDF p. 2, imprint PDF p. 3, preface PDF p. 4, table of contents PDF p. 5 (all unnumbered).

### Sources for this filing

- `g12-advanced.pdf` (257 pp., sha `61b8a08d4d78`), text layer + visual reads as listed above.
- Companion: `g12-advanced-teacher.pdf` — see `g12-advanced-teacher.md`.
- Track comparison: see `band-c2a-summary.md`.


## 2. `g12-basic.pdf` — Grade 12 Basic student

**File:** `g12-basic.pdf` — 257 PDF pages, 34,506,047 bytes, sha256 `8212f04eed64`
**Band:** C2a (Cambodia Grade 12 mathematics, 21st maths read)
**Language:** Khmer. Filed in English.

### How this book was read

- **Text layer:** legacy-font mojibake. `pdftotext -layout` extracts Khmer prose as garbled Latin characters (same font family as the Advanced book). The ToUnicode mapping is lossy, so prose was not decoded char-by-char. **Maths, numbers and formulas extract legibly**, so worked examples were error-checked from the text layer and re-verified by hand.
- **Visual read:** cover, title page, imprint, preface and table of contents (PDF pp. 1–5), chapter openings (Chapters 1, 3, 5, 6), and the section starts of 1.1 (p. 7, printed), 3.1, 4.1, 4.2, 5.1, 5.2, 6.1, plus the answers-section start (printed p. 221) and the end page (printed p. 252), rendered with `pdftoppm` (110–200 DPI) and read visually. Section starts 1.2, 2.1, 2.2, 3.2, 3.3, 6.2, 6.3 were filed from the table of contents plus the text layer and the teacher guide's lesson plans (2.1 and 2.2 were also read visually in this session; 1.2, 3.2, 3.3, 6.2, 6.3 were not read in detail in the student book — see the notes under each section).
- **Printed-page numbers** below are the book's own printed numbers, verified at the chapter starts and section starts. Never invented.

- Title: "គណិតវិទ្យា" (Mathematics), "កម្រិតមូលដ្ឋាន" (Basic / Foundation level), "ថ្នាក់ទី ១២" (Grade 12). Red title; same sphere-and-curve cover graphic as the 2013 printing.
- Publisher: "គ្រឹះស្ថានបោះពុម្ពនិងចែកចាយ" (Publishing and Distribution House), Building 148, Preah Norodom Boulevard, Phnom Penh.
- Approval: MoEYS Prakas No. **3178**, 28 December **2011**.
- **"បោះពុម្ពលើកទី១២ ឆ្នាំ២០២០ ចំនួន១៥ ០០០ក្បាល" — 12th printing, 2020, 15,000 copies.**

- Pages carry the MoEYS watermarks `krou.moeys.gov.kh`, `facebook.com/moeys`, `youtube.com/moeys`.
