### Unit-by-unit filing (printed pp.)

#### Chapter 1 — Limits (pp. 1–42)

- **Chapter opening (p. 1):** pencil-curve illustration. Introduction: Grade 11 studied limits; this chapter covers limits of rational, trigonometric, exponential and logarithmic functions, supporting continuity, derivatives, graphing and solving equations.
- **1.1 Limits of functions (p. 2):** ε–δ definition of lim f(x) = L; infinite limits. Verified from the text layer: f(x) = 2x + 3, lim_{x→1} = 5 with δ = ε/2 ✓; lim_{x→−2}(2 + 5x) = −8, with α = 0.0004 for ε = 0.002 ✓; f(x) = x² − 3x + 10 = (x − 3/2)² + 31/4 ✓ (printed p. 6).
  - [Checker correction 2026-11-05: two items formerly filed in this bullet were **NOT FOUND** in the student book and removed — the (4x² + 5x − 1)/(x + 3) value table (f(−1) = −1, f(−2) = 5, f(0) = −1/3, f(1) = 2, f(2) = 5, f(3) = 25/3) was not located on printed pp. 2–6; the example is the **teacher guide's** own lesson content (guide printed p. 2, verified there — see `g12-basic-teacher.md`), not the student book's. The composite-functions example ((f∘g)(x) = 1/x + 1 for f = x + 1, g = 1/x) was likewise not located in the student book. Do not cite either from this book.]
  - **Printed p. 7 (read visually):** limit laws (items ក–ច) and worked examples — lim_{x→−1}(3x³ − 4x + 8) = 3(−1)³ − 4(−1) + 8 = 9 ✓ (it is 3x³, not 3x² — an earlier text-layer suspicion resolved); lim_{x→−4}5x³ = 5(−4)³ = −320 ✓; lim_{x→1}(x² − 1)/(x² − 3x + 2) = (x + 1)/(x − 2) = −2 ✓; practice limits lim_{x→+∞}(2x + 1)/(x − 1) = 2 ✓ and lim_{x→+∞}(2x − x²)/(3x + 5) = −∞ ✓. **This page also carries the book's one confirmed limit error — see "Confirmed book errors".**
- **1.2 Continuity of functions (p. 28):** filed from the table of contents, the teacher guide's Chapter 1 lesson plan, and the answers section (printed pp. 224–226, which give the continuity exercise answers — e.g. lim_{x→0⁻} f(x) = −√2, lim_{x→0⁺} f(x) = √2 = f(0), so f is continuous at x = 0; f(x) = log₃x for x ≥ 1 with lim_{x→1⁺} f(x) = 0 = f(1), and continuity at x = 1 requires a = 2; f(x) = x − 1 − sin x has a root in (0, π) since f(0)·f(π) < 0). The section start itself was not read in detail in the student book.

#### Chapter 2 — Derivatives of functions (pp. 43–62)

- **2.1 The derivative function (p. 44):** objectives — find the derivative of a function; find 1st and 2nd derivatives. The derivative at x₀ as the limit of Δy/Δx: y′₀ = f′(x₀) = lim_{Δx→0} Δy/Δx = lim_{x→x₀} [f(x) − f(x₀)]/(x − x₀) = lim_{h→0} [f(x₀ + h) − f(x₀)]/h ✓. Worked example: y = x² − 1 at x₀ = 2: Δy/Δx = h + 4 → f′(2) = 4 ✓. Section 2 then treats the derivative of a composite function (chain rule) via a factory example (18 shirts per hour per worker, 40 workers per hour → 720 shirts in 1 hour; y = f(x), x = g(t)).
- **2.2 Applications of the derivative (p. 56):** objectives — use derivatives to find maxima; solve maximum/minimum problems; find rates of change. Worked example 1 (a shop): price x per kg, quantity 4000 − 400(x − 5), profit P(x) = (x − 2)[4000 − 400(x − 5)] = 400(x − 2)(15 − x); P′(x) = 400(15 − x) − 400(x − 2) = 400(17 − 2x); P′(x) = 0 → x = 17/2 = 8.5 ✓.

#### Chapter 3 — Study of functions and graphing (pp. 63–108)

- **3.1 Rational functions (p. 64):** asymptotes. Worked example: f(x) = x − 1 − 6/(x − 2); the line y = x − 1 is an oblique asymptote since lim −6/(x − 2) = 0 ✓. Example 2: f(x) = (x² − 3)/(2x − 4).
- **3.2 Exponential functions (p. 86):** filed from the table of contents and the answers section (printed pp. 233–235: limits of exponential functions — lim_{x→+∞} e^{3x} = +∞, lim_{x→−∞} e^{3x} = 0, lim_{x→+∞} x·e^{x+1} = +∞, (1 + n/x)^x → e^x, ((x + 3)/(x − 1))^{x+2} → e⁴, (e^x − e^{−x})/x → 2; derivatives of exponential functions). The section start was not read in detail in the student book.
- **3.3 Logarithmic functions (p. 94):** filed from the table of contents and the answers section (printed p. 240: y = f(x) = (3x² − 7x + 15)/(x − 1) = 3x − 4 + 11/(x − 1), lim_{x→1} f(x) = ∞, vertical asymptote x = 1 — a rational-function answer; logarithmic-function answers sit nearby). The section start was not read in detail in the student book.

#### Chapter 4 — Integrals (pp. 109–144)

- **4.1 Primitives / indefinite integrals (p. 110):** falling body V(t) = gt → S(t) = ½gt² ✓; F′(x) = 2x → F(x) = x² ✓.
- **4.2 Definite integrals (p. 132):** objectives — define the definite integral of a function on an interval; compute definite integrals by the rules, by substitution, by parts; find a function from its definite integral. Worked example 1: t(x) = 5 + 3x (a production rate); T(x) = ∫t(x)dx = ∫(5 + 3x)dx = 5x + (3/2)x² + c; T(0) = c = 0; T(8) = 40 + 96 + c = 136 + c; T(8) − T(0) = 136 ✓. Worked example 2: an object moves with velocity V(t) = 5 + 3t² m/min; S(t) = ∫V(t)dt = ∫(5 + 3t²)dt = 5t + t³ + c ✓. (Minor slip: the introductory line writes "V(x)" where the function is of t; the mathematics is consistent — see "Confirmed book errors".)

#### Chapter 5 — Bivariate statistics (pp. 145–176)

- **Chapter opening (p. 145):** photograph of cars in a showroom; introduction to studying statistics.
- **5.1 Bivariate statistics (p. 146):** objectives — define bivariate statistics; represent bivariate statistics as points; find the mean point of bivariate statistics; find the equation of the line through the mean point. Example 1: a table of baby masses (kg) and heights (cm) — **see the confirmed error below ("8 babies" against 6 data columns)**. Example 2 (p. 147): 6 students' heights at 6 ages (48, 48.4, 49.5, 48.9, 49.3, 48.7) — 6 points, consistent.
- **5.2 Equation of the linear regression line (p. 158):** company monthly-sales example (xᵢ, yᵢ) = (0,120), (1,170), (2,220), (3,270); the successive slopes are all 50; (y − 120)/x = 50 → y = 50x + 120 ✓.

#### Chapter 6 — Conics (pp. 177–220)

- **Chapter opening (p. 177):** satellite-dish photograph; introduction — curves from 2nd-degree equations in x and y; conics named after Apollonius (260–200 BC); three types: ellipse, parabola, hyperbola.
- **6.1 Parabola (p. 178):** objectives — define a parabola; find its equation; convert a parabola equation to standard form. §1 "the conic curve" (a cone cut by a plane); §2 definition of a parabola (focus F, directrix Δ; a point V on line l₁, F on l₂, FV ⊥ Δ; V is 1 unit from F and 1 unit from Δ — the parabola as the locus of points equidistant from F and Δ).
- **6.2 Ellipse (p. 192):** filed from the table of contents and the teacher guide's lesson (ellipse: e = c/a, foci, vertices — guide printed p. 200) and the answers section. The section start was not read in detail in the student book.
- **6.3 Hyperbola (p. 204):** filed from the table of contents, the teacher guide's lesson (hyperbola: asymptotes y = k ± b/a·(x − h), c² = a² + b² — guide printed p. 210) and the end pages (printed pp. 250–252). The section start was not read in detail in the student book. The hyperbola exercises include an inscribed-rectangle optimisation (area A = 4b/a·x√(a² − x²), maximum at x = a/√2) — verified in the 2013 printing's identical pages.

#### Answers to exercises (printed pp. 221–252)

- Worked answers for the exercises of all six chapters, organised by chapter and lesson: Chapter 1 Lesson 1 from p. 221; Chapter 1 Lesson 2 (continuity) answers follow (verified at printed p. 225); Chapter 2 Lesson 1 from p. 226; Chapter 3 answers in the pp. ~230–240 range (verified at printed p. 240 in the 2013 printing: (3x² − 7x + 15)/(x − 1) = 3x − 4 + 11/(x − 1), vertical asymptote x = 1); Chapter 4 (primitives/integrals) answers follow in the same region; Chapter 6 (conics) answers at pp. 250–252.
- End pages (printed pp. 250–252, read visually): hyperbola exercises; Exercise 10 (ellipse): V(±9/2, 0), M(0, ±3); x = 1.8 → y² = 7.56, y ≈ 2.75 m ✓ (y² = 9(1 − 18²/8100 × 4) = 6804 × 9/8100 = 7.56 ✓). Exercise 11 (tangent to the ellipse x²/100 + y²/25 = 1 at (−8, 3)): y′ = −x/(4y), m = 8/12 = 2/3, tangent 3y = 2x + 25, y-intercept (0, 25/3) ✓. **The graph on this page mislabels the bottom vertex — see "Confirmed book errors".**

### Confirmed book errors (each with the correct value)

1. **Printed p. 7 — wrong fraction in a limit worked example (read visually).** The book writes "(x − 3/2)² > 3969/4" where it should be **369/4** for the stated target f(x) > 100 (f(x) = x² − 3x + 10 = (x − 3/2)² + 31/4, so f(x) > 100 ⟺ (x − 3/2)² > 100 − 31/4 = 369/4). The printed 3969/4 (= 63²/4) belongs to f(x) > 1000 (1000 − 31/4 = 3969/4). Consequently the book's thresholds x > 33 / x < −30 should be **x > 3/2 + √369/2 ≈ 11.1 / x < 3/2 − √369/2 ≈ −8.1**. The limit conclusion (lim f(x) = +∞ both ways) is unaffected. **This error is shared with the 2013 printing** (`g12-2000.pdf`, same page).
2. **Printed p. 146 (Section 5.1, Example 1) — "8 babies" against 6 data columns (read visually at 200 DPI and confirmed in the text layer).** The example says the data are for **8 babies** ("ក្នុងចំណោមទារក 8 នាក់"), but the table gives only **6** (masses 2.8, 2.9, 3.8, 3.6, 3, 3.7 kg; heights 38, 38, 42, 39, 40, 43 cm) and the worked points list 6 babies. **Correct: 6 babies** (or 2 data columns are missing — the table as printed has 6). **Shared with the 2013 printing.**
3. **Printed p. 252 (end page, Exercise 11 graph) — mislabelled vertex (read visually).** The bottom vertex of the ellipse x²/100 + y²/25 = 1 is labelled **"(−5, 0)"** but the point shown is **(0, −5)** — the coordinates are transposed. **Shared with the 2013 printing** (the same label appears in `g12-2000.pdf`, printed p. 252).
4. **Printed p. 132 (Section 4.2, Example 2) — minor notation slip.** The introductory line writes the velocity as "V(x)" where the function is of t (V(t) = 5 + 3t²); the integral line correctly uses V(t) and S(t) = 5t + t³ + c. The mathematics is consistent; only the variable letter is wrong in the intro line.

### NOT errors — checked and cleared (do not file as errors)

- **Printed p. 7 limit examples:** lim_{x→−1}(3x³ − 4x + 8) = 9 ✓ (it is 3x³, not 3x² — an earlier text-layer suspicion resolved by the visual read); lim_{x→−4}5x³ = −320 ✓; lim_{x→1}(x² − 1)/(x² − 3x + 2) = −2 ✓; lim_{x→+∞}(2x + 1)/(x − 1) = 2 ✓; lim_{x→+∞}(2x − x²)/(3x + 5) = −∞ ✓.
- **Section 5.2 regression example:** (y − 120)/x = 50 → y = 50x + 120 ✓.
- **End pages (pp. 250–252):** ellipse Exercise 10 (y² = 7.56, y ≈ 2.75) ✓ and Exercise 11 (tangent 3y = 2x + 25) ✓ — apart from the "(−5, 0)" graph label above.

### Unresolved — carried, not settled

- **Answers section, printed p. 226, Exercise 7ខ:** the printed derivative line "2yy′ = 8cos³2x − 8sin³2x" does not match the derivative of the function as it appears to be stated (y² = sin2x + cos⁴2x, or y² = sin⁴2x + cos⁴2x — the question text on the exercise page was not re-read). The neighbouring answers on the same page are verified correct (ex. 6: y = 3x² − sin2x → y′ = 6x − 2cosx, y″ = 6 + 4sin2x ✓; g(x) = x²/(x+1) → g′ = (x² + 2x)/(x+1)², g″ = 2/(x+1)³ ✓; ex. 8: 2x² + y² = 4 → y′ = −2x/y, y″ = (−4x² − 2y²)/y³ ✓). Do not cite ex. 7ខ as fact until the question text is checked. (The same line appears identically in the 2013 printing.)

### Honest negatives

Topics a Grade 12 book might be expected to teach that this Basic book does **NOT**:

- **No complex numbers** (separate topic booklet — another band's file).
- **No matrices or determinants.**
- **No differential equations** (Advanced-only, Chapter 5).
- **No probability** — the book teaches bivariate statistics and linear regression (Chapter 5), but no probability as such (probability distributions are Advanced-only, Chapter 6).
- **No vectors in space** (Advanced-only, Chapter 7).
- **No number theory / divisibility** (Advanced-only, Chapter 8).
- **No parametric equations or polar coordinates** (Advanced-only, Chapter 9).

### Held-copy defects

- **None.** No missing, duplicated or unreadable pages found. All 257 PDF pages present; content runs printed pp. 1–252 plus the answers section.

### Printed-page offset

**Printed = PDF − 5** throughout, verified at several points: Chapter 1 printed p. 1 = PDF p. 6; Chapter 3 printed p. 63 = PDF p. 68; Chapter 5 printed p. 145 = PDF p. 150; Chapter 6 printed p. 177 = PDF p. 182; answers section printed p. 221 = PDF p. 226; last page printed p. 252 = PDF p. 257. Front matter: cover PDF p. 1, title PDF p. 2, imprint PDF p. 3, preface PDF p. 4, table of contents PDF p. 5 (all unnumbered).

## 3. `g12-advanced-teacher.pdf` — Grade 12 Advanced teacher guide

**File:** `g12-advanced-teacher.pdf` — 240 PDF pages, 16,040,510 bytes, sha256 `44931bd6d212`
**Band:** C2a (Cambodia Grade 12 mathematics, 21st maths read)
**Language:** Khmer. Filed in English.

### How this book was read

- **Text layer:** none. The file is a pure black-and-white photocopy-style scan; `pdftotext` returns empty pages.
- **Visual read:** title page (PDF p. 1), imprint (PDF p. 2), preface (PDF p. 3), table of contents (PDF p. 4), Chapter 1 lesson plan (PDF pp. 5–10), Chapter 7 lesson (PDF p. 200), Chapter 8 lesson and exercise answers (PDF pp. 210, 223–226), Chapter 9 lesson plan (PDF pp. 231–235) and the last page (PDF p. 240), rendered with `pdftoppm` (110–200 DPI) and read visually.
- **Printed-page numbers** below are the guide's own printed numbers, read off the page images. "Guide printed p." means the guide's own pagination; "student p." means the student book's pagination. Never invented.

### Identity (title page + imprint, PDF pp. 1–2, visual)

- Title: "សៀវភៅគ្រូ" (Teacher's book) + "គណិតវិទ្យា" (Mathematics) + "កម្រិតខ្ពស់" (Advanced level) + "ថ្នាក់ទី ១២" (Grade 12). Same publisher/address as the student book (Publishing and Distribution House, Building 148, Preah Norodom Boulevard, Phnom Penh).
- Author committee (slightly different from the student book): Lok U Siangly; Lok Srey Di Bunlivet; Lok Lu Chinta; Lok Sin Sichun; Lok Nik Lina; Lok Bun Rith; Lok Chan Vada; Lok Srey Uk Somaly; typist Lok Khem Mari; illustrator Lok Hak Randa; adviser Lok In Kimsean; proofreader Lok Khem Mari; review committee Lok Suon Di, Lok Men Kam.
- Approval: MoEYS Prakas No. **1646**, **5 May 2011**.
- **"បោះពុម្ពឆ្នាំ២០១២ ចំនួន ១ ៦០០ក្បាល" — printed 2012, 1,600 copies.**
- **No ISBN visible on the imprint page.**
- A smudged/whited-out area at the top-right of the imprint page (a stamp or name blacked out). The top-right of the table-of-contents page is smudged the same way.

### Structure

- **Preface (PDF p. 3, "អារម្ភកថា"):** lists what the teacher's book contains — lesson objectives, time allocation, teaching suggestions, answers to exercises, etc. It is a preface, **not** a table of contents.
- **Table of contents (PDF p. 4):** reproduces the student book's table of contents almost exactly — same 9 chapters, same sections, same printed page numbers — **except**: 8.3 listed at p. 216 (held student book: p. 218), Chapter 9 at p. 227 (held: p. 235), 9.1 at p. 228 (held: p. 236). All earlier chapters' page numbers match the held student book (1, 2, 19, 20, 41, 42, 54, 63, 64, 76, 91, 92, 112, 133, 134, 150, 160, 173, 174, 186, 201, 202, 210).
- **Per chapter:** an introduction/rationale, a time table, then per lesson — objectives, time, materials, lesson content (worked examples with solutions), exercises with answers. It **embeds reduced student-book pages** (e.g. Chapter 1 student pp. 1–5 appear on guide pp. 5–7).
- **Printed-page offset: printed = PDF − 4** — verified at PDF 5 → 1, PDF 200 → 196, PDF 210 → 206, PDF 230 → 226, PDF 240 → 236. Last page: printed p. 236 = Chapter 9 (parametric/polar) exercise answers.

### Lesson-by-lesson filing (samples read)

#### Chapter 1 — Limits of sequences (guide printed pp. 1–18)

- Time table: **Chapter 1: 15 hours**; Lesson 1 (limits of sequences) 15 hours.
- Lesson content: limits of sequences including geometric sequences (r > 1 → +∞, r = 1 → 1, r = 0 → 0, 0 < |r| < 1 → 0, r ≤ −1 oscillating).
- Exercise answers verified correct: none of the six sequence limits formerly filed here were located as printed answers in the guide (Chapter 1, guide pp. 5–18 checked; two of the expressions appear as exercises on guide p. 5 with no printed answer there). The values are correct arithmetic, but **do not cite them as printed guide answers**. [Checker correction 2026-11-05: the sentence formerly here — "lim (n+1)/n = 1; lim n² − 3n³ = −∞; lim (n² − 1)/(2n) = +∞; lim 4n²/(2n² + 1) = 2; lim (2n + 1)/(3n√3) = 2√3/9; lim (1 + 1/n + 1/n²)/(2 + 1/n²) = 1/2 ✓" — was removed as NOT FOUND.]
- [Checker correction 2026-11-05: the p. 10 r-case error formerly filed here — "'r = 1' written twice (the second should be r = 0) and 'r < |r| < 1' garbled" — was **NOT FOUND**. Guide p. 10 (PDF p. 14) re-read at 300 DPI shows the r-case table correct (r = 1 once, r = 0 present, 0 < |r| < 1, |r| ≥ 1 divergent); neighbouring pp. 9 and 11 are likewise correct. Removed from the error list.]

#### Chapter 7 — Vectors in space (guide printed p. 196)

- Lesson content: vectors in space, distance/plane/sphere formulas. Worked example: distance from Q(1,2,3) to a line L, D = |PQ × û| / |û| = 5/√6 = 5√6/6 ✓.

#### Chapter 8 — Divisibility and Euclidean division (guide printed pp. 206, 223–226)

- Lesson (guide printed p. 206): Euclidean division a = bq + r, 0 ≤ r < b; examples 194 = 7 × 27 + 5 ✓; −317 = 21 × (−16) + 19 as an equation ✓ — **but the guide then concludes q = −15 (wrong; see error 1 below)**, and the exercise box asks a = −1317 while the solution solves −317 (see error 2). The guide's 3^57 decomposition is also wrong (see error 3). Exercise 6 answers: −27 = 5 × (−6) + 3; −18 = 5 × (−4) + 2; −12 = 5 × (−3) + 3; 21 = 5 × 4 + 1; 13 = 5 × 2 + 3; 37 = 5 × 7 + 2 ✓. 3^5 = 243 ≡ 1 (mod 11), so 3^57 ≡ 9 (mod 11) ✓ — the final remainder is right even though the guide's exponent decomposition is wrong (see error 3).
- **Exercise answers (guide printed pp. 223–226): contain multiple wrong answers — see "Confirmed guide errors".**

#### Chapter 9 — Parametric equations and polar coordinates (guide printed pp. 227–236)

- Lesson plan starts at guide printed p. 227 and embeds the student book's Chapter 9 title page (satellite-dish photograph). Objectives: define parametric equations of a curve; convert parametric equations to Cartesian equations. **Time: 3 hours** for Lesson 1.
- Lesson content: f and g defined on an interval I; curve C with points (f(t), g(t)); parametric equations x = f(t), y = g(t); circle x = h + a·sin θ, y = k + b·cos θ, 0 ≤ θ ≤ 2π; ellipse (x − h)²/a² + (y − k)²/b² = 1.
- Worked example (projectile at 45°): the guide writes **v₀ = 45 cm/s** — the held student book writes **48 cm/s**, and the equation the guide itself gives, y = −x²/72 + x, requires 48 (with the book's g = 32 cm/s²: y = x − 32x²/(2·48²·½) = x − x²/72; with 45 it would be x − x²/126.56). **Guide slip: v₀ should be 48 cm/s.**
- Polar coordinates (guide printed p. 231): (r, θ) = (r, θ + 2nπ) and (r, θ) = (−r, θ + (2n + 1)π); x = r·cos θ, y = r·sin θ; r² = x² + y², tan θ = y/x (x ≠ 0) ✓. Section 2.1 (polar coordinates of a point): pole O, polar axis, point P(r, θ); examples (2, π/3), (3, −π/6), (3, 11π/6) ✓.
- The lesson plan cites student pages **241 and 243** for the polar-coordinates material. Those citations **do match the held student book's pagination** (Chapter 9 runs printed pp. 235–252; polar coordinates sit around pp. 241–247) — unlike the guide's table-of-contents numbers for Chapter 9 (227/228), which are stale. The guide's page citations are therefore mixed: stale at the chapter/section level from 8.3 onward, but matching inside the Chapter 9 lesson itself.

### Confirmed guide errors (each with the correct value)

**20 errors** on guide printed pp. 206, 223–224, 226, 227, 236, verified by hand and by computation. **Teach the corrected values, never the guide's as fact.** (Checker 2026-11-05: the list formerly had 14 errors; the p. 10 error formerly listed first was NOT FOUND and removed, and 7 further errors were found on pp. 206 and 236. The list below is renumbered 1–20 in page order.)

1. **p. 206 (Chapter 8 lesson, worked example):** the guide writes "−317 = 21 × (−16) + 19, so q = −15 and r = 19" — the equation is right but the quotient is wrong: **q = −16** (21 × (−16) + 19 = −317; 21 × (−15) + 19 = −296 ≠ −317). The wrong q = −15 appears twice on the page (lesson content and exercise solution).
2. **p. 206 (Chapter 8 lesson, exercise box vs solution):** the exercise asks for **a = −1317**, b = 21, but the worked solution solves **a = −317**; the lesson content above it states a = 317 (positive). Statement/solution mismatch — for a = −1317 the answer would be q = −63, r = 6 (−1317 = 21 × (−63) + 6).
3. **p. 206 (Chapter 8 lesson, 3^57 mod 11):** the guide writes "3^57 = 3^(7×5+2) = (3^5)^7 × 3²" — the decomposition is wrong: 7 × 5 + 2 = 37, not 57. Correct: **3^57 = 3^(5×11+2) = (3^5)^11 × 3²**. The final remainder 9 is still right (3^5 = 243 ≡ 1 (mod 11), so (3^5)^k ≡ 1 for any k, and 3² ≡ 9).
4. **p. 223, ex. 1:** D(375) ∩ D(2070) = **{1, 3, 5, 15}**; the guide says {1, 5, 15} — **missing 3** (2070 = 2 × 3² × 5 × 23, so 3 divides it).
5. **p. 223, ex. 2:** D(1625) ∩ D(825) = **{1, 5, 25}**; the guide says {1, 5, 25, 75} — **75 does not divide 1625** (1625 = 5³ × 13).
6. **p. 223, ex. 3:** D(3570) ∩ D(5455) = **{1, 5}**; the guide says {5} — **missing 1** (GCD(3570, 5455) = 5, whose divisors are 1 and 5).
7. **p. 223, ex. 4គ:** LCM = 2⁴ × 3³ × 5 × 23 × 11² = **6,011,280**; the guide says **20,386,080** (that number is 2⁵ × 3⁴ × 5 × 11² × 13 — a different number entirely).
8. **p. 223, ex. 7:** pairs with GCD 15 and sum 150: only **(15,135) and (45,105)** (plus swaps) are valid; the guide also lists (30,120), (60,90), (75,75), whose GCDs are 30, 30 and 75 — the coprimality filter is missing.
9. **p. 223, ex. 8:** GCD(n, 252) = 7, n < 100: only **n ∈ {7, 35, 49, 77, 91}**; the guide lists all 13 multiples of 7 (7, 14, 21, …, 91) — most have GCD 14, 21, 28, 35, 42, 49, 56, 63, 70, 84 or 126 with 252, not 7.
10. **p. 223, ex. 13:** 5 × 35 × 21 = **3,675**; the guide says 3,885. (Part b, 11 × 21 = 231, is correct.)
11. **p. 224, ex. 1:** mn (m ≡ 11, n ≡ 25 mod 43) leaves remainder **17** (mod 43) — 275 = 43 × 6 + 17; the guide says 23. Also n⁴ leaves remainder **13** (mod 43); the guide's line is garbled (it uses 25³ = 15,625, remainder 16 — that is 25³, not 25⁴). (m³ remainder 41 is correct.)
12. **p. 224, ex. 2ខ:** (2x − 3)(3y + 1) = −2 **has** an integer solution, (x, y) = (2, −1); the guide tested only two of the four factor pairs and concluded there is no solution.
13. **p. 226, ex. 12គ:** "x = 5k = 6" is a misprint for **"x = 5k + 6"** (the pair (5k + 6, 11k + 13) is correct).
14. **p. 226, ex. 14:** pairs with a + b = 360, GCD(a, b) = 18: valid are **(18,342), (54,306), (126,234), (162,198)** (plus swaps); the guide lists (36,324), (72,288), (90,270), (108,252), (144,216), (180,180), whose GCDs are 36, 72, 90, 36, 72, 180 — the coprimality filter is missing. Also the table's right-column (α, β) labels do not match its values (α = 7, β = 13 is shown with the values 108, 252, which belong to α = 6, β = 14).
15. **p. 227 (Chapter 9 lesson, projectile example):** v₀ written as **45 cm/s**; should be **48 cm/s** (see Chapter 9 above).
16. **p. 236 (last page), ex. 4 (hyperbola):** the guide writes "25 = 16 − b²" (which would give b² = −9); it should be c² = a² + b² → **25 = 16 + b² → b² = 9, b = 3**. (The final b = 3 is correct.)
17. **p. 236, ex. 7ក (polar form of a conic):** the guide writes (cosθ − sinθ)² = **1 − 2sin2θ**; it should be **1 − sin2θ** (so r² = 9/(1 − sin2θ)).
18. **p. 236, ex. 7គ (polar form of an ellipse):** the guide uses the wrong identities cos²θ = (1 + 2cos2θ)/2 and sin²θ = (1 − 2cos2θ)/2 (correct: (1 ± cos2θ)/2) and prints **r² = 72/(13 − 10cos2θ)**; correct is **r² = 72/(13 − 5cos2θ)** (4cos²θ + 9sin²θ = (13 − 5cos2θ)/2).
19. **p. 236, ex. 8ក (polar → Cartesian):** r·cosθ = 5 → the guide writes the Cartesian equation **x² = 5**; it should be **x = 5**.
20. **p. 236, ex. 8ឃ (polar → Cartesian):** r = 8sinθ − 2cosθ → the guide writes r = 8·y/r **− 3·x/r** and x² + y² = 8y − 3x; it should be **2·x/r** and **x² + y² = 8y − 2x**.

Verified correct on guide pp. 223–226 (so the answer key is not wrong throughout): GCD(1800, 1296) = 72 by Euclid ✓; 3570 = 2 × 3 × 5 × 7 × 17, 5455 = 5 × 1091 ✓; 1331 = 11³, 4913 = 17³ ✓; ex. 4 (a), (b) GCD/LCM ✓ (630; 360, 360); ex. 5 GCD(2600, 1925) = 25, LCM = 200,200 ✓; ex. 16 a + b = 15, LCM = 56 → {7, 8} ✓; ex. 17 GCD = 6, LCM = 36 → (6,36), (12,18) ✓; ex. 18 GCD = 21, LCM = 126 → (21,126), (42,63) ✓; p. 224 ex. 3 (sum of powers of 13 divisible by 170) ✓; ex. 4 (induction: 4ⁿ + 6n − 1 divisible by 9) ✓; ex. 5 ((a+1)ⁿ⁺¹ − a(n+1) − 1 divisible by a²) ✓; ex. 6 (polar-point conversions) ✓ — (√2, π/4) → (1, 1), (0, π/2) → (0, 0), (3, 5π/6) → (−3√3/2, 3/2) ✓; ex. 8ខ (r − 6sinθ = 0 → x² + y² − 6y = 0) ✓ and ex. 8គ (r(sinθ + rcos²θ) = 1 → y = 1 − x²) ✓; ex. 7ឃ/ង/ច appear correct. (Ex. 7ក, 7គ, 8ក and 8ឃ are WRONG — see errors 17–20. The polar formulas r = 2a/(1 − sin θ) and r = 2a·cos θ formerly filed here as verified correct were not re-located to a specific exercise on the page and are dropped from the verified list.); p. 226 ex. 10 f(x) = (2x² + P)/x, f(1) = 2 + P, f(p) = 2p + 1 → P = p ✓; ex. 11 (a + 21)/(b + 15) = a/b → 7b = 5a → (7, 5) ✓; ex. 12 5x + 16y = 1 → x = 13 + 16k ✓, 44x − 40y = 4 → (10k − 9, 11k − 10) ✓; ex. 13 D(171) ∩ D(99) = {1, 9}, GCD = 9 ✓.

### Companion verdict — Advanced guide: TRUE COMPANION (with stale pages and wrong answers)

**Verdict: TRUE COMPANION in structure and content, with two caveats that must be carried.**

Evidence for TRUE COMPANION:

- Same 9 chapters in the same sequence as the student book; same section structure.
- Embeds reduced student-book pages (Chapter 1 student pp. 1–5 on guide pp. 5–7; Chapter 9 title page on guide p. 227).
- Answers the student book's exercises, chapter by chapter, with worked solutions.
- Its Chapter 9 lesson plan's in-text citations to student pp. 241 and 243 match the held student book's pagination.

Caveats:

1. **Page citations are stale from 8.3 onward against the held 2020 student printing.** The guide's table of contents lists 8.3 at p. 216 (held student book: p. 218), Chapter 9 at p. 227 (held: p. 235) and 9.1 at p. 228 (held: p. 236). The guide's embedded student page for the 8.3 lesson is itself numbered 216. So the guide was keyed to an **earlier student-book printing** (its own printing is 2012; the held student book is the 12th printing, 2020). Earlier chapters' page numbers all match the held book. Use the guide's chapter/section sequence as evidence for what the student book teaches; do **not** trust its page numbers from 8.3 onward against the held printing.
2. **Its answer key contains multiple wrong answers, concentrated in Chapter 8 (number theory)** — 20 errors listed above: 11 of them on pp. 223–226, 5 on the Chapter 9 last page (p. 236), 3 in the Chapter 8 lesson (p. 206), 1 on p. 227. The guide may be used as evidence for **what the student book teaches** (sequence, content, lesson structure, objectives) but **its answers must be checked, never trusted blindly** — especially the Chapter 8 exercise answers and the Chapter 9 last page.

### Held-copy defects

- **None in pagination:** all 240 PDF pages present, no missing or duplicated pages found.
- **Smudged/whited-out area** at the top-right of the imprint page (PDF p. 2) and the same smudge at the top-right of the table-of-contents page (PDF p. 4) — a stamp or name blacked out in the source copy. No content is lost.

### Printed-page offset

**Guide printed = PDF − 4**, verified at PDF 5 → 1, PDF 200 → 196, PDF 210 → 206, PDF 230 → 226, PDF 240 → 236. The guide's printed pagination runs 1–236; front matter is PDF pp. 1–4 (title, imprint, preface, table of contents).

- Tested against: `g12-advanced.pdf` (257 pp., sha `61b8a08d4d78`) — see `g12-advanced.md`.

### Identity (title page + imprint, PDF pp. 1–2, visual)

- Title: "សៀវភៅគ្រូ" (Teacher's book) + "គណិតវិទ្យា" (Mathematics) + "កម្រិតមូលដ្ឋាន" (Basic / Foundation level) + "ថ្នាក់ទី ១២" (Grade 12). Same publisher/address as the student book (Publishing and Distribution House, Building 148, Preah Norodom Boulevard, Phnom Penh).
- Authors: Lok U Siangly, Lok Srey Uk Somaly, Lok Nik Lina, Lok Bun Rith, Lok Lu Chinta, Lok Bu Son; typist Lok Srey Hak Dany; illustrator Lok Hak Randa; adviser Lok Chu Supheng; proofreader Lok Hak Randa; review committee Lok Chea Heang, Lok Nang Bu.
- Approval: MoEYS Prakas No. **1645**, 5 May 2011.
- **"បោះពុម្ពឆ្នាំ២០១២ ចំនួន ១៤៥០" — printed 2012, 1,450 copies.**
- **No ISBN visible on the imprint page.**
- A smudged/whited-out area at the top-right of the table-of-contents page (PDF p. 4), like the Advanced guide's imprint page.

### Structure

- **Preface (PDF p. 3):** mirrors the Basic student preface — 6 chapters: limits of functions, derivatives, function study/graphing, integrals, statistics, conics.
- **Table of contents (PDF p. 4):** matches the student Basic table of contents **exactly** — same 6 chapters, same sections, same printed page numbers (1, 2, 28, 43, 44, 56, 63, 64, 86, 94, 109, 110, 132, 145, 146, 158, 177, 178, 192, 204) — **plus** one extra final entry: "ចម្លើយលំហាត់" (Answers to exercises), p. 221.
- **Per chapter:** an introduction, a time table, then per lesson — objectives, time, materials, lesson content, exercises with answers. It **embeds reduced student-book pages** (Chapter 1 student pp. 1–3 appear on guide pp. 5–7).
- **The guide's printed page numbers mirror the student book's pagination:** each guide page discusses the same-numbered student page (e.g. guide printed p. 200 discusses student p. 200's ellipse material; guide printed p. 210 discusses student p. 210's hyperbola material).
- **Answers section (printed pp. 221–236):** a condensed answers section covering the exercises not already answered inside the lesson plans. Printed p. 221 opens with Chapter 3, Lesson 2 (exponential functions) answers — limits and derivatives of exponential functions (lim_{x→+∞} e^{3x} = +∞, (1 + n/x)^x → e^x, ((x + 3)/(x − 1))^{x+2} → e⁴, f(x) = 1/(2 − e^{−x}) with vertical asymptote x = −ln 2, domain D = ℝ ∖ {−ln 2}). The last page (printed p. 236) is Chapter 6 (conics) exercise answers — tangent-line derivation; ellipse with 2a = 10, a = 5, 2b = 6, b = 3, x²/25 + y²/9 = 1 ✓.

### Lesson-by-lesson filing (samples read)

#### Chapter 1 — Limits (guide printed pp. 1–42)

- Time table: **Chapter 1: 31 hours**; Lesson 1 (limits of functions) **19 hours**, Lesson 2 (continuity) **12 hours**.
- **Lesson 1 (limits of functions), guide printed pp. 1–20:** the ε–δ definition of lim f(x) = L; infinite limits. Worked example f(x) = (4x² + 5x − 1)/(x + 3) — values f(−1) = −1, f(−2) = 5, f(0) = −1/3, f(1) = 2, f(2) = 5, f(3) = 25/3 ✓ (the guide's own lesson-content example — **not** in the student book; see the checker note in `g12-basic.md`). Exercises 6–8 with solutions — **see the two slips in "Confirmed guide errors"**.
- **Lesson 2 (continuity): the opening of this lesson plan is MISSING from the held copy — see "Held-copy defects".** The lesson's later pages (printed pp. 29–38) survive: continuity exercises 6, 7, 8 with worked solutions (embedded student p. 33).
- **HELD-COPY DEFECT (printed pp. 21–28 missing, pp. 33–36 duplicated) — full evidence under "Held-copy defects".**

#### Chapter 4 — Integrals (guide printed pp. 109–144)

- Answers (guide printed p. 226, read visually) — all verified correct: ∫₀ᵇ eˣ/(eˣ + e^{b−x}) dx = b/2 ✓; ∫sin²x dx = x/2 − (1/4)sin 2x ✓; ∫sin⁴x·sin 2x dx = sin⁶x/3 ✓; ∫ln x / x dx = (ln x)²/2 ✓; ∫eˣ/(1 + eˣ) dx = ln(1 + eˣ) ✓; ∫x·e^{2x} dx = x·e^{2x}/2 − e^{2x}/4 ✓; ∫e^{−x}·sin x dx = −e^{−x}(sin x + cos x)/2 ✓; ∫₁² x·ln x dx = 2 ln 2 − 3/4 ✓; ∫₀¹ ln(x + 1) dx = −1 + 2 ln 2 ✓; ∫₀^π x·sin 2x dx = −π/2 ✓; ∫₀^{π/2} x²·sin 2x dx = π²/8 − 1/2 ✓; ∫₀^{π/2} x·cos x dx = π/2 − 1 ✓; ∫₀¹ x²·eˣ dx = e − 2 ✓.

#### Chapter 6 — Conics (guide printed pp. 177–220)

- **6.2 Ellipse (guide printed p. 200):** e = c/a, foci, vertices.
- **6.3 Hyperbola (guide printed p. 210):** asymptotes y = k ± b/a·(x − h), c² = a² + b².
- **Answers (guide printed p. 220):** Exercise 8 — rectangle inscribed in an ellipse, maximum at x = a/√2, width √2·a, height √2·b ✓. Exercises 10–11 (same as the student book's end pages) ✓ — including the student book's "(−5, 0)" graph label, which the guide reproduces without correction (see `g12-basic.md`).

### Confirmed guide errors (each with the correct value)

Two minor slips, both in the Chapter 1, Lesson 1 exercise solutions. Teach the corrected values, never the guide's as fact.

1. **Chapter 1, Exercise 6 (ε–δ proof for lim_{x→2}√x = √2):** the guide's proof uses **δ = 2ε** with a sloppy step (it writes ε(√x + 2) for ε(√x + √2)). δ = 2ε is valid only for small ε; a correct δ would be e.g. **min(1, ε√2)**. The limit itself is right; the proof step is sloppy.
2. **Chapter 1, Exercise 8:** |4x²| < 0.01 → the guide writes **"2|x| < 0.01"** (wrong algebra; it should be **2|x| < 0.1**), but the final answer **|x| < 0.05** is correct. (Exercise 8.2, lim_{x→3}(4x − 1) = 11 with δ = ε/4, is correct.)

### Companion verdict — Basic guide: TRUE COMPANION (with a held-copy defect and minor slips)

**Verdict: TRUE COMPANION, with one held-copy defect that must be carried.**

Evidence for TRUE COMPANION:

- Table of contents matches the student Basic book **exactly** — same 6 chapters, same sections, same printed page numbers — plus the answers section at p. 221.
- Same unit sequence as the student book; embeds reduced student-book pages (Chapter 1 student pp. 1–3 on guide pp. 5–7).
- Its printed pagination mirrors the student book's pagination page for page.
- Answers the student book's exercises, both inside the lesson plans and in its answers section (printed pp. 221–236).
- The Chapter 4 integral answers sampled (13 integrals, guide printed p. 226) are all correct; the Chapter 6 answers (pp. 220, 236) are correct.

Caveats:

1. **Held-copy defect (see below): printed pp. 21–28 are missing and pp. 33–36 are duplicated.** The missing pages are the opening of the Lesson 2 (continuity) lesson plan — its objectives, time, materials and lesson content are absent from this copy. The rest of the guide is intact.
2. **Two minor answer slips** in the Chapter 1 exercise solutions (above). Nothing like the Advanced guide's Chapter 8 error cluster.

**The guide may be used as evidence for what its student book teaches** (sequence, content, lesson structure, objectives, time allocation), with the missing-pages defect noted and the two slips corrected.

### Held-copy defects

**Printed pp. 21–28 (8 pages) are MISSING from this PDF; printed pp. 33–36 appear TWICE.**

Evidence (bottom-of-page numbers read visually):

- PDF p. 5 → printed 1; PDF p. 6 → 2; PDF p. 10 → 6; PDF p. 20 → 16; PDF p. 21 → 17; PDF p. 22 → 18; PDF p. 23 → 19; PDF p. 24 → 20; then **PDF pp. 25–28 → printed 33–36**; PDF p. 29 → 29; PDF p. 30 → 30; PDF pp. 31–38 → 31–38; PDF p. 40 → 40; PDF p. 50 → 50; PDF p. 100 → 100; PDF p. 150 → 150; PDF p. 200 → 200; PDF p. 210 → 210; PDF p. 220 → 220; PDF p. 226 → 226; PDF p. 236 → 236.
- PDF p. 25 and PDF p. 33 were read in full: **identical content** (continuity exercises 6/7/8, embedded student p. 33).
- The 4 duplicated pages + 8 missing pages net −4, which balances 236 PDF pages against 240 physical pages (4 front-matter pages + 236 numbered pages).

What is lost: the opening of the **Lesson 2 (continuity) lesson plan** — its objectives, time allocation, materials list and lesson content (printed pp. 21–28). The lesson's later pages (exercises from printed p. 29 onward) survive. Nothing else is missing.

### Printed-page offset

The offset **breaks at the defect**:

- **Printed = PDF − 4** for PDF pp. 5–24 (printed pp. 1–20).
- **PDF pp. 25–28 duplicate printed pp. 33–36** (the same pages reappear at PDF pp. 33–36).
- **Printed = PDF** from PDF p. 29 onward (verified at PDF 29 → 29, 30 → 30, 40 → 40, 100 → 100, 150 → 150, 200 → 200, 220 → 220, 226 → 226, 236 → 236).
- Front matter: title PDF p. 1, imprint PDF p. 2, preface PDF p. 3, table of contents PDF p. 4 (all unnumbered).

### Question 2 — settled: this is NOT a "MoEYS 2000 edition"

**The task brief's "MoEYS 2000 edition" label is a record error. No "2000" appears anywhere on the cover, title page, imprint, preface or table of contents as read.**

What the book itself says:

- It is the **Basic-level** Grade 12 mathematics book, **5th printing, 2013**, approved by Prakas 3178 (28 Dec 2011), ISBN 978-995-001-131.
- It is an **earlier printing of the same book** as the current Basic book (`g12-basic.pdf`, 12th printing 2020) — not a different edition, and not from the year 2000.

**Record it as: 5th printing 2013 of the Basic book. It is never to be passed off as the current edition** (current = 12th printing 2020). Per the edition rule, an earlier printing may be used where the current one is unavailable, but the year must be recorded plainly — 2013, not 2000.

### Comparison with the current Basic book (`g12-basic.pdf`, 12th printing 2020)

Checked page by page (visual reads of both files at the same PDF pages):

- **PDF p. 200 = printed p. 195** in both: ellipse summary box (x²/a² + y²/b² = 1, a > b > 0; vertices (±a,0); foci (±c,0), c² = a² − b²; vertical form x²/b² + y²/a² = 1) and worked example 1 (ellipse through (0,2) with vertices (0,−3),(0,3): c = 2, a = 3, b² = 9 − 4 = 5, equation x²/5 + y²/9 = 1) — identical, and correct.
- **PDF p. 230 = printed p. 225** in both: continuity exercise answers (Chapter 1, Lesson 2) — identical.
- **PDF p. 231 = printed p. 226** in both: "ចម្លើយលំហាត់មេរៀនទី 1 ជំពូក 2" (Answers, Lesson 1, Chapter 2 — derivatives) — identical. (One line in the derivative answers, ex. 7ខ, printed "2yy′ = 8cos³2x − 8sin³2x" — see "Unresolved" below; it appears in both printings identically.)
- **PDF pp. 257–259 = printed pp. 250–252** in this file: Chapter 6 (hyperbola) exercises and the final answers page (ellipse ex. 10–11) — identical to the current Basic book's PDF pp. 255–257, including the "(−5, 0)" graph label (see "Confirmed book errors").
- **Content ends at printed p. 252 in both books.**

Conclusion: **the 2013 printing and the 2020 printing carry the same content on the same printed pagination.** The only differences are the imprint (printing number, year, print run), the scan quality (B/W photocopy vs the watermarked colour scan), and the PDF-page count (see "Held-copy defects").

### Confirmed book errors (shared with the 2020 printing)

These three errors are in this printing exactly as in the 2020 printing — same pages, same wrong values. Correct values are given so lessons teach them corrected, never as fact. Full verification is in `g12-basic.md`.

1. **Printed p. 7 — wrong fraction in a limit worked example.** The book writes "(x − 3/2)² > 3969/4" where it should be **369/4** for the stated target f(x) > 100 (f(x) = x² − 3x + 10 = (x − 3/2)² + 31/4, so f(x) > 100 ⟺ (x − 3/2)² > 100 − 31/4 = 369/4). The printed 3969/4 (= 63²/4) belongs to f(x) > 1000. Consequently the book's thresholds x > 33 / x < −30 should be **x > 3/2 + √369/2 ≈ 11.1 / x < 3/2 − √369/2 ≈ −8.1**. The limit conclusion (lim f(x) = +∞ both ways) is unaffected.
2. **Printed p. 146 (Section 5.1, Example 1) — "8 babies" against 6 data columns.** The example says the data are for 8 babies, but the table gives only 6 (masses 2.8, 2.9, 3.8, 3.6, 3, 3.7 kg; heights 38, 38, 42, 39, 40, 43 cm) and the worked points list 6 babies. **Correct: 6 babies** (or 2 data columns are missing — the table as printed has 6).
3. **Printed p. 252 (end page, Exercise 11 graph) — mislabelled vertex.** The bottom vertex of the ellipse x²/100 + y²/25 = 1 is labelled "(−5, 0)" but the point shown is **(0, −5)** — the coordinates are transposed.

### Unresolved — carried, not settled

- **Answers section, printed p. 226, Exercise 7ខ:** the printed derivative line "2yy′ = 8cos³2x − 8sin³2x" does not match the derivative of the function as it appears to be stated (y² = sin2x + cos⁴2x, or y² = sin⁴2x + cos⁴2x — the question text on the exercise page was not re-read). The neighbouring answers on the same page are verified correct (ex. 6: y = 3x² − sin2x → y′ = 6x − 2cosx, y″ = 6 + 4sin2x ✓; g(x) = x²/(x+1) → g′ = (x² + 2x)/(x+1)², g″ = 2/(x+1)³ ✓; ex. 8: 2x² + y² = 4 → y′ = −2x/y, y″ = (−4x² − 2y²)/y³ ✓). Do not cite ex. 7ខ as fact until the question text is checked.

Same as the current Basic book (see `g12-basic.md`): no complex numbers, no matrices/determinants, no differential equations, no probability (statistics only), no vectors in space, no number theory, no parametric equations or polar coordinates. Those topics live in the Advanced book and the topic booklets (another band's files).

No content is missing.

- Otherwise complete: cover through printed p. 252, plus back cover.

## 6. `g12-regular.pdf` — Grade 12 exam-paper collection, school year 2023–2024 (NOT a textbook)

**File:** `g12-regular.pdf` — 69 PDF pages, 341,109 bytes, sha256 `76b8831b853b`
**Band:** C2a (Cambodia Grade 12 mathematics, 21st maths read)
**Language:** Khmer. Filed in English.

### How this book was read

- **Text layer:** partial. `pdftotext -layout` extracts legible Khmer (some mojibake) for the question papers and solutions; maths, numbers and formulas extract legibly. All worked-example checks below were first taken from the text layer.
- **Visual read:** cover (PDF p. 1), table of contents (PDF p. 3), and PDF pp. 11, 19, 20, 27, 28 rendered with `pdftoppm` (130 DPI) and read visually. The visual reads settled two apparent errors that turned out to be misattributions from the garbled text layer (see "NOT errors" below).
- **Printed-page numbers** below are the book's own printed numbers, verified against the table of contents and the page images. Never invented.

### Identity — what this file actually is

**This is NOT a textbook.** It is a school-made collection of Grade 12 mathematics **exam papers with worked solutions** for the school year 2023–2024, science stream.

- Cover (PDF p. 1, visual): the title in a black pill reads "វិញ្ញាសាប្រឡងប្រចាំឆ្នាំសិក្សា ២០២៣-២០២៤" — *Exam papers for school year 2023–2024* — above "ថ្នាក់ទី១២" (Grade 12). The cover lists the papers inside: December ✓, January ✓, February ✓, Semester 1 exam (💣), May ✓, June ✓, Semester 2 exam (💣). No March or April paper.
- **No publisher, no imprint, no ISBN, no edition statement anywhere.** It appears school-made (a teacher's or school's own compilation), not a MoEYS publication.
- The papers are for the **science stream** ("វិទ្យាសាស្ត្រ" — stated on the December paper, printed p. 1).

### Contents (from the table of contents, PDF p. 3, "មាតិកា")

Seven papers, each with a divider page, a question paper (~2 printed pages) and worked solutions ("កំណែ", ~4 printed pages):

| # | Paper | Printed p. | PDF p. (paper start) |
|---|-------|-----------|----------------------|
| 1 | December monthly exam | 1 | 7 |
| 2 | January monthly exam | 9 | 15 |
| 3 | February monthly exam | 17 | 23 |
| 4 | Semester 1 exam | 27 | 33 |
| 5 | May monthly exam | 37 | 43 |
| 6 | June monthly exam | 47 | 53 |
| 7 | Semester 2 exam | 57 | 63 |

Dividers at PDF pp. 5, 13, 21, 31, 41, 51, 61. Solutions run: Dec PDF pp. 9–12; Jan PDF pp. 16–20; Feb PDF pp. 25–30; Sem 1 PDF pp. 35–39; May PDF pp. 45–49; June PDF pp. 55–59; Sem 2 PDF pp. 65–69.

### Paper-by-paper filing (printed pp.)

#### 1. December monthly exam — printed pp. 1–6 (PDF pp. 7–12)

- 150 minutes, 125 points, science stream.
- Part I (20 points): complex numbers. (E): x² + ax + b = 0; (3 + i)z + z̄ = −i; root x₁ = 2 − 3i.
- Solutions (printed pp. 3–6): complex-number algebra; space geometry (triangle areas by cross product — see "NOT errors"); proof that a⃗ × b⃗ = b⃗ × c⃗ = c⃗ × a⃗ when a⃗ + b⃗ + c⃗ = 0⃗ (printed p. 5); point/line/plane work in space.

#### 2. January monthly exam — printed pp. 9–14 (PDF pp. 15–20)

- Part I (15 points): complex numbers — x = −½ − (√3/2)i, y = −½ + (√3/2)i.
- Solutions (printed pp. 10–14): space geometry. Question 2 (15 points): points A(0,−2,0), B(−4,1,2), C(0,3,7), D(4,0,5); proves ABCD is a rectangle (BC⃗ = AD⃗, AB⃗·AD⃗ = 0, |AB⃗| ≠ |AD⃗|). Question 3 (20 points): A(1,−1,4), B(7,−1,−2), C(1,5,−2); cross product (36,36,36), plane x + y + z − 4 = 0, equilateral triangle, area 18√3.

#### 3. February monthly exam — printed pp. 17–24 (PDF pp. 23–30)

- Part I (5 points). Topics in the paper and solutions: counting and probability; space geometry (the same A(1,−1,4), B(7,−1,−2), C(1,5,−2) triangle as January — area 18√3, angle 60°, distance from the origin to the plane 4√3/3, tetrahedron volume 24); conics — parabola x² + 4x − 8y − 12 = 0, i.e. (x + 2)² = 8(y + 2), vertex V(−2,−2), focus F(−2,0), directrix y = −4.

#### 4. Semester 1 exam — printed pp. 27–33 (PDF pp. 33–39)

- Formal header, 150 minutes, 125 points.
- Solutions (printed pp. 29–33): complex numbers — roots of z² + 4z + 16 = 0 are −2 ± 2√3 i (Δ′ = −12); space geometry; function study.

#### 5. May monthly exam — printed pp. 37–43 (PDF pp. 43–49)

- Solutions (printed pp. 39–43): complex numbers — z² − 4z + 16 = 0, roots 2 ± 2√3 i (Δ = −48); conics — ellipse from (2x + y)² + (x − 2y)² = x² − 4y² + 36, i.e. 4x² + 9y² = 36; plane through A(0,1,4) with normal (8,4,8): 2x + y + 2z − 9 = 0.

#### 6. June monthly exam — printed pp. 47–53 (PDF pp. 53–59)

- Question: solve z² − z√2 + 2 = 0 (Δ = −6). Solutions (printed pp. 49–53): complex numbers, limits, integrals (∫ cos x · ln(1 + cos x) dx), function study/graphing.

#### 7. Semester 2 exam — printed pp. 57–63 (PDF pp. 63–69)

- Solutions (printed pp. 59–63): differential equations — y″ + 4y′ + 8y = 0, characteristic equation r² + 4r + 8 = 0, r = −2 ± 2i; function study — f(x) = 2eˣ − 2 − ln(eˣ + 1) = 2eˣ − x − 2 − ln(1 + e⁻ˣ).

### Question 1 — settled: what this file is, and its relation to `g12-2000.pdf`

**Verdict: this file is a school-made exam-paper collection for the science stream, NOT a textbook and NOT an extract of `g12-2000.pdf`.**

Evidence:

1. It has no textbook structure at all — no chapters, no lessons, no theory, no objectives; only seven dated exam papers with worked solutions.
2. Its content **does not match** `g12-2000.pdf` (the Basic student book, 5th printing 2013 — see `g12-2000.md`). The papers test **complex numbers, differential equations, vectors in space and conics**, none of which the Basic book teaches (the Basic book covers limits, derivatives, function study, integrals, bivariate statistics and conics — and conics only as a Basic-only chapter, while the papers' complex numbers and differential equations belong to the Advanced track and the separate topic booklets). The stub's questions span both tracks' content plus the topic booklets.
3. It is far too small for a full-year textbook (63 printed pages of content) and covers only exam revision, not teaching.

**Its content must NOT be treated as the full Grade 12 regular book.** There is no "regular" Grade 12 student textbook in this band — Grade 12 mathematics is taught through the Advanced and Basic track books plus the eight topic booklets (another band's files).

### Confirmed book errors

**None confirmed** in the samples checked. Every worked example spot-checked against the text layer and the visual renders is correct (see "NOT errors" below).

### NOT errors — checked and cleared (do not file as errors)

- **December solutions, printed p. 5 (PDF p. 11):** the page shows both "S = 12" and "S = √61/2" for triangle areas. These belong to **two different questions** (V and VII) and both are correct: the first from ½√(8²(1² + 2² + 2²)) = ½ × 8 × 3 = 12; the second from ½√((−6)² + (−3)² + 4²) = ½√61. The text layer had interleaved the two questions.
- **January solutions, printed p. 14 (PDF pp. 19–20):** "S = 18√3" belongs to the **equilateral triangle A(1,−1,4), B(7,−1,−2), C(1,5,−2)** (Question 3), where AB⃗ × AC⃗ = (36,36,36) and S = ½√(36² + 36² + 36²) = 18√3 — correct. It does **not** belong to the A(0,−2,0), B(−4,1,2), C(0,3,7) triangle (Question 2), whose area is √1305/2 ≈ 18.06. The text layer had interleaved the two questions.

Other spot-checks passed (all from the text layer unless noted, with the page read visually where stated):

- Dec: A(1,1,2), B(2,3,5), C(1,5,5): AB⃗ × AC⃗ = (−6,−3,4) ≠ 0⃗, so A, B, C are not collinear ✓. A(1,0,1), AB⃗ = (−1,2,1) → B(0,2,2) ✓; AB⃗·AC⃗ = 0 ✓.
- Jan Q2: AB⃗ = (−4,3,2), BC⃗ = (4,2,5), CD⃗ = (4,−3,−2), AD⃗ = (4,2,5); |AB⃗| = √29, |AD⃗| = √45 ✓.
- Jan Q3: AB = AC = BC = 6√2 ✓; AB⃗·AC⃗ = 36; cos θ = 36/(6√2 · 6√2) = 1/2 → θ = 60° ✓ (PDF pp. 27–28, visual).
- Feb III: two-digit numbers from {1,6,8} with repetition: 3² = 9 ✓; n(S) = C(9,3) = 84; P(A) = 1/84 ✓; P(B) = C(4,1)·C(5,2)/84 = 40/84 = 10/21 ✓ (PDF p. 27, visual).
- Feb V: (x + 2)² = 8(y + 2), p = 2, V(−2,−2), F(−2,0), directrix y = −4 ✓ (PDF p. 28, visual).
- Sem 1: z² + 4z + 16 = 0 → −2 ± 2√3 i ✓. May: z² − 4z + 16 = 0 → 2 ± 2√3 i ✓. June: z² − z√2 + 2 = 0, Δ = −6 ✓. Sem 2: r² + 4r + 8 = 0 → r = −2 ± 2i ✓; f(x) = 2eˣ − 2 − ln(eˣ + 1) rewritten as 2eˣ − x − 2 − ln(1 + e⁻ˣ) ✓.

### Honest negatives

- This is an exam collection, not a teaching book: it teaches **nothing** systematically — no definitions, no theory, no worked teaching examples, no graded exercises for learning. It can only be used as revision/assessment material.
- It must not be cited as evidence for what the Grade 12 course teaches — it **tests** material from the Advanced book, the Basic book and the topic booklets, but it does not teach any of it.

### Held-copy defects

- **18 blank interleaved PDF pages** (verified by ink count — 0 dark pixels each): PDF pp. 2, 4, 6, 8, 14, 22, 24, 32, 34, 40, 42, 44, 50, 52, 54, 60, 62, 64. These are blank scan pages between the content pages (the book was scanned double-sided with blank versos). No content is lost — the printed pagination runs continuously 1–63.
- Otherwise complete: cover, table of contents and all seven papers with solutions are present.

### Printed-page offset

**Printed = PDF − 6** for all content pages, verified at every paper start (Dec printed 1 = PDF 7; Jan 9 = 15; Feb 17 = 23; Sem 1 27 = 33; May 37 = 43; June 47 = 53; Sem 2 57 = 63). Content ends at printed p. 63 (PDF p. 69). The cover (PDF p. 1) and table of contents (PDF p. 3) are unnumbered.

### Sources for this filing

- `g12-regular.pdf` (69 pp., sha `76b8831b853b`), text layer + visual reads of PDF pp. 1, 3, 11, 19, 20, 27, 28.
- Comparison: `g12-2000.pdf` and `g12-basic.pdf` (see `g12-2000.md`, `g12-basic.md`).


# BAND C2b — Grade 12 topic booklets + baccalaureate papers (11 files, 2,757 PDF pages)

Eight private Khem Putthi exam-preparation booklets (first printing 2026, trial version © 2026, Angkor Bookstore) — the Grade 12 teaching material for the topics the MoEYS student books do not cover — plus three baccalaureate exam-paper files recorded for identity only. Two citation checks: **C2b-1 — 83 anchors (78 passed, 5 corrected, 0 not found)** on Complex Numbers, Derivatives, Differential Equations and Limits (check-band-c2b1.md); **C2b-2 — 86 anchors (79 passed, 7 corrected, 0 not found)** on Primitives, Probability, Space Geometry and Variation and Graphs (check-band-c2b2.md). The baccalaureate files were NOT anchor-checked (identity only, not read unit-by-unit). All eight booklets read visually from renders; printed page = PDF page throughout.


## 1. `g12-complex-numbers.pdf` — Grade 12 Complex Numbers booklet (Khem Putthi)

**File:** `g12-complex-numbers.pdf` — 183 PDF pages, 2,724,935 bytes,
SHA-256 `97b9303cb5e1…` (full hash in `grab-results.json`).
**Read:** text-layer read (Khmer extracts legibly; maths formulae fragment
across lines) plus visual read of the cover, p. 2 imprint, and pp. 16–17,
60–61, 130 (Limits check), 152, 155 to verify suspected errors.
Formula-heavy pages were checked visually where the text layer garbled
them. Page numbers below are PRINTED page numbers; the printed number
equals the PDF page number throughout (verified: TOC footer "7" on PDF
p. 7; Lesson 1 opens on printed p. 9 = PDF p. 9; footer "130" on PDF
p. 130 in the Limits booklet of the same series).

### Identity — NOT a MoEYS publication (correction to the brief)

- **Title as printed (Khmer):** ចំនួនកុំផ្លិច — transliteration:
  *Chamnuan Komplech* — **COMPLEX NUMBERS**, Grade 12
  (cover header: គណិតវិទ្យាថ្នាក់ទី១២, "Grade 12 Mathematics"; series
  code **KP012** printed top-right of every cover in the series).
- **Author:** compiled by លោកគ្រូ ខេម ពុទ្ធី (Teacher Khem Putthi).
  Editorial/review committee (p. 2): Khem Putthi and
  លោកគ្រូ ឡាយ ចាន់ហេន (Lay Chanheng) — Chanheng checks content;
  Putthi does content arrangement, cover/page design, figures/tables
  and proofreading.
- **Edition/year as printed:** cover foot: បោះពុម្ពលើកទី១ ឆ្នាំ ២០២៦
  ("First printing, year 2026"). Imprint p. 2: កំណែសាកល្បង © ២០២៦
  ("Trial version © 2026").
- **ISBN:** none printed.
- **Publisher:** no ministry imprint anywhere in the book (the word
  ក្រសួង / "ministry" occurs zero times in the whole text layer).
  P. 2 states the book was prepared for Grade 12 students for
  self-study and preparation for the Baccalaureate
  (សញ្ញាបត្រមធ្យមសិក្សាទុតិយភូមិ, បាក់ឌុប) and the outstanding-student
  exams, and names បណ្ណាគារអង្គរ (Angkor Bookstore) as the
  printing/distribution channel ("សម្រាប់បោះពុម្ពផ្សាយ៖ បណ្ណាគារអង្គរ").
  It is a **private, commercially published exam-preparation booklet**,
  not a MoEYS textbook, and it nowhere claims to be a companion to,
  or a chapter of, the Grade 12 textbook. Its references (p. 183)
  cite the MoEYS Grade 12 textbook (basic + advanced levels) and past
  Baccalaureate papers 2002–present as sources.
- **Dedication (p. 3):** to the author's wife
  អែង៉ត សុគន្ធ (Aeng Socheatha?) and daughter ខេម សោភា (Khem Sophea)
  — read from the text layer; names as printed.
- **Front matter:** cover (p. 1), committee/imprint (p. 2),
  dedication (p. 3), preface (pp. 4), acknowledgements (pp. 5),
  table of mathematical symbols (p. 6), contents (p. 7), **p. 8 blank**.

### Contents (from the printed table of contents, p. 7)

- Lesson 1: Complex numbers in algebraic form (ចំនួនកុំផ្លិចទម្រង់ពីជគណិត) — p. 9
  - 1 Definition of a complex number — p. 10; 2 Operations on complex
    numbers — p. 16; 3 Conjugate complex number — p. 17; 4 Quadratic
    equations with real coefficients — p. 18; 5 Geometric representation
    of a complex number — p. 21; 6 Complex numbers in the complex plane
    — p. 21; 7 Image vectors of sums, differences and real multiples —
    p. 22. MCQ set (100 exercises) — p. 25. Additional practice
    exercises — p. 34.
- Lesson 2: Complex numbers in trigonometric form — p. 55
  - 1 Modulus — p. 55; 2 Argument — p. 58; 3 Trigonometric form — p. 60;
    4 Multiplication in trigonometric form — p. 61; 5 Division in
    trigonometric form — p. 63; 6 Rotation about the origin of the
    complex plane — p. 64. MCQ set (100 exercises) — p. 65. Practice
    exercises — p. 74.
- Lesson 3: nth powers and nth roots of a complex number — p. 95
  - 1 nth power — p. 95; 2 Applications in trigonometry — p. 97;
    3 nth roots — p. 98. MCQ set (100 exercises) — p. 102. Lesson
    exercises — p. 111. Chapter 1 exercises — p. 119. Exercises that
    have appeared [in the Baccalaureate] — p. 131.
- Lesson 4: Research exercises and competency multiple-choice — p. 151
  - 1 Competition-preparation MCQ — p. 151; 2 Research and high-level
    exercises — p. 156.
- Lesson 5: Answer section — p. 163
  - 1 MCQ answer keys (Answer Keys) — p. 163; 2 Detailed solutions,
    Lesson 1 practice — p. 167; 3 Detailed solutions, Lesson 2 —
    p. 172; 4 Detailed solutions, Lesson 3 + chapter exercises —
    p. 175; 5 Detailed solutions, research and high-level exercises —
    p. 178.
- References — p. 183.

### What the booklet teaches, lesson by lesson

#### Lesson 1 — Complex numbers in algebraic form (pp. 9–24 theory)

- **Imaginary unit (pp. 9–10):** √(−c) = i√c for c > 0; i² = −1.
  Worked Example 1 computes √(−144) = 12i, √(−12) = 2i√3,
  √(−169) = 13i, √(−25) = 5i. Powers of i: i⁴ᵏ = 1, i⁴ᵏ⁺¹ = i,
  i⁴ᵏ⁺² = −1, i⁴ᵏ⁺³ = −i (p. 10); Example 2 evaluates i¹⁹⁷⁵,
  i¹⁹⁷⁹, i²⁰¹⁹, i²⁰²¹, i²⁰²⁵, i²⁰²⁶ (all correct: −i, −i, −i, i, i, −1).
- **Definition (p. 11):** a complex number is a + bi (a, b real);
  set denoted ℂ. Conjugate z̄ = a − bi (p. 12); opposite −z = −a − bi
  (p. 13). Equality of two complex numbers iff real parts and
  imaginary parts are equal (p. 13).
- **Operations (p. 16):** addition, subtraction, multiplication
  (ac − bd) + (ad + bc)i, division (ac + bd)/(c² + d²) +
  (bc − ad)i/(c² + d²). Worked Example 8 (see Errors: items ង/ច).
- **Conjugate properties (p. 17):** conjugate of sum/difference/
  product/quotient equals sum/difference/product/quotient of
  conjugates; verified numerically for z = 2 − 3i, w = 3 − i
  (Example 9, correct).
- **Quadratics with real coefficients (pp. 18–20):** discriminant
  cases Δ > 0 (two real roots), Δ = 0 (double root), Δ < 0 (two
  conjugate complex roots (−b ± i√(−Δ))/2a); proof by completing the
  square. Example 10 solves nine quadratics (see Errors: duplicate
  item). Special cases: b = 0, c = 0, a + b + c = 0 (roots 1, c/a),
  a − b + c = 0 (roots −1, −c/a). Vieta's formulas S = −b/a,
  P = c/a (p. 20).
- **Geometry (pp. 21–24):** complex plane (Argand plane), affix
  point M(a, b) / image vector OM→; real axis, imaginary axis.
  Examples 11–14 plot 1 + i, −1 + 2i, −2 − i; image vector of a sum
  (parallelogram rule: (2+i)+(1+2i) = 3+3i), of a difference
  (1 − i), and of a real multiple (2z = 4 + 2i for z = 2 + i).
- **Exercises:** MCQ 100 items, pp. 25–33, explicitly ordered by
  Bloom's Taxonomy levels 1–6. Additional practice exercises,
  pp. 34–53 (sets on writing numbers as imaginaries, operations,
  conjugates, quadratics). P. 54 is blank.

#### Lesson 2 — Trigonometric form (pp. 55–64 theory)

- **Modulus (pp. 55–57):** |z| = √(a² + b²); properties
  |z| = |−z| = |z̄|, |z|² = z·z̄, |z₁z₂| = |z₁||z₂|,
  |z₁/z₂| = |z₁|/|z₂|, triangle inequality — each proved.
  Example 1 evaluates |4 − 3i| etc. (= 5 in all four sign variants);
  Example 2 verifies the properties numerically for z = 4 − 3i,
  z₁ = 2 − i, z₂ = 3 + 2i (all correct).
- **Argument (pp. 58–60):** arg(z) = φ + 2kπ; cos φ = a/r,
  sin φ = b/r; quadrant (ASTC) method for finding the principal
  argument, with a worked table for all four quadrants.
  Example 3 writes 1 + i√3, −1 + i, √3 − i in trig form
  (2 cis π/3, √2 cis 3π/4, 2 cis(−π/6) — correct).
- **Trigonometric form (p. 60):** z = r(cos φ + i sin φ).
  Example 4 (see Errors: items គ/ឃ).
- **Multiplication/division in trig form (pp. 61–64):** multiply
  moduli, add arguments; divide moduli, subtract arguments; both
  proved from angle-sum identities. Example 5:
  (2√3 − 2i)(−1 + i√3) = 8i (correct). Practice 1 (p. 62):
  (1 + i)(−1 + i√3) = 2√2 cis 11π/12 (correct). Example 6:
  i / (√3/2 + i/2) = cos π/3 + i sin π/3 (correct).
- **Rotation (p. 64):** rotation about O through angle α sends
  z to z′ = (cos α + i sin α)z; proved.
- **Exercises:** MCQ 100 items, pp. 65–73; practice exercises,
  pp. 74–93. P. 94 is blank.

#### Lesson 3 — nth powers and nth roots (pp. 95–101 theory)

- **De Moivre's theorem (pp. 95–96):** [r(cos θ + i sin θ)]ⁿ =
  rⁿ(cos nθ + i sin nθ). Example 1: [2 cis π/3]⁷ = 64 + 64i√3
  (correct). Example 2: (1+i)²⁰²⁴ = 2¹⁰¹²; (−2+2i)²⁰²⁶ = −2³⁰³⁹i;
  (1+i√3)²⁰²⁵ = −2²⁰²⁵ (all correct).
- **Applications in trigonometry (p. 97):** the ratio
  (zC − zA)/(zB − zA) has modulus AC/AB and argument the directed
  angle (AB→, AC→); right triangle at A iff the ratio is ±i;
  equilateral triangle iff the ratio is cos(±π/3) + i sin(±π/3),
  equivalently zA² + zB² + zC² = zAzB + zBzC + zCzA. Example 3 proves
  the three cube roots of unity form an equilateral triangle
  (side √3, correct).
- **nth roots (pp. 98–101):** wₖ = ⁿ√r cis((φ + 2kπ)/n),
  k = 0…n−1; proved; roots are vertices of a regular n-gon on the
  circle of radius ⁿ√r. Example 4: 5th roots of −1 + i (angles
  3π/20, 11π/20, 19π/20, 27π/20, 35π/20 — correct). Example 5:
  4th roots of −8 − 8i√3 are 1 + i√3, −√3 + i, −1 − i√3,
  √3 − i (correct). Practice 1 (p. 101): solve (z−2)³ − 1 = 0,
  z⁶ + 2z³ + 1 = 0, z⁶ + 1 = 0, z⁶ + 64 = 0 in ℂ (answers in §5).
- **Exercises:** MCQ 100 items, pp. 102–110; lesson exercises
  (11 items), pp. 111–118; Chapter 1 exercises, pp. 119–130;
  "exercises that have appeared" — past Baccalaureate questions
  tagged by year ([2002], [2004], …), pp. 131–150.

#### Lesson 4 — Research exercises and competency MCQ (pp. 151–162)

- **Competition-preparation MCQ (44 items, pp. 151–155):** advanced
  items — De Moivre with large exponents, moduli/arguments, loci
  (e.g. |z − 3 + 4i| = 2 is the circle centre (3, −4), radius 2),
  sums of powers of i, exact values cos π/12, sin π/12 via complex
  numbers. See Errors for five flawed items (Q7, Q9, Q16, Q38, Q43).
- **Research and high-level exercises (45 items, pp. 156–161):**
  solving linear and quadratic equations in ℂ, systems in ℂ×ℂ,
  proofs (z² − z̄² is purely imaginary; zⁿ + z̄ⁿ is real),
  cube roots of unity j (j² = j̄, 1 + j + j² = 0),
  deriving sin 3θ / cos 3θ / tan 4θ from De Moivre,
  Euler-formula expansions, trigonometric sums
  Sₙ = 1 + cos x + … + cos nx, loci and Cartesian equations
  (arg(z − i) = π/2; |z+2|/|z−2| = 2), polynomial factorisation
  over ℂ (P(z) = z⁴ − 2z³ + 3z² − 2z + 2), and
  (z+i)ⁿ − (z−i)ⁿ = 0 with roots zₖ = cot(kπ/n).
  P. 162 is blank.

#### Lesson 5 — Answers (pp. 163–182)

- MCQ answer-key tables for Lessons 1–3 (100 answers each,
  pp. 163–166; the Lesson 2 table is headed "ម៉ូឌុល និង អាគុយម៉ង់"
  / modulus and argument). Detailed worked solutions: Lesson 1
  practice (pp. 167–171), Lesson 2 (pp. 172–174), Lesson 3 +
  chapter exercises (pp. 175–177), research/high-level exercises
  (pp. 178–182). Spot-checks of the solutions read (e.g. p. 178:
  z² + 6z + 34 = 0 → z = −3 ± 5i; z² − 2√3 z + 4 = 0 → z = √3 ± i)
  are correct.

### Confirmed book errors and slips (teach corrected, never as fact)

1. **Lesson 1, Example 8, items ង/ច (pp. 16–17) — solutions answer
   different problems than stated (visually verified).** Stated:
   ង (6 + 4i)/(2 − i); ច (3 − 5i)/(1 + 2i). Printed solutions:
   ង (2 + 3i)/(1 − 2i) = −4/5 + 7/5 i; ច (4 − i)/(3 + 2i) =
   10/13 − 11/13 i. Both printed solutions are correct for the
   problems they solve. Correct answers for the STATED problems:
   (6 + 4i)/(2 − i) = 8/5 + 14/5 i; (3 − 5i)/(1 + 2i) = −7/5 − 11/5 i.
2. **Lesson 2, Example 4, items គ/ឃ (pp. 60–61) — solutions answer
   different problems than stated (visually verified).** Stated:
   គ z₃ = −8 − 8i; ឃ z₄ = 1 − i√3. Printed solutions:
   គ z₃ = −3i = 3 cis(−π/2); ឃ z₄ = 4 = 4 cis 0. Correct trig forms
   for the STATED items: −8 − 8i = 8√2 cis(−3π/4);
   1 − i√3 = 2 cis(−π/6).
3. **Lesson 1, Example 10 (pp. 19–20) — duplicated item.** Items ង
   and ជ are the SAME equation, x² + 2x + 5 = 0, solved identically
   (x = −1 ± 2i) in both places. One of them was presumably meant to
   be a different quadratic.
4. **Lesson 4, Q7 (p. 152) — no correct option as printed (visually
   verified).** With a = √3 − i, b = 2 − 2√3 i, the stated
   z = b/a = √3 − i, i.e. x = √3, y = −1. All four options have
   x = √3/4 or √3/2. The options fit z = a/b = √3/4 + i/4
   (option ខ). The fraction in the question is inverted relative to
   its options.
5. **Lesson 4, Q9 (p. 152) — two options coincide (visually
   verified).** A = ((7 − 15i)/(15 + 7i))²⁰²⁵ = −i (base = −i,
   2025 ≡ 1 mod 4), so option ខ (A = −i) is right — but option ក
   (A = −i²⁰²⁵) is also −i. Two keyed answers for one question.
6. **Lesson 4, Q16 (p. 152) — correct answer absent (visually
   verified).** |z|·z = 15 − 20i gives |z|² = 25, so |z| = 5
   (z = 3 − 4i). Options are 5√2, 2√5, 3√5 and "no solution";
   5 is not among them (and a solution does exist, so "no solution"
   is wrong too).
7. **Lesson 4, Q38 (p. 155) — wrong topic and duplicate options
   (visually verified).** Asks for a cube root of 8; all four options
   have modulus 1 (cis π/2, cis 3π/2, cis 2π/3, cis 3π/2), so none
   cubes to 8 — they are cube roots of unity, not of 8 (the intended
   answer is presumably cis 2π/3, option គ, as a root of z³ = 1).
   Options ខ and ឃ are identical (both cis 3π/2). True cube roots
   of 8: 2, 2 cis 2π/3 = −1 + i√3, 2 cis 4π/3 = −1 − i√3.
8. **Lesson 4, Q43 (p. 155) — no single correct answer (visually
   verified).** S = 1 + i² + i⁴ + … + i²ⁿ = 1 if n is even, 0 if n
   is odd; the value depends on the parity of n, so neither "S = 1"
   nor "S = 0" is always right.

No other worked-example errors were found in the theory sections
read (Lessons 1–3 theory and the sampled solutions were checked
item by item above). The MCQ answer keys were not exhaustively
re-checked against the questions.

### Honest negatives — what this booklet does NOT teach

- No matrices, no vectors beyond the complex-plane image vector, no
  sequences/series beyond the single trigonometric-sum exercise.
- No logarithm/exponential of a complex number; no Euler's formula
  e^{iθ} (Euler's name appears only in the "Euler formulas" exercise,
  L4 Q28, for cos θ / sin θ expansions).
- No complex roots of polynomials above degree 2 except via the
  single factorisation exercise (L4 Q43) and the (z+i)ⁿ − (z−i)ⁿ = 0
  exercise (L4 Q44).
- No locus work beyond circles/lines in L4 (Q35, Q36, Q42); no
  conic sections, no three-dimensional geometry.
- The booklet is self-contained exam preparation: it does not teach
  limits, derivatives or integrals (those are separate booklets in
  this series).

### Held-copy defects

- Blank pages (verified visually blank, with only the coloured
  footer bar): PDF pp. 8, 54, 94, 150, 162. These sit at lesson
  boundaries and are intentional section blanks, not missing content
  — the contents on either side run continuously and every TOC page
  number lands on its item.
- No duplicated pages, no truncated scan, no unreadable pages.
  The book ends at printed p. 183 (references); there is no
  back-cover page in the file.
- Text-layer note: on pp. 16–17 the multiplication formula renders
  in the text layer as if a parenthesis were missing; the printed
  page (visually checked) is correct: z₁·z₂ = (ac − bd) + (ad + bc)i.
  Do not treat text-layer artefacts as book errors.

body pages carry their printed number in the header/footer.

### What the booklet teaches, lesson by lesson

#### Lesson 1 — Derivatives of functions (pp. 9–17 theory)

- **Derivative at a point (pp. 9–10):** average rate of change
  Δy/Δx; instantaneous rate / derivative as its limit; the three
  equivalent definitions f′(x₀) = lim Δy/Δx = lim [f(x₀+h)−f(x₀)]/h
  = lim [f(x)−f(x₀)]/(x−x₀). Example 1 computes by definition:
  f(x) = 2x² + 3x at x₀ = 2 → 11; f(x) = (x+1)/(2x−1) at x₀ = 1 →
  −3; f(x) = √(2x+1) at x₀ = 4 → 1/3 (all correct).
- **Geometric meaning (pp. 11–13):** f′(x₀) is the slope of the
  tangent, = tan α; tangent equation y − y₀ = f′(x₀)(x − x₀);
  normal equation y − y₀ = −(x − x₀)/f′(x₀). Special cases:
  horizontal tangent (f′ = 0), tangent parallel to y = ax + b
  (f′ = a), tangent perpendicular (f′ = −1/a), tangent through an
  exterior point A(xA, yA). Example 2 for f(x) = x³ − 3x + 1:
  tangent/normal at x₀ = 2 (y = 9x − 15; y = −x/9 + 29/9);
  horizontal tangents at (1, −1) and (−1, 3); tangents parallel to
  y = 9x − 5 are y = 9x − 15 and y = 9x + 17 (all correct).
- **Differentiability and continuity (pp. 14–15):** differentiable ⇒
  continuous (proved); converse false — |x| at 0
  (f₋′(0) = −1 ≠ f₊′(0) = 1). Left/right derivatives; cases with no
  derivative: corner/cusp, vertical tangent (∛x at 0),
  discontinuity.
- **Derivative formulas (pp. 15–16):** full table for basic
  functions and composite functions u(x): constants, xⁿ,
  1/x, √x, sin/cos/tan/cot, eˣ, aˣ, ln x, log_a x, arcsin, arccos,
  arctan — all correct, including inverse-trigonometric derivatives.
  Operations: (u ± v)′, (ku)′, (uv)′ = u′v + v′u, (u/v)′.
- **Special techniques (pp. 16–17):** logarithmic differentiation
  (for y = u(x)^v(x)); implicit differentiation; higher-order
  derivatives and the Leibniz formula for (uv)⁽ⁿ⁾.
- **Exercises:** MCQ 50 items, pp. 18–23; Bac-standard practice
  50 items, pp. 24–43. P. 44 is blank.

#### Lesson 2 — Applications of derivatives (pp. 45–56 theory)

- **Monotonicity (pp. 45–46):** f′ > 0 ⇒ strictly increasing,
  f′ < 0 ⇒ strictly decreasing, f′ = 0 ⇒ constant — proved via
  the Mean Value Theorem. Critical numbers: f′(c) = 0 or f′(c)
  does not exist. Example 1: f(x) = 2x³ − 3x² − 12x + 5 increases
  on (−∞, −1] and [2, ∞), decreases on [−1, 2] (correct).
- **Concavity and inflection (pp. 47–49):** f″ > 0 ⇒ concave up,
  f″ < 0 ⇒ concave down (proved by comparing the curve with its
  tangent); inflection point where f″ changes sign. Example 2:
  f(x) = x³ − 6x² + 9x + 1, concave down on (−∞, 2), concave up
  on (2, ∞), inflection I(2, 3) (correct).
- **Extrema (pp. 49–50):** first- and second-derivative tests in a
  summary table; absolute extrema on a closed interval [a, b] by
  checking critical numbers and endpoints. Worked graph:
  f(x) = x³ − 3x, relative max (−1, 2), relative min (1, −2),
  inflection (0, 0) (correct).
- **Rolle's and Mean Value theorems (pp. 50–52):** both stated and
  proved (MVT proved from Rolle via the auxiliary function
  g(x) = f(x) − f(a) − (f(b)−f(a))(x−a)/(b−a)); geometric meaning.
- **L'Hôpital's rule (pp. 53–54):** 0/0 and ∞/∞ forms, proved for
  0/0 via Cauchy's extended MVT; other forms 0·∞, ∞−∞, 1^∞, 0⁰,
  ∞⁰ reduced to quotients. Example 3: (e²ˣ−1)/sin 3x → 2/3;
  (x−sin x)/x³ → 1/6; xe^(−x) → 0 as x → +∞ (all correct).
- **Applied optimisation (p. 55):** five-step method; worked fence
  problem — 120 m of fencing against a wall, rectangle 30 m × 60 m,
  maximum area 1800 m² (correct).
- **Kinematics and related rates (p. 56):** v = s′, a = v′ = s″;
  speeding up/slowing down by the sign of v·a. Example 5:
  s(t) = t³ − 6t² + 9t, v = 3t² − 12t + 9, a = 6t − 12; at rest at
  t = 1 s and t = 3 s (correct).
- **Exercises:** MCQ 50 items, pp. 57–61; Bac-standard practice
  50 items, pp. 62–86.

#### Lesson 3 — Other applications and studying functions (pp. 87–97 theory)

- **Differentials and approximation (pp. 87–89):** dy = f′(x)dx;
  Δy ≈ dy; f(x₀+Δx) ≈ f(x₀) + f′(x₀)Δx (proved). Absolute,
  relative and percentage errors. Example 1: √26 ≈ 5.1;
  ∛28 ≈ 3.037 (correct).
- **Finite increment inequality (pp. 89–90):** if m ≤ f′ ≤ M on
  (a, b) then m(b−a) ≤ f(b)−f(a) ≤ M(b−a); in particular
  |f(b)−f(a)| ≤ M|b−a|. Proved from MVT. Example 2: sin x ≤ x and
  x/(1+x) ≤ ln(1+x) ≤ x for x > 0 (correct).
- **Economics (pp. 91–94):** marginal cost/revenue/profit;
  total/average cost C(x) = FC + VC(x); average cost is minimised
  where C′(x) = C̄(x) (proved by the quotient rule); profit is
  maximised where R′(x) = C′(x) and P″(x) < 0. Example 3:
  C(x) = 500 + 10x + 0.05x²; marginal cost of the 101st unit ≈ $20
  vs exact $20.05 (0.25% error). Example 4: C(x) = 1000 + 20x +
  0.1x² (thousand riel), p(x) = 80 − 0.1x; maximum profit at
  x = 150 units, P = 3,500 thousand riel (correct).
- **Asymptotes and symmetry (pp. 95–96):** vertical, horizontal
  and oblique asymptotes defined by limits; centre of symmetry
  I(a, b) iff f(2a−x) + f(x) = 2b; axis of symmetry x = a iff
  f(2a−x) = f(x) (both proved). For y = (ax+b)/(cx+d) the centre
  is the intersection of the asymptotes, I(−d/c, a/c).
- **General study method (pp. 96–97):** four steps — domain;
  variation (derivative, limits, asymptotes, variation table);
  special points and inflection; sketch. Worked study of
  f(x) = (2x−1)/(x−1): D = ℝ\{1}, f′ = −1/(x−1)² < 0, asymptotes
  y = 2 and x = 1, intercepts (0, 1) and (1/2, 0), centre I(1, 2)
  (correct).
- **Exercises:** MCQ 50 items, pp. 98–102; Bac-standard practice
  50 items, pp. 103–126. P. 126 is blank.

#### Lesson 4 — Solutions (pp. 127–146)

- MCQ answer keys for Lessons 1–3 (50 answers each, at the head of
  each lesson's solutions: pp. 127, 133, 140 — spot-checked: L1 Q1
  and L2 Q1 keyed correctly) followed by detailed worked solutions
  of the Bac-standard practice exercises (numbered exercises, e.g.
  L1 solutions pp. 127–132 cover exercises 1–23+ including
  logarithmic and implicit differentiation).
- Appendix (pp. 147–155): formula tables for derivatives,
  tangents/normals, monotonicity/extrema/concavity, related rates,
  economics, L'Hôpital, trigonometry. References p. 156.

### Confirmed book errors and slips

- **No worked-example or theorem error was found** in the theory
  sections read (Lessons 1–3, checked item by item above) or in the
  sampled solutions. The MCQ answer keys were spot-checked only
  (Lesson 1–2 Q1), not exhaustively re-checked.
- **Minor presentation point (p. 49, figure):** the illustration
  "graph showing concavity and inflection" labels an inflection
  point I(1, 2.33), but the worked example directly below it
  (Example 2, f(x) = x³ − 6x² + 9x + 1) has inflection I(2, 3).
  The figure is a generic, unlabelled illustration (its curve is a
  different cubic), not a drawing of Example 2's function — a
  learner reading figure and example together could take I(1, 2.33)
  as belonging to the example. Teach the example's I(2, 3).

### Honest negatives — what this booklet does NOT teach

- No integrals/primitives (separate booklet); no differential
  equations (separate booklet); no complex numbers (separate
  booklet).
- No Taylor/Maclaurin series; no parametric or polar curves;
  no partial derivatives.
- L'Hôpital is stated for 0/0 and ∞/∞ only; the other
  indeterminate forms are handled by reduction, with no worked
  example of a 1^∞ / 0⁰ / ∞⁰ limit.
- No numerical methods (Newton's method, etc.) and no curve
  sketching of exponential/logarithmic functions in worked examples
  (the worked study is a rational function only; exponential and
  logarithmic functions are studied in the Variation and Graphs
  booklet).

- No duplicated pages, no truncated scan, no unreadable pages.
